[3769] At the same time:
[3770] Security Admin removes approval permission
[3771] The system must define deterministic behaviour.
[3772] Sensitive operations should revalidate authorization immediately before execution.
[3774] 4.64 Authorization and Transactions
[3775] For critical operations:
[3776] READ AUTHORIZATION
[3777] ↓
[3778] BUSINESS VALIDATION
[3779] ↓
[3780] APPROVAL
[3781] ↓
[3782] FINAL AUTHORIZATION
[3783] ↓
[3784] EXECUTION
[3785] Authorization must be checked again at the final commit point for high-risk operations.
[3787] 4.65 Administrative Interface
[3788] The administration console should provide:
[3789] User Management
[3790] Create user
[3791] Disable user
[3792] Suspend user
[3793] Reset authentication
[3794] Revoke sessions
[3795] View access
[3796] Role Management
[3797] Create role
[3798] Modify role
[3799] Assign permission
[3800] Remove permission
[3801] View inherited permissions
[3802] Scope Management
[3803] Organization
[3804] Region
[3805] Branch
[3806] Department
[3807] Provider
[3808] Employer
[3809] Security
[3810] MFA status
[3811] Devices
[3812] Sessions
[3813] Risk indicators
[3814] Security events
[3815] Access Review
[3816] Pending reviews
[3817] Expired access
[3818] Excessive privileges
[3819] Dormant accounts
[3820] Temporary privileges
[3822] 4.66 Permission Visibility
[3823] Administrators should be able to answer:
[3824] “What can this user do?”
[3825] The system should display:
[3826] User
[3827] ↓
[3828] Roles
[3829] ↓
[3830] Inherited Roles
[3831] ↓
[3832] Direct Permissions
[3833] ↓
[3834] Scopes
[3835] ↓
[3836] ABAC Policies
[3837] ↓
[3838] SoD Restrictions
[3839] ↓
[3840] Temporary Permissions
[3841] ↓
[3842] Effective Permissions
[3843] It should also answer:
[3844] “Who can perform this action?”
[3845] Example:
[3846] claims.claim.approve
[3847] → list authorized roles/users/scopes.
[3849] 4.67 Effective Permission Calculation
[3850] The system should calculate:
[3851] Effective Permissions
[3852] =
[3853] Role Permissions
[3854] +
[3855] Approved Direct Permissions
[3856] +
[3857] Temporary Permissions
[3858] -
[3859] Explicit Restrictions
[3860] -
[3861] SoD Conflicts
[3862] -
[3863] Expired Permissions
[3864] -
[3865] Suspended Access
[3866] Then apply:
[3867] Resource Scope
[3868] +
[3869] ABAC
[3870] +
[3871] Risk
[3872] +
[3873] Business State
[3874] before making the final decision.
[3876] 4.68 Zero-Trust Principle
[3877] The architecture should operate on:
[3878] Never automatically trust
[3879] Always verify
[3880] Being:
[3881] inside the corporate network,
[3882] an employee,
[3883] a manager,
[3884] an administrator,
[3885] an authenticated user,
[3886] does not automatically mean the user is authorized for every operation.
[3888] 4.69 Security Failure Behaviour
[3889] For sensitive operations:
[3890] Authorization Service unavailable
[3891] ↓
[3892] DENY
[3893] For low-risk read operations where an explicitly approved safe fallback exists:
[3894] Authorization unavailable
[3895] ↓
[3896] Configured safe fallback
[3897] ↓
[3898] Audit
[3899] No system component should invent its own fallback authorization behaviour.
[3901] 4.70 Part IV End-to-End Authorization Flow
[3902] A high-risk operation such as approving a provider settlement should follow:
[3903] USER LOGIN
[3904] ↓
[3905] IDENTITY VERIFIED
[3906] ↓
[3907] MFA VERIFIED
[3908] ↓
[3909] SESSION CREATED
[3910] ↓
[3911] USER REQUESTS APPROVAL
[3912] ↓
[3913] RBAC CHECK
[3914] ↓
[3915] SCOPE CHECK
[3916] ↓
[3917] RESOURCE OWNERSHIP CHECK
[3918] ↓
[3919] ABAC CHECK
[3920] ↓
[3921] BUSINESS STATE CHECK
[3922] ↓
[3923] APPROVAL LIMIT CHECK
[3924] ↓
[3925] SoD CHECK
[3926] ↓
[3927] RISK CHECK
[3928] ↓
[3929] STEP-UP AUTHENTICATION
[3930] ↓
[3931] APPROVAL POLICY
[3932] ↓
[3933] FINAL AUTHORIZATION
[3934] ↓
[3935] TRANSACTION LOCK
[3936] ↓
[3937] EXECUTION
[3938] ↓
[3939] AUDIT EVENT
[3940] ↓
[3941] SECURITY MONITORING
[3943] 4.71 Part IV Success Criteria
[3944] Part IV is considered complete when:
[3945] Every user has an identifiable identity.
[3946] Authentication is centralized.
[3947] MFA can be enforced.
[3948] Roles are configurable.
[3949] Permissions are granular.
[3950] Data scopes are enforced.
[3951] ABAC policies can be configured.
[3952] Employer isolation works.
[3953] Provider isolation works.
[3954] SoD conflicts are enforced.
[3955] Approval workflows are centralized.
[3956] Temporary privileges expire automatically.
[3957] Emergency access is controlled.
[3958] Privileged access is monitored.
[3959] API authorization is enforced server-side.
[3960] Background services have explicit identities.
[3961] Sensitive fields can be masked.
[3962] Export operations are controlled.
[3963] Authorization events are auditable.
[3964] Permission changes invalidate stale access.
[3965] Terminated users lose access immediately.
[3966] Unknown authorization conditions fail safely.
[3967] No frontend-only authorization is trusted.
[3968] No default configuration accidentally grants broad access.
[3970] 4.72 Architectural Principle
[3971] The HMO platform's security model can ultimately be summarized as:
[3972] IDENTITY
[3973] +
[3974] AUTHENTICATION
[3975] +
[3976] ROLE
[3977] +
[3978] PERMISSION
[3979] +
[3980] SCOPE
[3981] +
[3982] ATTRIBUTES
[3983] +
[3984] BUSINESS RULES
[3985] +
[3986] SoD
[3987] +
[3988] RISK
[3989] +
[3990] APPROVAL
[3991] =
[3992] EFFECTIVE AUTHORIZATION
[3993] And:
[3994] NO EXPLICIT AUTHORIZATION
[3995] =
[3996] DENY
[3997] This principle must remain consistent across the web application, mobile applications, APIs, microservices, background workers, integrations, reporting systems, administrative tools, and future modules.
[3998] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[3999] PART V — MEMBER, EMPLOYER, PROVIDER & ENROLLMENT ARCHITECTURE
[4001] 5.0 Purpose
[4002] This part defines the operational architecture for managing the major entities that form the foundation of the HMO platform:
[4003] Members
[4004] Dependants
[4005] Employers
[4006] Employer employees
[4007] Providers
[4008] Provider users
[4009] Health plans
[4010] Benefits
[4011] Enrollment
[4012] Eligibility
[4013] Coverage
[4014] Provider networks
[4015] Identification
[4016] Onboarding
[4017] Verification
[4018] Lifecycle management
[4019] These entities are interconnected.
[4020] For example:
[4021] EMPLOYER
[4022] ↓
[4023] EMPLOYEE
[4024] ↓
[4025] MEMBER
[4026] ↓
[4027] PLAN
[4028] ↓
[4029] ENROLLMENT
[4030] ↓
[4031] ELIGIBILITY
[4032] ↓
[4033] PROVIDER
[4034] ↓
[4035] HEALTHCARE SERVICE
[4036] ↓
[4037] CLAIM
[4038] A mistake at any stage can affect downstream operations.
[4039] Therefore, the system must maintain strong:
[4040] Identity integrity
[4041] Referential integrity
[4042] Authorization
[4043] Data validation
[4044] Lifecycle management
[4045] Effective dating
[4046] Auditability
[4047] Version control
[4048] Duplicate detection
[4050] 5.1 Core Entity Architecture
[4051] The major entities are:
[4052] Organization
[4053] │
[4054] ├── Employer
[4055] │    └── Employer Employee
[4056] │          └── Member
[4057] │                └── Dependant
[4058] │
[4059] ├── Provider
[4060] │    ├── Provider Location
[4061] │    ├── Provider User
[4062] │    ├── Provider Credential
[4063] │    └── Provider Contract
[4064] │
[4065] ├── Health Plan
[4066] │    ├── Benefit
[4067] │    ├── Exclusion
[4068] │    ├── Limit
[4069] │    └── Provider Network
[4070] │
[4071] └── Enrollment
[4072] └── Eligibility
[4074] 5.2 Master Data Principle
[4075] Core entities must have a single authoritative record.
[4076] For example:
[4077] There must be one authoritative member record rather than separate conflicting member records in:
[4078] Claims
[4079] Finance
[4080] Customer service
[4081] Enrollment
[4082] Medical
[4083] Reporting
[4084] Other modules should reference the master member identity.
[4086] 5.3 Member Management
[4087] A member is a person entitled to healthcare coverage under the HMO.
[4088] Member categories may include:
[4089] Principal Member
[4090] The primary person enrolled in a health plan.
[4091] Dependant
[4092] A person whose coverage derives from the principal member.
[4093] Individual Member
[4094] A person enrolled independently rather than through an employer.
[4095] Corporate Member
[4096] An employee enrolled through an employer-sponsored arrangement.
[4098] 5.4 Member Identity Separation
[4099] The platform must distinguish:
[4100] Member ID
[4101] User Account ID
[4102] Employer Employee ID
[4103] Provider Patient Reference
[4104] National/Identity Reference
[4105] Claim ID
[4106] Enrollment ID
[4107] Example:
[4108] Member ID:
[4109] MEM-000123
[4111] User Account:
[4112] USR-008821
[4114] Employer Employee:
[4115] EMP-00451
[4117] Enrollment:
[4118] ENR-90012
[4119] These identifiers must not be used interchangeably.
[4121] 5.5 Member Lifecycle
[4122] The member lifecycle shall support:
[4123] PROSPECT
[4124] ↓
[4125] APPLICATION
[4126] ↓
[4127] PENDING_VERIFICATION
[4128] ↓
[4129] VERIFIED
[4130] ↓
[4131] ENROLLMENT_PENDING
[4132] ↓
[4133] ENROLLED
[4134] ↓
[4135] ACTIVE
[4136] ↓
[4137] SUSPENDED
[4138] ↓
[4139] REACTIVATED
[4140] ↓
[4141] EXPIRED
[4142] ↓
[4143] TERMINATED
[4144] ↓
[4145] ARCHIVED
[4146] Not every member must pass through every state.
[4148] 5.6 Member Status Definitions
[4149] Prospect
[4150] Potential customer/member who has not completed enrollment.
[4151] Application
[4152] Enrollment application has been submitted.
[4153] Pending Verification
[4154] Required information is being verified.
[4155] Verified
[4156] Identity and required information have passed verification.
[4157] Enrollment Pending
[4158] Member is verified but coverage has not yet become active.
[4159] Active
[4160] Member has valid coverage.
[4161] Suspended
[4162] Coverage temporarily restricted according to a valid business rule.
[4163] Reactivated
[4164] Previously suspended member has regained active status.
[4165] Expired
[4166] Coverage period ended.
[4167] Terminated
[4168] Membership formally ended.
[4169] Archived
[4170] Historical record retained but no longer operational.
[4172] 5.7 Member Profile
[4173] A member profile may contain:
[4174] Identity
[4175] Member ID
[4176] Full name
[4177] Date of birth
[4178] Gender where required
[4179] Photograph where appropriate
[4180] Identification references
[4181] Contact
[4182] Phone
[4183] Email
[4184] Address
[4185] Emergency contact
[4186] Employment
[4187] Employer
[4188] Employee ID
[4189] Employment status
[4190] Coverage
[4191] Plan
[4192] Enrollment
[4193] Effective date
[4194] Expiry date
[4195] Coverage status
[4196] Provider network
[4197] Operational
[4198] Registration date
[4199] Source
[4200] Assigned branch
[4201] Account manager where applicable
[4203] 5.8 Minimum Necessary Data
[4204] Different users should see different member information.
[4205] For example:
[4206] Customer service may need:
[4207] Name
[4208] Member ID
[4209] Coverage status
[4210] Plan
[4211] Contact details
[4212] A medical user may need authorized clinical information.
[4213] Finance may require:
[4214] Billing
[4215] Premium
[4216] Payment
[4217] Financial responsibility
[4218] A system administrator should not automatically gain unrestricted medical visibility.
[4220] 5.9 Member Duplicate Detection
[4221] Duplicate members are a major operational risk.
[4222] Potential matching attributes:
[4223] Name
[4224] Date of birth
[4225] Phone
[4226] Email
[4227] Employer employee ID
[4228] Approved identity references
[4229] Existing member ID
[4230] Other configured identifiers
[4231] The system should calculate a duplicate-risk score.
[4232] Example:
[4233] Possible duplicate
[4234] ↓
[4235] High similarity
[4236] ↓
[4237] Manual verification
[4238] ↓
[4239] MERGE / KEEP SEPARATE / REJECT
[4240] Automatic merging of members should be heavily restricted.
[4242] 5.10 Member Merge
[4243] Where duplicate records are confirmed:
[4244] SOURCE MEMBER
[4245] ↓
[4246] MERGE REVIEW
[4247] ↓
[4248] MASTER MEMBER
[4249] ↓
[4250] REFERENCES UPDATED
[4251] Historical claims, payments, authorizations, and audit events must not be destroyed.
[4252] The system should preserve:
[4253] Original Member ID
[4254] Merged Into Member ID
[4255] Merge Reason
[4256] Approver
[4257] Date
[4259] 5.11 Member Split
[4260] The platform should also support controlled correction when one member record was incorrectly merged.
[4261] Example:
[4262] Incorrect Merge
[4263] ↓
[4264] Investigation
[4265] ↓
[4266] Approved Split
[4267] ↓
[4268] Historical Reference Restoration
[4269] ↓
[4270] Audit
[4271] This must never be a normal user operation.
[4273] 5.12 Dependant Management
[4274] Dependants may include eligible:
[4275] Spouse
[4276] Children
[4277] Other configured dependant categories
[4278] Eligibility rules must be configurable according to the applicable plan and business policy.
[4279] Dependant records should maintain:
[4280] Dependant ID
[4281] Principal Member ID
[4282] Relationship
[4283] Date of Birth
[4284] Eligibility Status
[4285] Effective Date
[4286] Expiry Date
[4287] Verification Status
[4289] 5.13 Dependant Lifecycle
[4290] REQUESTED
[4291] ↓
[4292] DOCUMENTATION_PENDING
[4293] ↓
[4294] UNDER_REVIEW
[4295] ↓
[4296] APPROVED
[4297] ↓
[4298] ENROLLED
[4299] ↓
[4300] ACTIVE
[4301] ↓
[4302] SUSPENDED
[4303] ↓
[4304] EXPIRED
[4305] ↓
[4306] TERMINATED
[4308] 5.14 Dependant Edge Cases
[4309] The system should handle:
[4310] Duplicate dependant
[4311] Same dependant attached to multiple principals where prohibited
[4312] Incorrect relationship
[4313] Missing documentation
[4314] Invalid effective date
[4315] Dependant reaching configured eligibility limit
[4316] Principal termination
[4317] Principal suspension
[4318] Dependant becoming independently eligible
[4319] Retroactive addition
[4320] Removal after claims have occurred
[4321] Conflicting dependant records
[4323] 5.15 Employer Management
[4324] An employer is an organization purchasing or sponsoring healthcare coverage.
[4325] Employer lifecycle:
[4326] PROSPECT
[4327] ↓
[4328] APPLICATION
[4329] ↓
[4330] ONBOARDING
[4331] ↓
[4332] VERIFICATION
[4333] ↓
[4334] CONTRACT_PENDING
[4335] ↓
[4336] ACTIVE
[4337] ↓
[4338] RENEWAL
[4339] ↓
[4340] SUSPENDED
[4341] ↓
[4342] TERMINATED
[4343] ↓
[4344] ARCHIVED
[4346] 5.16 Employer Profile
[4347] The employer record may include:
[4348] Employer ID
[4349] Legal name
[4350] Trading name
[4351] Registration information
[4352] Contact details
[4353] Address
[4354] Industry
[4355] Contact persons
[4356] Billing information
[4357] Contract information
[4358] Assigned HMO account manager
[4359] Plans
[4360] Employee count
[4361] Status
[4363] 5.17 Employer Contacts
[4364] Multiple employer contacts may be supported.
[4365] Example:
[4366] Employer
[4367] ├── HR Manager
[4368] ├── Finance Contact
[4369] ├── Administrator
[4370] └── Executive Contact
[4371] Each contact should have separate privileges.
[4372] The HR contact should not automatically access employer financial records.
[4374] 5.18 Employer User Architecture
[4375] Employer users should belong to a specific employer organization.
[4376] Example:
[4377] HMO
[4378] ↓
[4379] Employer ABC
[4380] ↓
[4381] Employer Admin
[4382] Employer Admin may manage:
[4383] Employee records
[4384] Enrollment requests
[4385] Dependants
[4386] Approved reports
[4387] but should not access:
[4388] Other employers
[4389] Internal HMO administration
[4390] Other providers
[4391] Internal financial controls
[4392] Security administration
[4394] 5.19 Employer Employee Management
[4395] Employee records may include:
[4396] Employee ID
[4397] Name
[4398] Department
[4399] Job title
[4400] Employment status
[4401] Start date
[4402] Exit date
[4403] Contact details
[4404] Member relationship
[4405] Plan
[4406] Coverage status
[4408] 5.20 Employee-to-Member Relationship
[4409] The system should support:
[4410] Employer Employee
[4411] ↓
[4412] Member
[4413] ↓
[4414] Enrollment
[4415] ↓
[4416] Plan
[4417] However, employee identity and member identity remain separate.
[4418] An employee may:
[4419] Become a member
[4420] Change plan
[4421] Leave employer
[4422] Rejoin employer
[4423] Have dependants
[4424] Have multiple historical enrollments
[4426] 5.21 Bulk Employee Upload
[4427] Employers should be able to upload employee data.
[4428] Recommended pipeline:
[4429] UPLOAD
[4430] ↓
[4431] MALWARE SCAN
[4432] ↓
[4433] FILE VALIDATION
[4434] ↓
[4435] SCHEMA VALIDATION
[4436] ↓
[4437] FIELD VALIDATION
[4438] ↓
[4439] BUSINESS VALIDATION
[4440] ↓
[4441] DUPLICATE DETECTION
[4442] ↓
[4443] PREVIEW
[4444] ↓
[4445] EMPLOYER CONFIRMATION
[4446] ↓
[4447] PROCESSING
[4448] ↓
[4449] RESULT REPORT
[4451] 5.22 Bulk Upload Validation
[4452] Validate:
[4453] Required fields
[4454] Data types
[4455] Dates
[4456] Duplicate employee IDs
[4457] Duplicate members
[4458] Invalid plan codes
[4459] Invalid provider codes
[4460] Invalid relationships
[4461] Invalid employer
[4462] Invalid coverage dates
[4463] Missing required documentation
[4464] A failed row must not cause valid rows to be silently corrupted.
[4466] 5.23 Partial Success Handling
[4467] Example:
[4468] 1,000 employees uploaded
[4470] Valid:
[4471] 950
[4473] Invalid:
[4474] 50
[4475] The system should allow:
[4476] 950 processed successfully
[4477] 50 returned with errors
[4478] provided the business operation supports partial processing.
[4479] The result must clearly identify each failed row and reason.
[4481] 5.24 Provider Management
[4482] Providers may include:
[4483] Hospitals
[4484] Clinics
[4485] Pharmacies
[4486] Laboratories
[4487] Diagnostic centers
[4488] Specialist facilities
[4489] Dental providers where applicable
[4490] Optical providers where applicable
[4492] 5.25 Provider Lifecycle
[4493] APPLICATION
[4494] ↓
[4495] DOCUMENT_COLLECTION
[4496] ↓
[4497] VERIFICATION
[4498] ↓
[4499] CREDENTIAL_ASSESSMENT
[4500] ↓
[4501] APPROVAL
[4502] ↓
[4503] CONTRACT_NEGOTIATION
[4504] ↓
[4505] CONTRACT_ACTIVE
[4506] ↓
[4507] NETWORK_ACTIVE
[4508] ↓
[4509] SUSPENDED
[4510] ↓
[4511] REACTIVATED
[4512] ↓
[4513] TERMINATED
[4514] ↓
[4515] ARCHIVED
[4517] 5.26 Provider Profile
[4518] Provider information may include:
[4519] Organization
[4520] Provider ID
[4521] Legal name
[4522] Trading name
[4523] Provider type
[4524] Registration information
[4525] Location
[4526] Address
[4527] State
[4528] LGA
[4529] Coordinates where needed
[4530] Branches
[4531] Contact
[4532] Phone
[4533] Email
[4534] Emergency contact
[4535] Services
[4536] Medical services
[4537] Laboratory
[4538] Pharmacy
[4539] Diagnostics
[4540] Specialist services
[4541] Contract
[4542] Contract ID
[4543] Effective date
[4544] Expiry
[4545] Tariff
[4546] Payment terms
[4547] Network status
[4549] 5.27 Provider Locations
[4550] A provider organization may have multiple locations.
[4551] Provider ABC
[4552] ├── Lagos Branch
[4553] ├── Ikeja Branch
[4554] └── Abuja Branch
[4555] Each location should have its own:
[4556] Address
[4557] Contact
[4558] Operating hours
[4559] Services
[4560] Licenses
[4561] Network status
[4563] 5.28 Provider Credentialing
[4564] Provider credentials may include:
[4565] Licenses
[4566] Certifications
[4567] Accreditation
[4568] Registration documents
[4569] Professional credentials
[4570] Facility certificates
[4571] Insurance documentation where required
[4572] Banking information
[4573] Contract documentation
[4574] Each credential should have:
[4575] Credential Type
[4576] Document
[4577] Issue Date
[4578] Expiry Date
[4579] Verification Status
[4580] Verified By
[4581] Verification Date
[4583] 5.29 Credential Expiration
[4584] The platform should automatically detect upcoming expiration.
[4585] Example:
[4586] Credential expires in 60 days
[4587] ↓
[4588] Notification
[4590] Credential expires in 30 days
[4591] ↓
[4592] Warning
[4594] Credential expired
[4595] ↓
[4596] Escalation
[4597] ↓
[4598] Provider restriction according to policy
[4600] 5.30 Provider Suspension
[4601] Provider suspension may result from:
[4602] Expired credential
[4603] Contract expiration
[4604] Compliance issue
[4605] Serious operational issue
[4606] Financial dispute
[4607] Security concern
[4608] Fraud investigation
[4609] Suspension should not automatically destroy historical provider records.
[4611] 5.31 Provider Network Management
[4612] Networks may define:
[4613] Provider eligibility
[4614] Plan access
[4615] Geographic availability
[4616] Provider category
[4617] Service availability
[4618] Effective dates
[4619] Example:
[4620] PLAN GOLD
[4621] ↓
[4622] Network GOLD
[4623] ↓
[4624] Provider A
[4625] Provider B
[4626] Provider C
[4628] 5.32 Network Effective Dating
[4629] Network changes must support effective dates.
[4630] Example:
[4631] Provider A
[4632] Network:
[4633] 01 Jan – 31 Mar
[4635] Provider B
[4636] Network:
[4637] 01 Apr onward
[4638] Claims and eligibility checks must use the network version applicable at the service date.
[4640] 5.33 Health Plan Management
[4641] Plans define the coverage offered to members.
[4642] A plan may include:
[4643] Plan name
[4644] Plan code
[4645] Coverage type
[4646] Premium
[4647] Benefits
[4648] Exclusions
[4649] Limits
[4650] Co-pay
[4651] Authorization requirements
[4652] Provider network
[4653] Effective period
[4655] 5.34 Plan Versioning
[4656] Plans must be versioned.
[4657] Example:
[4658] Gold Plan v1
[4659] Effective:
[4660] 2026-01-01 → 2026-06-30
[4662] Gold Plan v2
[4663] Effective:
[4664] 2026-07-01 → onward
[4665] Historical transactions must reference the applicable version.
[4666] Changing a plan must not silently rewrite historical claims.
[4668] 5.35 Benefit Architecture
[4669] Each benefit should have structured rules.
[4670] Example:
[4671] Benefit:
[4672] Outpatient Consultation
[4674] Coverage:
[4675] Yes
[4677] Limit:
[4678] 10 visits/year
[4680] Co-pay:
[4681] Configured amount
[4683] Authorization:
[4684] Not required
[4685] Another:
[4686] Benefit:
[4687] MRI
[4689] Coverage:
[4690] Yes
[4692] Limit:
[4693] 2/year
[4695] Authorization:
[4696] Required
[4698] 5.36 Benefit Rules
[4699] Rules may include:
[4700] Coverage status
[4701] Frequency
[4702] Annual limit
[4703] Financial limit
[4704] Co-pay
[4705] Deductible
[4706] Waiting period
[4707] Age restriction
[4708] Provider restriction
[4709] Network restriction
[4710] Authorization requirement
[4711] Referral requirement
[4713] 5.37 Exclusions
[4714] Plans may contain excluded services.
[4715] Examples:
[4716] Excluded procedures
[4717] Excluded conditions where contractually applicable
[4718] Cosmetic services
[4719] Non-covered services
[4720] The system must distinguish:
[4721] Not Covered
[4722] from:
[4723] Covered but Requires Authorization
[4724] and:
[4725] Covered but Limit Exhausted
[4726] These are operationally different outcomes.
[4728] 5.38 Enrollment Architecture
[4729] Enrollment links:
[4730] Member
[4731] +
[4732] Employer
[4733] +
[4734] Plan
[4735] +
[4736] Coverage Period
[4737] +
[4738] Eligibility
[4739] Enrollment sources may include:
[4740] Employer upload
[4741] HMO employee
[4742] Member self-service
[4743] API
[4744] Bulk migration
[4745] Approved integration
[4747] 5.39 Enrollment Lifecycle
[4748] DRAFT
[4749] ↓
[4750] SUBMITTED
[4751] ↓
[4752] VALIDATING
[4753] ↓
[4754] PENDING_VERIFICATION
[4755] ↓
[4756] APPROVED
[4757] ↓
[4758] SCHEDULED
[4759] ↓
[4760] ACTIVE
[4761] ↓
[4762] SUSPENDED
[4763] ↓
[4764] EXPIRED
[4765] ↓
[4766] TERMINATED
[4767] Possible exception states:
[4768] REJECTED
[4769] CANCELLED
[4770] PENDING_CORRECTION
[4772] 5.40 Enrollment Validation
[4773] Before approval:
[4774] Member exists
[4775] +
[4776] Member eligible
[4777] +
[4778] Employer active
[4779] +
[4780] Plan active
[4781] +
[4782] Contract valid
[4783] +
[4784] Effective dates valid
[4785] +
[4786] No conflicting enrollment
[4787] +
[4788] Required documents present
[4789] +
[4790] Required approvals complete
[4792] 5.41 Effective Dating
[4793] Every coverage record must have:
[4794] Effective From
[4795] Effective To
[4796] The system must prevent impossible periods.
[4797] Example:
[4798] Effective From:
[4799] 01/07/2026
[4801] Effective To:
[4802] 30/06/2026
[4803] must be rejected.
[4805] 5.42 Overlapping Enrollment
[4806] Example:
[4807] Plan A
[4808] 01 Jan → 31 Dec
[4810] Plan B
[4811] 01 Jun → 31 Dec
[4812] The system must determine whether overlapping coverage is permitted.
[4813] If not:
[4814] REJECT
[4815] If permitted:
[4816] ALLOW WITH EXPLICIT BUSINESS RULE
[4817] The system must never assume.
[4819] 5.43 Backdated Enrollment
[4820] Backdating is a high-risk operation.
[4821] Example:
[4822] Current Date:
[4823] 15 Aug
[4825] Requested Effective Date:
[4826] 01 Jul
[4827] Controls should include:
[4828] Specific permission
[4829] Reason
[4830] Approval
[4831] Audit
[4832] Financial impact evaluation
[4833] Claim impact evaluation
[4835] 5.44 Future-Dated Enrollment
[4836] Future enrollment should be supported.
[4837] Example:
[4838] Enrollment approved:
[4839] 15 August
[4841] Coverage begins:
[4842] 01 September
[4843] The system should schedule activation automatically.
[4845] 5.45 Eligibility Engine
[4846] Eligibility answers:
[4847] Is this member entitled to this service from this provider at this time?
[4848] The engine should evaluate:
[4849] Member exists
[4850] +
[4851] Member active
[4852] +
[4853] Coverage effective
[4854] +
[4855] Plan active
[4856] +
[4857] Service covered
[4858] +
[4859] Provider eligible
[4860] +
[4861] Network valid
[4862] +
[4863] Benefit available
[4864] +
[4865] Limit available
[4866] +
[4867] Authorization valid where required
[4869] 5.46 Eligibility Outcomes
[4870] The engine may return:
[4871] ELIGIBLE
[4872] PARTIALLY_ELIGIBLE
[4873] NOT_ELIGIBLE
[4874] REQUIRES_AUTHORIZATION
[4875] REQUIRES_REFERRAL
[4876] LIMIT_EXCEEDED
[4877] PROVIDER_NOT_ELIGIBLE
[4878] COVERAGE_NOT_ACTIVE
[4879] MANUAL_REVIEW
[4881] 5.47 Eligibility API
[4882] A provider system may submit:
[4883] Member ID
[4884] Provider ID
[4885] Service Code
[4886] Service Date
[4887] Plan
[4888] The system returns a controlled response.
[4889] Example:
[4890] {
[4891] "eligible": true,
[4892] "coverageStatus": "ACTIVE",
[4893] "authorizationRequired": false,
[4894] "benefitStatus": "AVAILABLE"
[4895] }
[4896] The API must not unnecessarily expose sensitive information.
[4898] 5.48 Eligibility Caching
[4899] Eligibility checks may be cached for performance.
[4900] However:
[4901] Member terminated
[4902] must invalidate relevant eligibility caches.
[4903] Critical coverage changes should propagate rapidly.
[4905] 5.49 Eligibility Concurrency
[4906] Example:
[4907] Request A:
[4908] Eligibility check
[4910] Request B:
[4911] Member termination
[4912] The system must define which event wins based on service date and transaction timestamp/business rules.
[4913] The result must be deterministic.
[4915] 5.50 Member Provider Relationship
[4916] A member may interact with multiple providers.
[4917] The system should record:
[4918] Member
[4919] Provider
[4920] Service
[4921] Authorization
[4922] Encounter
[4923] Claim
[4924] This creates the operational healthcare chain:
[4925] MEMBER
[4926] ↓
[4927] PROVIDER
[4928] ↓
[4929] SERVICE
[4930] ↓
[4931] AUTHORIZATION
[4932] ↓
[4933] CLAIM
[4934] ↓
[4935] PAYMENT
[4937] 5.51 Provider User Management
[4938] Provider users should be independently managed.
[4939] Example:
[4940] Provider ABC
[4941] ├── Administrator
[4942] ├── Claims Officer
[4943] ├── Billing Officer
[4944] └── Medical User
[4945] Each receives appropriate permissions.
[4946] A provider administrator should not automatically gain HMO administrator privileges.
[4948] 5.52 Employer-Provider-HMO Relationship
[4949] The system should support:
[4950] HMO
[4951] │
[4952] ├── Employer
[4953] │      └── Members
[4954] │
[4955] ├── Provider
[4956] │      └── Services
[4957] │
[4958] └── Plan
[4959] └── Network
[4960] Eligibility joins these entities.
[4961] Example:
[4962] Employer
[4963] ↓
[4964] Member
[4965] ↓
[4966] Plan
[4967] ↓
[4968] Network
[4969] ↓
[4970] Provider
[4971] ↓
[4972] Service
[4974] 5.53 Enrollment Change Management
[4975] Changes may include:
[4976] Plan change
[4977] Provider network change
[4978] Employer change
[4979] Employee status change
[4980] Dependant addition
[4981] Dependant removal
[4982] Coverage extension
[4983] Coverage termination
[4984] Every change must have:
[4985] Who
[4986] What
[4987] Before
[4988] After
[4989] When
[4990] Why
[4991] Approval
[4993] 5.54 Enrollment Termination
[4994] Termination must define:
[4995] Effective termination date
[4996] Reason
[4997] Initiator
[4998] Approval if required
[4999] Financial consequences
[5000] Existing authorization handling
[5001] Existing claims handling
[5002] Dependants
[5003] Outstanding services
[5004] Termination must not automatically erase historical data.
[5006] 5.55 Existing Authorizations After Termination
[5007] Example:
[5008] Authorization approved:
[5009] 10 August
[5011] Member coverage ends:
[5012] 15 August
[5014] Service date:
[5015] 20 August
[5016] The system must apply an explicit policy to determine whether the authorization remains valid.
[5017] This must never be decided accidentally by a generic status check.
[5019] 5.56 Claims After Enrollment Termination
[5020] A claim may arrive after a member has terminated.
[5021] The system should evaluate:
[5022] Service Date
[5023] Coverage Effective Period
[5024] Plan Version
[5025] Provider Status
[5026] Authorization
[5027] Claim Submission Date
[5028] A claim submission occurring after termination does not automatically mean the service itself was uncovered.
[5030] 5.57 Provider Contract Changes
[5031] Provider contracts should support versioning.
[5032] Contract v1
[5033] 01 Jan → 30 Jun
[5035] Contract v2
[5036] 01 Jul → 31 Dec
[5037] Tariffs, payment terms, service availability, and network status may change between versions.
[5038] Historical claims must reference the applicable contract version.
[5040] 5.58 Banking Information Changes
[5041] Provider bank-account changes are highly sensitive.
[5042] Workflow:
[5043] Provider Requests Change
[5044] ↓
[5045] Verification
[5046] ↓
[5047] Document Validation
[5048] ↓
[5049] Independent Confirmation
[5050] ↓
[5051] Approval
[5052] ↓
[5053] Security Monitoring
[5054] ↓
[5055] Activation
[5056] A provider user should not be able to change bank details and immediately approve their own settlement.
[5058] 5.59 Data Import Architecture
[5059] All external data imports should follow:
[5060] RECEIVE
[5061] ↓
[5062] AUTHENTICATE SOURCE
[5063] ↓
[5064] VALIDATE FORMAT
[5065] ↓
[5066] VALIDATE SCHEMA
[5067] ↓
[5068] VALIDATE BUSINESS RULES
[5069] ↓
[5070] DUPLICATE CHECK
[5071] ↓
[5072] PREVIEW
[5073] ↓
[5074] APPROVAL
[5075] ↓
[5076] PROCESS
[5077] ↓
[5078] RECONCILE
[5079] ↓
[5080] REPORT
[5082] 5.60 Master Data Correction
[5083] Sensitive master data corrections should require controlled workflows.
[5084] Examples:
[5085] Member date of birth
[5086] Identity information
[5087] Employer assignment
[5088] Provider legal name
[5089] Plan code
[5090] Coverage dates
[5091] The platform should preserve the original value and corrected value.
[5093] 5.61 Unknown Edge-Case Strategy
[5094] Unexpected scenarios should follow:
[5095] Unexpected Input
[5096] ↓
[5097] Validate
[5098] ↓
[5099] Reject or Place in Review
[5100] ↓
[5101] Preserve Existing State
[5102] ↓
[5103] Generate Error
[5104] ↓
[5105] Audit
[5106] ↓
[5107] Notify Where Necessary
[5108] ↓
[5109] Manual/Automated Recovery
[5110] The system must never silently:
[5111] create duplicate members,
[5112] activate invalid coverage,
[5113] change historical plans,
[5114] move a member to another employer,
[5115] expose another organization's records,
[5116] delete historical information.
[5118] 5.62 Cross-Module Integrity Rules
[5119] Rule 1
[5120] A claim cannot reference a nonexistent member.
[5121] Rule 2
[5122] An enrollment cannot reference a nonexistent plan.
[5123] Rule 3
[5124] An active enrollment cannot reference an inactive plan without an explicit historical/business rule.
[5125] Rule 4
[5126] Eligibility cannot return active coverage for an enrollment outside its effective period.
[5127] Rule 5
[5128] Provider service access must respect network rules.
[5129] Rule 6
[5130] Employer users cannot access members belonging to another employer unless explicitly authorized.
[5131] Rule 7
[5132] Historical transactions must preserve historical versions.
[5133] Rule 8
[5134] Member merges must preserve claims and financial history.
[5135] Rule 9
[5136] Provider termination must not erase historical claims.
[5137] Rule 10
[5138] Plan updates must not rewrite historical transactions.
[5140] 5.63 Core State-Machine Principle
[5141] Every major entity must have controlled transitions.
[5142] Example:
[5143] ACTIVE
[5144] ↓
[5145] SUSPENDED
[5146] ↓
[5147] REACTIVATED
[5148] should not allow:
[5149] ACTIVE
[5150] ↓
[5151] ARCHIVED
[5152] unless the business rules explicitly permit it.
[5153] Each transition must verify:
[5154] Current State
[5155] +
[5156] Requested State
[5157] +
[5158] User Permission
[5159] +
[5160] Business Rules
[5161] +
[5162] Required Approval
[5163] +
[5164] Effective Date
[5166] 5.64 Audit Requirements
[5167] Every critical lifecycle event should generate an audit record.
[5168] Examples:
[5169] MEMBER_CREATED
[5170] MEMBER_UPDATED
[5171] MEMBER_MERGED
[5172] MEMBER_SPLIT
[5173] DEPENDANT_ADDED
[5174] ENROLLMENT_CREATED
[5175] ENROLLMENT_APPROVED
[5176] ENROLLMENT_TERMINATED
[5177] PLAN_CHANGED
[5178] PROVIDER_APPROVED
[5179] PROVIDER_SUSPENDED
[5180] CREDENTIAL_EXPIRED
[5181] ELIGIBILITY_CHECKED
[5183] 5.65 Operational Dashboard
[5184] Management should have visibility into:
[5185] Members
[5186] Active members
[5187] Pending enrollment
[5188] Suspended members
[5189] Expired coverage
[5190] New members
[5191] Terminations
[5192] Employers
[5193] Active employers
[5194] Pending onboarding
[5195] Renewals
[5196] Suspensions
[5197] Employee counts
[5198] Providers
[5199] Active providers
[5200] Pending credentialing
[5201] Expiring credentials
[5202] Suspended providers
[5203] Contract expirations
[5204] Enrollment
[5205] Pending enrollments
[5206] Failed enrollments
[5207] Backdated requests
[5208] Future activations
[5209] Terminations
[5211] 5.66 Performance Requirements
[5212] Core operations should be designed for high-volume processing.
[5213] Examples:
[5214] Member lookup:
[5215] ≤ 2 seconds target
[5217] Eligibility check:
[5218] ≤ 1 second target
[5220] Standard enrollment:
[5221] ≤ 2 seconds target
[5223] Bulk upload:
[5224] Asynchronous processing
[5226] Provider search:
[5227] ≤ 2 seconds target
[5228] Actual targets should be validated during performance testing.
[5230] 5.67 Reliability Requirements
[5231] The architecture must support:
[5232] Idempotent enrollment
[5233] Idempotent member creation
[5234] Safe retries
[5235] Transaction integrity
[5236] Duplicate prevention
[5237] Queue-based bulk processing
[5238] Dead-letter queues
[5239] Recovery workflows
[5240] Reconciliation
[5241] Audit trails
[5243] 5.68 Availability Requirements
[5244] Critical services should be designed so that temporary failure in one subsystem does not unnecessarily bring down the entire HMO platform.
[5245] Example:
[5246] Notification Service DOWN
[5247] ↓
[5248] Enrollment still processes
[5249] ↓
[5250] Notification queued
[5251] ↓
[5252] Notification sent when service recovers
[5253] But:
[5254] Authorization Service DOWN
[5255] ↓
[5256] Sensitive operation
[5257] ↓
[5258] DENY / SAFE FAILURE
[5260] 5.69 Part V End-to-End Example
[5261] A new employee joins Employer ABC.
[5262] EMPLOYER
[5263] ↓
[5264] EMPLOYEE CREATED
[5265] ↓
[5266] MEMBER RECORD CREATED
[5267] ↓
[5268] DUPLICATE CHECK
[5269] ↓
[5270] PLAN SELECTED
[5271] ↓
[5272] ENROLLMENT CREATED
[5273] ↓
[5274] VALIDATION
[5275] ↓
[5276] APPROVAL
[5277] ↓
[5278] COVERAGE ACTIVATED
[5279] ↓
[5280] ELIGIBILITY AVAILABLE
[5281] ↓
[5282] MEMBER VISITS PROVIDER
[5283] ↓
[5284] PROVIDER VERIFIES ELIGIBILITY
[5285] ↓
[5286] SERVICE PROVIDED
[5287] ↓
[5288] CLAIM CREATED
[5289] This demonstrates why member, employer, provider, plan, enrollment, and eligibility data must be designed as one connected architecture.
[5291] 5.70 Part V Success Criteria
[5292] Part V is complete when the system can reliably:
[5293] Create and manage members
[5294] Manage dependants
[5295] Detect duplicate members
[5296] Safely merge records
[5297] Manage employers
[5298] Manage employer employees
[5299] Process bulk employee uploads
[5300] Manage providers
[5301] Manage provider users
[5302] Credential providers
[5303] Track credential expiration
[5304] Manage provider contracts
[5305] Manage provider networks
[5306] Create and version health plans
[5307] Configure benefits
[5308] Manage exclusions and limits
[5309] Create enrollments
[5310] Support effective dating
[5311] Support future-dated enrollment
[5312] Control backdated enrollment
[5313] Determine eligibility
[5314] Prevent cross-organization access
[5315] Preserve historical records
[5316] Handle lifecycle transitions
[5317] Audit important changes
[5318] Process high-volume operations safely
[5319] Fail safely when unexpected conditions occur
[5321] 5.71 Architectural Summary
[5322] The core HMO operational model is:
[5323] EMPLOYER
[5324] │
[5325] └── EMPLOYEE
[5326] │
[5327] └── MEMBER
[5328] │
[5329] ├── DEPENDANTS
[5330] │
[5331] └── ENROLLMENT
[5332] │
[5333] └── PLAN
[5334] │
[5335] ├── BENEFITS
[5336] ├── LIMITS
[5337] ├── EXCLUSIONS
[5338] └── PROVIDER NETWORK
[5339] │
[5340] └── PROVIDER
[5341] │
[5342] └── HEALTHCARE SERVICE
[5343] And eligibility is determined through:
[5344] MEMBER
[5345] +
[5346] ENROLLMENT
[5347] +
[5348] PLAN
[5349] +
[5350] BENEFIT
[5351] +
[5352] SERVICE
[5353] +
[5354] PROVIDER
[5355] +
[5356] NETWORK
[5357] +
[5358] EFFECTIVE DATE
[5359] +
[5360] AUTHORIZATION
[5361] =
[5362] ELIGIBILITY
[5363] This model becomes the foundation upon which preauthorization, claims, medical review, utilization management, provider billing, settlements, member service, analytics, and reporting will operate.
[5364] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[5365] PART VI — HEALTHCARE OPERATIONS & MEDICAL MANAGEMENT
[5367] 6.0 Purpose
[5368] This part defines how the HMO platform manages the healthcare delivery process from the moment a member requests care until the service is completed and becomes eligible for claims processing.
[5369] The healthcare operations architecture connects:
[5370] MEMBER
[5371] ↓
[5372] ELIGIBILITY
[5373] ↓
[5374] PROVIDER
[5375] ↓
[5376] SERVICE REQUEST
[5377] ↓
[5378] PREAUTHORIZATION
[5379] ↓
[5380] REFERRAL
[5381] ↓
[5382] MEDICAL REVIEW
[5383] ↓
[5384] CARE DELIVERY
[5385] ↓
[5386] UTILIZATION
[5387] ↓
[5388] CLAIM
[5389] The system must support both routine healthcare services and high-value/high-risk services requiring additional controls.
[5391] 6.1 Healthcare Operations Objectives
[5392] The platform shall provide:
[5393] Real-time eligibility verification
[5394] Healthcare service verification
[5395] Preauthorization
[5396] Referral management
[5397] Medical review
[5398] Utilization management
[5399] Clinical rule evaluation
[5400] Benefit-limit checking
[5401] Provider communication
[5402] Service tracking
[5403] Medical documentation
[5404] Exception handling
[5405] Fraud/risk indicators
[5406] Auditability
[5407] Integration with claims
[5409] 6.2 Healthcare Operations Principles
[5410] The system must follow these principles:
[5411] Principle 1 — Member First
[5412] The member's valid coverage should be determined quickly and accurately.
[5413] Principle 2 — Clinical Governance
[5414] Medical decisions should be made or reviewed by appropriately authorized medical personnel.
[5415] Principle 3 — Least Privilege
[5416] A user should only see the healthcare information necessary for their role.
[5417] Principle 4 — No Silent Clinical Overrides
[5418] Manual overrides must be explicitly recorded.
[5419] Principle 5 — Historical Integrity
[5420] Past authorization and medical decisions must not be silently rewritten.
[5421] Principle 6 — Explainable Decisions
[5422] Where a request is rejected, restricted, or sent for review, the system should record the applicable reason.
[5424] 6.3 Healthcare Service Master
[5425] The platform requires a standardized healthcare service catalogue.
[5426] Each service may contain:
[5427] Service code
[5428] Service name
[5429] Category
[5430] Description
[5431] Service type
[5432] Standard tariff
[5433] Plan coverage
[5434] Authorization requirement
[5435] Referral requirement
[5436] Frequency limit
[5437] Financial limit
[5438] Age restrictions
[5439] Gender restrictions where clinically/business appropriate
[5440] Provider restrictions
[5441] Network restrictions
[5442] Required documentation
[5444] 6.4 Service Categories
[5445] Example categories:
[5446] OUTPATIENT
[5447] INPATIENT
[5448] EMERGENCY
[5449] LABORATORY
[5450] RADIOLOGY
[5451] PHARMACY
[5452] SURGERY
[5453] SPECIALIST
[5454] DENTAL
[5455] OPTICAL
[5456] MATERNITY
[5457] DIAGNOSTICS
[5458] REHABILITATION
[5459] MENTAL_HEALTH
[5460] Actual categories should be configurable.
[5462] 6.5 Service Coding
[5463] Services should use standardized internal codes.
[5464] Example:
[5465] LAB-CBC
[5466] RAD-MRI-BRAIN
[5467] RAD-CT-CHEST
[5468] PHARM-DRUG-001
[5469] CONS-GEN
[5470] SURG-001
[5471] The system may also maintain mappings to external coding systems where required.
[5473] 6.6 Service Versioning
[5474] Healthcare services must support versioning.
[5475] Example:
[5476] Service:
[5477] MRI
[5479] Version 1:
[5480] Tariff = X
[5482] Version 2:
[5483] Tariff = Y
[5485] Effective Date:
[5486] 01 July
[5487] Historical claims should reference the service definition applicable at the relevant date.
[5489] 6.7 Eligibility Verification
[5490] Before healthcare service delivery:
[5491] Member Identified
[5492] ↓
[5493] Coverage Verified
[5494] ↓
[5495] Plan Verified
[5496] ↓
[5497] Provider Verified
[5498] ↓
[5499] Service Verified
[5500] ↓
[5501] Benefit Checked
[5502] ↓
[5503] Authorization Requirement Checked
[5505] 6.8 Real-Time Eligibility
[5506] Providers should be able to verify eligibility through:
[5507] Web portal
[5508] API
[5509] Mobile interface
[5510] Approved integration channels
[5511] A provider may submit:
[5512] Member ID
[5513] Provider ID
[5514] Service
[5515] Service Date
[5516] The platform evaluates eligibility.
[5518] 6.9 Eligibility Response
[5519] Responses should be concise and secure.
[5520] Example:
[5521] {
[5522] "status": "ELIGIBLE",
[5523] "coverageStatus": "ACTIVE",
[5524] "authorizationRequired": true
[5525] }
[5526] The response should not expose unnecessary medical or personal information.
[5528] 6.10 Eligibility Decision Reasons
[5529] Possible reasons include:
[5530] ACTIVE_COVERAGE
[5531] COVERAGE_EXPIRED
[5532] MEMBER_SUSPENDED
[5533] PLAN_NOT_ACTIVE
[5534] SERVICE_NOT_COVERED
[5535] BENEFIT_LIMIT_REACHED
[5536] PROVIDER_OUT_OF_NETWORK
[5537] AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRED
[5538] REFERRAL_REQUIRED
[5539] MANUAL_REVIEW_REQUIRED
[5541] 6.11 Preauthorization
[5542] Preauthorization is required for selected services before treatment.
[5543] Examples may include:
[5544] Major procedures
[5545] Surgery
[5546] High-cost diagnostics
[5547] Certain imaging
[5548] Certain specialist services
[5549] Inpatient admission
[5550] High-cost medications
[5551] Other configured services
[5553] 6.12 Preauthorization Lifecycle
[5554] REQUESTED
[5555] ↓
[5556] RECEIVED
[5557] ↓
[5558] VALIDATING
[5559] ↓
[5560] ELIGIBILITY_CHECK
[5561] ↓
[5562] CLINICAL_REVIEW
[5563] ↓
[5564] MEDICAL_REVIEW
[5565] ↓
[5566] APPROVAL_REVIEW
[5567] ↓
[5568] APPROVED
[5569] Alternative outcomes:
[5570] PARTIALLY_APPROVED
[5571] DENIED
[5572] PENDING_INFORMATION
[5573] EXPIRED
[5574] CANCELLED
[5575] ESCALATED
[5577] 6.13 Preauthorization Request
[5578] A request may contain:
[5579] Member
[5580] Member ID
[5581] Plan
[5582] Coverage status
[5583] Provider
[5584] Provider ID
[5585] Provider location
[5586] Attending provider
[5587] Clinical information
[5588] Diagnosis information where required
[5589] Requested service
[5590] Clinical justification
[5591] Relevant supporting documentation
[5592] Administrative information
[5593] Request date
[5594] Requested service date
[5595] Urgency
[5596] Expected cost
[5597] Referral information
[5599] 6.14 Preauthorization Validation
[5600] Before medical review:
[5601] Member exists
[5602] +
[5603] Member active
[5604] +
[5605] Plan active
[5606] +
[5607] Provider eligible
[5608] +
[5609] Service exists
[5610] +
[5611] Service covered
[5612] +
[5613] Authorization required
[5614] +
[5615] Required information present
[5616] Invalid requests should be returned for correction rather than being silently rejected.
[5618] 6.15 Urgency Classification
[5619] Requests may be classified as:
[5620] ROUTINE
[5621] URGENT
[5622] EMERGENCY
[5623] The system should apply different processing targets according to configured operational policies.
[5624] Emergency handling must not be used to bypass all controls automatically.
[5626] 6.16 Medical Review
[5627] Medical review should be performed by appropriately authorized medical personnel.
[5628] Possible roles:
[5629] Medical Officer
[5630] Medical Reviewer
[5631] Clinical Supervisor
[5632] Medical Director
[5633] The platform should not allow ordinary administrative users to make restricted clinical decisions.
[5635] 6.17 Medical Review Workflow
[5636] Request
[5637] ↓
[5638] Clinical Information Review
[5639] ↓
[5640] Policy/Benefit Review
[5641] ↓
[5642] Clinical Criteria Review
[5643] ↓
[5644] Reviewer Decision
[5645] ↓
[5646] Reason
[5647] ↓
[5648] Approval / Denial / More Information
[5650] 6.18 Medical Review Decisions
[5651] Possible decisions:
[5652] APPROVE
[5653] PARTIALLY_APPROVE
[5654] DENY
[5655] REQUEST_MORE_INFORMATION
[5656] REFER_TO_SPECIALIST
[5657] ESCALATE
[5658] Each decision must have:
[5659] Reviewer
[5660] Date/time
[5661] Decision
[5662] Reason
[5663] Supporting evidence/reference
[5664] Applicable rule
[5665] Version of policy/criteria
[5667] 6.19 Clinical Criteria
[5668] The system may support configurable criteria.
[5669] Example:
[5670] Requested Service:
[5671] MRI
[5673] Rule:
[5674] Authorization required
[5676] Evaluation:
[5677] Benefit available
[5678] +
[5679] Clinical criteria satisfied
[5680] +
[5681] Provider eligible
[5682] The rules engine should assist reviewers rather than automatically replacing professional judgment where human review is required.
[5684] 6.20 Clinical Rule Engine
[5685] The rule engine may evaluate:
[5686] Coverage
[5687] Age
[5688] Benefit
[5689] Frequency
[5690] Previous Services
[5691] Authorization
[5692] Provider
[5693] Plan
[5694] Diagnosis
[5695] Clinical criteria
[5696] Service history
[5697] The engine should return:
[5698] PASS
[5699] FAIL
[5700] REVIEW_REQUIRED
[5702] 6.21 Clinical Rule Versioning
[5703] Rules must be versioned.
[5704] Example:
[5705] Rule:
[5706] MRI Authorization
[5708] Version:
[5709] 2.1
[5711] Effective:
[5712] 01 August 2026
[5713] A decision made under version 2.1 should retain that reference.
[5715] 6.22 Rule Change Management
[5716] Changing clinical rules may have major operational consequences.
[5717] Changes should require:
[5718] Draft
[5719] ↓
[5720] Medical Review
[5721] ↓
[5722] Business Review
[5723] ↓
[5724] Configuration Validation
[5725] ↓
[5726] Approval
[5727] ↓
[5728] Effective Date
[5729] ↓
[5730] Activation
[5732] 6.23 Referral Management
[5733] Referrals may be required before specialist care.
[5734] Workflow:
[5735] Primary Provider
[5736] ↓
[5737] Referral Request
[5738] ↓
[5739] Eligibility
[5740] ↓
[5741] Referral Validation
[5742] ↓
[5743] Specialist Provider
[5744] ↓
[5745] Specialist Service
[5747] 6.24 Referral Record
[5748] A referral may include:
[5749] Referral ID
[5750] Member
[5751] Referring provider
[5752] Receiving provider
[5753] Referral reason
[5754] Service/specialty
[5755] Issue date
[5756] Expiry date
[5757] Status
[5758] Authorization relationship
[5760] 6.25 Referral Status
[5761] REQUESTED
[5762] APPROVED
[5763] ACTIVE
[5764] USED
[5765] EXPIRED
[5766] CANCELLED
[5767] REJECTED
[5769] 6.26 Referral Edge Cases
[5770] The system should handle:
[5771] Expired referral
[5772] Duplicate referral
[5773] Referral to non-network provider
[5774] Referral outside member scope
[5775] Referral cancellation
[5776] Referral modification
[5777] Multiple referrals for same service
[5778] Referral used beyond configured limit
[5780] 6.27 Utilization Management
[5781] Utilization management monitors how healthcare benefits are being used.
[5782] The platform may monitor:
[5783] Visits
[5784] Admissions
[5785] Procedures
[5786] Diagnostic tests
[5787] Medication usage
[5788] Specialist referrals
[5789] High-cost services
[5790] Frequency
[5791] Provider utilization
[5793] 6.28 Utilization Rules
[5794] Examples:
[5795] 10 consultations/year
[5796] or:
[5797] 2 MRI procedures/year
[5798] or:
[5799] Maximum financial limit
[5800] The limits must be associated with plan and benefit versions.
[5802] 6.29 Benefit Consumption
[5803] The system should track:
[5804] Annual Limit
[5805] Used
[5806] Remaining
[5807] Pending
[5808] Reserved
[5809] Example:
[5810] MRI Annual Limit: 2
[5812] Used: 1
[5813] Pending: 1
[5814] Remaining Available: 0
[5815] Pending authorized services may need to reserve part of the benefit.
[5817] 6.30 Concurrency in Benefit Limits
[5818] Two providers may request the same limited benefit simultaneously.
[5819] Example:
[5820] Available:
[5821] 1 MRI
[5823] Provider A → requests MRI
[5824] Provider B → requests MRI
[5825] The system must prevent both requests from consuming the same final allowance.
[5826] Use:
[5827] Transaction locks
[5828] Atomic counters
[5829] Reservation records
[5830] Idempotency keys
[5832] 6.31 Benefit Reservation
[5833] An approved authorization may reserve benefit capacity.
[5834] AVAILABLE
[5835] ↓
[5836] RESERVED
[5837] ↓
[5838] CONSUMED
[5839] If authorization expires:
[5840] RESERVED
[5841] ↓
[5842] RELEASED
[5843] ↓
[5844] AVAILABLE
[5846] 6.32 Service Utilization History
[5847] The system should maintain historical utilization.
[5848] Example:
[5849] Member
[5850] ↓
[5851] Service
[5852] ↓
[5853] Date
[5854] ↓
[5855] Provider
[5856] ↓
[5857] Authorization
[5858] ↓
[5859] Claim
[5860] ↓
[5861] Benefit Consumption
[5863] 6.33 High-Cost Service Monitoring
[5864] The platform should identify configured high-cost events.
[5865] Examples:
[5866] Major surgery
[5867] Long inpatient admission
[5868] High-cost diagnostics
[5869] High-cost medication
[5870] Repeated emergency visits
[5871] Such cases may trigger:
[5872] Medical Review
[5873] Utilization Review
[5874] Case Management
[5875] Financial Review
[5877] 6.34 Inpatient Management
[5878] For inpatient care, the system may track:
[5879] Admission
[5880] Diagnosis
[5881] Provider
[5882] Admission date
[5883] Expected discharge
[5884] Actual discharge
[5885] Services
[5886] Procedures
[5887] Room category
[5888] Authorization
[5889] Length of stay
[5890] Cost
[5892] 6.35 Length-of-Stay Monitoring
[5893] The system may compare:
[5894] Authorized Length
[5895] vs
[5896] Actual Length
[5897] If actual length exceeds the authorized period:
[5898] Extension Request
[5899] ↓
[5900] Medical Review
[5901] ↓
[5902] Approved / Denied / More Information
[5904] 6.36 Inpatient Extension
[5905] An extension request should reference the original authorization.
[5906] This prevents creation of disconnected authorizations for the same admission.
[5908] 6.37 Emergency Care
[5909] Emergency healthcare requires special handling.
[5910] The system should support:
[5911] Emergency Encounter
[5912] ↓
[5913] Eligibility
[5914] ↓
[5915] Emergency Treatment
[5916] ↓
[5917] Notification
[5918] ↓
[5919] Post-Service Review
[5920] Emergency rules should be configurable.
[5922] 6.38 Emergency Edge Cases
[5923] Examples:
[5924] Member cannot provide membership ID
[5925] Provider cannot reach eligibility service
[5926] Member coverage cannot immediately be confirmed
[5927] Emergency treatment occurs before authorization
[5928] Duplicate emergency encounter
[5929] Emergency later determined not to meet configured criteria
[5930] The system should preserve the encounter and support later verification.
[5932] 6.39 Pharmacy Management
[5933] The pharmacy module may manage:
[5934] Drug catalogue
[5935] Medication codes
[5936] Formulary
[5937] Covered medications
[5938] Exclusions
[5939] Quantity limits
[5940] Frequency limits
[5941] Authorization requirements
[5942] Provider/pharmacy network
[5943] Dispensing records
[5945] 6.40 Drug/Formulary Rules
[5946] A drug may be:
[5947] COVERED
[5948] COVERED_WITH_LIMIT
[5949] AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRED
[5950] NOT_COVERED
[5951] ALTERNATIVE_REQUIRED
[5952] The exact outcome must be traceable to the applicable rule.
[5954] 6.41 Medication Authorization
[5955] High-cost or restricted medications may require preauthorization.
[5956] Workflow:
[5957] Prescription
[5958] ↓
[5959] Eligibility
[5960] ↓
[5961] Formulary Check
[5962] ↓
[5963] Limit Check
[5964] ↓
[5965] Authorization
[5966] ↓
[5967] Dispensing
[5968] ↓
[5969] Claim
[5971] 6.42 Laboratory Management

[5973] Test catalogue

[5976] Coverage
[5977] Authorization rules
[5978] Frequency limits
[5979] Tariffs
[5980] Result references where applicable
[5982] 6.43 Diagnostic Services
[5983] Diagnostic services may include:
[5984] X-ray
[5985] CT
[5986] MRI
[5987] Ultrasound
[5988] Other configured diagnostics
[5989] Each should have:
[5990] Service Code
[5991] Coverage

[5993] Authorization Requirement
[5994] Provider Requirement
[5995] Tariff
