[6015] 6.45 Provider Communication
[6016] The platform should provide controlled communication for:
[6017] Authorization requests
[6018] Missing documents
[6019] Review decisions
[6020] Claim corrections
[6021] Referral requests
[6022] Service notifications
[6023] Sensitive medical information must only be transmitted through authorized channels.
[6025] 6.46 Medical Documents
[6026] Documents may include:
[6027] Referral letters
[6028] Clinical notes
[6029] Diagnostic reports
[6030] Supporting medical documentation
[6031] Authorization attachments
[6032] Discharge summaries
[6033] Each document requires:
[6034] Access Control
[6035] Encryption
[6036] Metadata
[6037] Version
[6038] Uploader
[6039] Timestamp
[6040] Audit
[6041] Retention
[6043] 6.47 Medical Data Access
[6044] Medical information must be subject to enhanced access controls.
[6045] For example:
[6046] Customer Service
[6047] → Limited
[6049] Claims Officer
[6050] → Minimum required
[6052] Medical Reviewer
[6053] → Authorized clinical information
[6055] Medical Director
[6056] → Broader authorized clinical information
[6058] System Administrator
[6059] → Technical access only
[6061] 6.48 Sensitive Medical Access Logging
[6062] Every sensitive access should generate an audit event.
[6063] Examples:
[6064] MEDICAL_RECORD_VIEWED
[6065] CLINICAL_DOCUMENT_DOWNLOADED
[6066] MEDICAL_DECISION_CREATED
[6067] MEDICAL_DECISION_CHANGED
[6068] MEDICAL_DATA_EXPORTED
[6070] 6.49 Clinical Override
[6071] Authorized medical personnel may need to override an automated rule.
[6072] Example:
[6073] System:
[6074] Benefit limit reached
[6076] Medical Reviewer:
[6077] Requests exception
[6078] Override requires:
[6079] Reason
[6080] User identity
[6081] Medical justification where appropriate
[6082] Supporting information
[6083] Timestamp
[6084] Policy reference
[6085] Approval where required
[6086] The system must never hide the automated decision.
[6088] 6.50 Override Hierarchy
[6089] Possible levels:
[6090] Standard Reviewer
[6091] ↓
[6092] Senior Medical Reviewer
[6093] ↓
[6094] Medical Manager
[6095] ↓
[6096] Medical Director
[6097] The exact hierarchy should be configurable.
[6099] 6.51 Manual Review Queue
[6100] Requests requiring human intervention should enter queues.
[6101] Examples:
[6102] Clinical Review
[6103] Eligibility Exception
[6104] High-Cost Review
[6105] Provider Exception
[6106] Fraud Review
[6107] Missing Documentation
[6108] Each queue should support:
[6109] Assignment
[6110] Priority
[6111] SLA timer
[6112] Escalation
[6113] Reassignment
[6114] Notes
[6115] Audit
[6117] 6.52 Work Assignment
[6118] The system may assign cases based on:
[6119] Specialty
[6120] Branch
[6121] Region
[6122] Workload
[6123] Expertise
[6124] Availability
[6125] Conflict of interest
[6126] Case risk
[6127] Users should not be able to arbitrarily assign sensitive cases to themselves where policy prohibits it.
[6129] 6.53 Queue Prioritization
[6130] A queue may rank cases using:
[6131] Urgency
[6132] Clinical risk
[6133] Financial risk
[6134] Age of request
[6135] SLA
[6136] Member impact
[6137] Provider impact
[6138] Automated prioritization must remain explainable.
[6140] 6.54 SLA Management
[6141] Healthcare operations may have configurable SLAs.
[6142] Example:
[6143] Routine
[6144] → Standard processing target
[6146] Urgent
[6147] → Shorter target
[6149] Emergency
[6150] → Immediate/priority workflow
[6151] The platform should track:
[6152] Created time
[6153] First response
[6154] Current age
[6155] SLA target
[6156] Time remaining
[6157] Escalation status
[6159] 6.55 Escalation
[6160] Example:
[6161] Case Created
[6162] ↓
[6163] 75% SLA consumed
[6164] ↓
[6165] Reminder
[6166] ↓
[6167] SLA exceeded
[6168] ↓
[6169] Supervisor Escalation
[6170] ↓
[6171] Management Escalation
[6173] 6.56 Duplicate Service Requests
[6174] The system should detect potential duplicate requests.
[6175] Example:
[6176] Same member
[6177] +
[6178] Same provider
[6179] +
[6180] Same service
[6181] +
[6182] Same date
[6183] +
[6184] Same clinical context
[6185] may trigger:
[6186] DUPLICATE_REVIEW
[6187] Automatic rejection should only occur where explicitly supported by business rules.
[6189] 6.57 Duplicate Authorization
[6190] If the same request is submitted twice because of network retry:
[6191] Same Idempotency Key
[6192] the system must return the original result rather than creating a second authorization.
[6194] 6.58 Offline Provider Scenario
[6195] If a provider temporarily loses connectivity:
[6196] Provider Offline
[6197] ↓
[6198] Service Request Stored Locally
[6199] ↓
[6200] Connection Restored
[6201] ↓
[6202] Request Submitted
[6203] ↓
[6204] Server Validation
[6205] Offline functionality must be carefully constrained so it cannot bypass authorization.
[6207] 6.59 Authorization Service Failure
[6208] If eligibility or authorization services become unavailable:
[6209] Critical authorization unavailable
[6210] ↓
[6211] Do not falsely return "eligible"
[6212] ↓
[6213] Return controlled unavailable/review response
[6214] For emergency care, a configured emergency workflow may allow treatment followed by post-service verification.
[6216] 6.60 Healthcare Fraud/Risk Indicators
[6217] The platform may flag:
[6218] Repeated identical service requests
[6219] Unusual service frequency
[6220] Unusually high provider utilization
[6221] Suspicious billing patterns
[6222] Repeated cancelled authorizations
[6223] Unusual provider-member relationships
[6224] High-cost services outside normal patterns
[6225] Repeated duplicate requests
[6226] Excessive emergency encounters
[6227] Flags should trigger review rather than automatically declaring fraud.
[6229] 6.61 Healthcare Operations Audit
[6230] Important events include:
[6231] ELIGIBILITY_CHECKED
[6232] AUTHORIZATION_CREATED
[6233] AUTHORIZATION_APPROVED
[6234] AUTHORIZATION_DENIED
[6235] AUTHORIZATION_OVERRIDDEN
[6236] REFERRAL_CREATED
[6237] REFERRAL_APPROVED
[6238] CLINICAL_REVIEW_COMPLETED
[6239] BENEFIT_RESERVED
[6240] BENEFIT_RELEASED
[6241] SERVICE_RECORDED
[6242] MEDICAL_DOCUMENT_ACCESSED
[6244] 6.62 Healthcare State Integrity
[6245] An authorization cannot move directly from:
[6246] REQUESTED
[6247] to:
[6248] PAID
[6249] without the appropriate healthcare and claims workflow.
[6250] Likewise:
[6251] DENIED
[6252] cannot become:
[6253] APPROVED
[6254] without an authorized reconsideration/override process.
[6256] 6.63 Reconsideration
[6257] A denied request may be reconsidered.
[6258] Workflow:
[6259] DENIED
[6260] ↓
[6261] RECONSIDERATION REQUEST
[6262] ↓
[6263] NEW INFORMATION
[6264] ↓
[6265] REVIEW
[6266] ↓
[6267] APPROVED / DENIED
[6268] The original decision must remain preserved.
[6270] 6.64 Healthcare Audit Trail
[6271] For every medical decision, preserve:
[6272] Original Request
[6273] Reviewer
[6274] Decision
[6275] Reason
[6276] Rule Version
[6277] Clinical Evidence
[6278] Override
[6279] Approval
[6280] Timestamp
[6282] 6.65 Performance Requirements
[6283] Targets should include:
[6284] Eligibility API:
[6285] ≤ 1 second target
[6287] Standard authorization submission:
[6288] ≤ 2 seconds acknowledgement
[6290] Provider search:
[6291] ≤ 2 seconds target
[6293] Dashboard:
[6294] ≤ 3 seconds target
[6295] Long-running medical review should be asynchronous.
[6297] 6.66 Scalability
[6298] Healthcare operations may experience sudden spikes.
[6299] Examples:
[6300] Employer enrollment campaigns
[6301] New plan launches
[6302] Large provider network
[6303] Corporate renewal
[6304] Seasonal demand
[6305] The architecture should support:
[6306] Horizontal scaling
[6307] Queue processing
[6308] Async medical review
[6309] Caching
[6310] Database read replicas
[6311] Rate limiting
[6312] Backpressure
[6314] 6.67 Reliability
[6315] Healthcare operations require:
[6316] Idempotent requests
[6317] Retry handling
[6318] Duplicate detection
[6319] Transaction integrity
[6320] Queue recovery
[6321] Dead-letter queues
[6322] Reconciliation
[6323] Monitoring
[6324] A provider retrying a request must not accidentally create duplicate authorization.
[6326] 6.68 Unknown Edge-Case Framework
[6327] When an unexpected healthcare scenario occurs:
[6328] Unexpected Condition
[6329] ↓
[6330] Do Not Guess
[6331] ↓
[6332] Preserve Current State
[6333] ↓
[6334] Place in REVIEW if necessary
[6335] ↓
[6336] Log
[6337] ↓
[6338] Audit
[6339] ↓
[6340] Notify
[6341] ↓
[6342] Controlled Resolution
[6343] The platform must avoid silently making clinical decisions when the applicable rule is unclear.
[6345] 6.69 End-to-End Healthcare Example
[6346] A member needs an MRI.
[6347] MEMBER
[6348] ↓
[6349] Provider identifies member
[6350] ↓
[6351] Eligibility check
[6352] ↓
[6353] Plan identified
[6354] ↓
[6355] MRI benefit checked
[6356] ↓
[6357] Authorization required
[6358] ↓
[6359] Provider submits request
[6360] ↓
[6361] Clinical information validated
[6362] ↓
[6363] Medical reviewer assigned
[6364] ↓
[6365] Clinical criteria evaluated
[6366] ↓
[6367] Decision
[6368] ↓
[6369] Authorization approved
[6370] ↓
[6371] Benefit reservation
[6372] ↓
[6373] MRI performed
[6374] ↓
[6375] Service recorded
[6376] ↓
[6377] Claim submitted
[6378] ↓
[6379] Claim references authorization
[6380] This creates a controlled relationship between healthcare delivery and financial processing.
[6382] 6.70 Part VI Success Criteria
[6383] Part VI is complete when the platform can:
[6384] Verify member eligibility
[6385] Validate provider eligibility
[6386] Determine benefit availability
[6387] Manage healthcare services
[6388] Manage preauthorization
[6389] Manage referrals
[6390] Support medical review
[6391] Apply configurable clinical rules
[6392] Track benefit consumption
[6393] Prevent duplicate authorization
[6394] Support emergency workflows
[6395] Manage inpatient cases
[6396] Manage pharmacy rules
[6397] Manage laboratory services
[6398] Manage diagnostics
[6399] Track utilization
[6400] Manage clinical overrides
[6401] Protect medical information
[6402] Audit sensitive access
[6403] Support reconsideration
[6404] Manage SLAs and escalations
[6405] Handle service failures safely
[6406] Scale during high demand
[6407] Integrate healthcare operations with claims
[6409] 6.71 Healthcare Operations Architecture Summary
[6410] The complete healthcare workflow is:
[6411] MEMBER
[6412] ↓
[6413] ELIGIBILITY
[6414] ↓
[6415] PROVIDER
[6416] ↓
[6417] SERVICE
[6418] ↓
[6419] BENEFIT CHECK
[6420] ↓
[6421] AUTHORIZATION REQUIREMENT
[6422] ↓
[6423] PREAUTHORIZATION
[6424] ↓
[6425] CLINICAL REVIEW
[6426] ↓
[6427] APPROVAL
[6428] ↓
[6429] BENEFIT RESERVATION
[6430] ↓
[6431] CARE DELIVERY
[6432] ↓
[6433] UTILIZATION RECORD
[6434] ↓
[6435] CLAIM
[6436] The key architectural principle is:
[6437] The HMO platform should know not only whether a member is active, but whether a specific member is entitled to a specific service, from a specific provider, at a specific time, under a specific plan version and benefit rule.
[6438] That distinction is fundamental to accurate HMO operations.
[6439] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[6440] Part VII — Claims Management & Adjudication Architecture
[6441] Document Type: Enterprise HMO Master Blueprint
Part: VII of the Master Blueprint
Primary Domain: Claims, Adjudication, Review, Approval & Payment Readiness
Target Market: Nigerian HMO Operations
Architecture Principles: Performance, Security, Scalability, Reliability, Usability
[6443] 7.0 PURPOSE
[6444] The Claims Management and Adjudication module is responsible for receiving, validating, evaluating, approving, rejecting, correcting, disputing, and preparing healthcare claims for settlement.
[6445] The module must establish a controlled relationship between:
[6446] Member → Coverage → Provider → Service → Authorization → Benefit → Tariff → Claim → Adjudication → Approval → Payment → Reconciliation
[6447] The platform must never treat a claim simply as an invoice.
[6448] A claim is a financial and healthcare record representing services allegedly delivered to an eligible member under a specific coverage arrangement.
[6449] Therefore, every claim decision must be:
[6450] Traceable
[6451] Explainable
[6452] Reproducible
[6453] Authorized
[6454] Auditable
[6455] Version-aware
[6456] Financially consistent
[6457] Protected against duplicate processing
[6459] 7.1 CLAIMS MANAGEMENT OBJECTIVES
[6460] The system shall:
[6461] Receive claims through multiple channels.
[6462] Validate claims automatically.
[6463] Verify member eligibility for the service date.
[6464] Verify provider eligibility.
[6465] Verify plan and benefit coverage.
[6466] Verify authorization requirements.
[6467] Detect duplicate claims.
[6468] Apply provider-specific tariffs.
[6469] Apply member benefit rules.
[6470] Calculate HMO liability.
[6471] Calculate member responsibility where applicable.
[6472] Identify claims requiring clinical review.
[6473] Identify claims requiring fraud/risk review.
[6474] Route claims through appropriate approval workflows.
[6475] Prevent unauthorized claim approval.
[6476] Support partial approvals.
[6477] Support claim corrections and resubmissions.
[6478] Support disputes and reconsiderations.
[6479] Generate payment-ready claims.
[6480] Maintain complete historical records.
[6481] Integrate approved claims with financial operations.
[6482] Prevent double payment.
[6483] Provide provider-facing claim visibility.
[6484] Provide management dashboards and analytics.
[6486] 7.2 CLAIM SOURCES
[6487] Claims may originate from:
[6488] Provider Portal
[6489] Providers manually submit claims through the provider portal.
[6490] Provider API
[6491] Large healthcare providers may submit claims programmatically.
[6492] Bulk Upload
[6493] Providers may submit large batches of claims using approved file formats.
[6494] Internal HMO Entry
[6495] Authorized HMO employees may create claims on behalf of providers under controlled permissions.
[6496] System-Generated Claims
[6497] Certain contracted arrangements may generate claims automatically.
[6498] Migration
[6499] Historical claims may be imported from legacy systems.
[6500] Migrated claims must be clearly identified as migrated records.
[6502] 7.3 CLAIM TYPES
[6503] The system should support configurable claim categories including:
[6504] Outpatient
[6505] Inpatient
[6506] Emergency
[6507] Pharmacy
[6508] Laboratory
[6509] Diagnostic
[6510] Specialist
[6511] Surgery
[6512] Maternity
[6513] Dental
[6514] Optical
[6515] Physiotherapy
[6516] Mental/behavioral healthcare where supported
[6517] Ambulance
[6518] Home healthcare where supported
[6519] Capitation-related claims
[6520] Other contracted healthcare services
[6521] The claim-type catalogue must be configurable rather than hard-coded.
[6523] 7.4 CLAIM STRUCTURE
[6524] A claim consists of multiple logical components.
[6525] Claim Header
[6526] Contains:
[6527] Claim ID
[6528] Claim number
[6529] Organization ID
[6530] Provider ID
[6531] Provider location
[6532] Member ID
[6533] Employer ID where applicable
[6534] Plan ID
[6535] Plan version
[6536] Encounter ID
[6537] Authorization ID
[6538] Referral ID
[6539] Claim type
[6540] Service start date
[6541] Service end date
[6542] Submission date
[6543] Currency
[6544] Total provider amount
[6545] Total approved amount
[6546] Total rejected amount
[6547] Member responsibility
[6548] HMO liability
[6549] Claim status
[6550] Priority
[6551] Risk status
[6552] Clinical review status
[6553] Financial status
[6555] 7.5 CLAIM LINE
[6556] Each claim may contain multiple service lines.
[6557] Example:
[6558] Every claim line should be independently traceable.
[6560] 7.6 CLAIM LIFECYCLE
[6561] The standard lifecycle should be:
[6562] DRAFT
[6563] ↓
[6564] SUBMITTED
[6565] ↓
[6566] RECEIVED
[6567] ↓
[6568] VALIDATING
[6569] ↓
[6570] VALIDATION_FAILED
[6571] ↓
[6572] ACCEPTED
[6573] ↓
[6574] ELIGIBILITY_CHECK
[6575] ↓
[6576] DUPLICATE_CHECK
[6577] ↓
[6578] BENEFIT_CHECK
[6579] ↓
[6580] AUTHORIZATION_CHECK
[6581] ↓
[6582] TARIFF_PRICING
[6583] ↓
[6584] CLINICAL_REVIEW
[6585] ↓
[6586] RISK_REVIEW
[6587] ↓
[6588] ADJUDICATION
[6589] ↓
[6590] APPROVAL_REVIEW
[6591] ↓
[6592] APPROVED / PARTIALLY_APPROVED / REJECTED
[6593] ↓
[6594] PAYMENT_PENDING
[6595] ↓
[6596] PAYMENT_PROCESSING
[6597] ↓
[6598] PAID
[6599] ↓
[6600] RECONCILED
[6601] Exception states may include:
[6602] RETURNED_FOR_CORRECTION
[6603] DISPUTED
[6604] RESUBMITTED
[6605] CANCELLED
[6606] REVERSED
[6607] ON_HOLD
[6608] UNDER_INVESTIGATION
[6609] Not every claim must pass through every stage.
[6610] The workflow should be dynamically determined by claim characteristics.
[6612] 7.7 CLAIM SUBMISSION VALIDATION
[6613] Upon submission, the system performs multiple validation layers.
[6614] Layer 1 — Structural Validation
[6615] Verify:
[6616] Required fields
[6617] Data types
[6618] Valid formats
[6619] Valid dates
[6620] Valid amounts
[6621] Valid identifiers
[6622] Layer 2 — Provider Validation
[6623] Verify:
[6624] Provider exists
[6625] Provider is active
[6626] Provider location exists
[6627] Provider is contracted
[6628] Provider contract is valid for service date
[6629] Layer 3 — Member Validation
[6630] Verify:
[6631] Member exists
[6632] Member identity is valid
[6633] Member was covered on service date
[6634] Member belongs to the appropriate plan
[6635] Layer 4 — Service Validation
[6636] Verify:
[6637] Service exists
[6638] Service code is valid
[6639] Service date is valid
[6640] Quantity is reasonable according to configured rules
[6641] Service is compatible with claim type
[6642] Layer 5 — Authorization Validation
[6643] Verify:
[6644] Authorization exists where required
[6645] Authorization belongs to the member
[6646] Authorization belongs to the provider
[6647] Authorization covers the service
[6648] Authorization covers the relevant dates
[6649] Approved quantity has not been exceeded
[6650] Layer 6 — Financial Validation
[6651] Verify:
[6652] Amount is valid
[6653] Currency is valid
[6654] Tariff exists
[6655] Provider pricing applies
[6656] No invalid negative amounts
[6657] No impossible calculations
[6659] 7.8 CLAIM DUPLICATE DETECTION
[6660] Duplicate claims represent a major operational and financial risk.
[6661] The system should compare:
[6662] Member
[6663] Provider
[6664] Service date
[6665] Service code
[6666] Quantity
[6667] Amount
[6668] Authorization
[6669] Encounter
[6670] Provider reference
[6671] Claim reference
[6672] Submission timestamp
[6673] Duplicate detection should support multiple confidence levels.
[6674] Possible Results
[6675] NO_DUPLICATE
[6676] POSSIBLE_DUPLICATE
[6677] LIKELY_DUPLICATE
[6678] CONFIRMED_DUPLICATE
[6679] A possible duplicate should normally enter a review queue rather than automatically being declared fraud.
[6681] 7.9 IDEMPOTENT CLAIM SUBMISSION
[6682] Every API submission should support an idempotency key.
[6683] Example:
[6684] Idempotency-Key:
[6685] CLAIM_PROVIDER_20261001_000123
[6686] If the provider accidentally submits the same request twice, the system must not create two independent claims.
[6687] The response should return the existing claim where appropriate.
[6689] 7.10 HISTORICAL ELIGIBILITY
[6690] Eligibility must be evaluated against the service date, not simply the current date.
[6691] For example:
[6692] Service Date: 10 March
[6693] Current Date: 20 April
[6694] Member terminated: 31 March
[6695] The member may still have been eligible on 10 March.
[6696] Therefore:
[6697] Current membership status ≠ historical eligibility.
[6698] The system must evaluate:
[6699] Coverage period
[6700] Plan version
[6701] Benefit version
[6702] Provider contract version
[6703] Network status
[6704] Authorization validity
[6705] Applicable tariff
[6706] Service date
[6708] 7.11 PLAN VERSION CONTROL
[6709] Claims must reference the plan version applicable at the time of service.
[6710] Example:
[6711] Plan A
[6712] Version 1
[6713] Effective: Jan 1 – Jun 30
[6715] Plan A
[6716] Version 2
[6717] Effective: Jul 1 – Dec 31
[6718] A service provided on June 29 must be adjudicated using Version 1 even if the claim is submitted in July.
[6719] Historical business rules must therefore remain immutable.
[6721] 7.12 BENEFIT CHECK
[6722] The adjudication engine must determine whether a service is:
[6723] Covered
[6724] Partially covered
[6725] Excluded
[6726] Subject to authorization
[6727] Subject to referral
[6728] Subject to a monetary limit
[6729] Subject to quantity/frequency limits
[6730] Subject to co-payment
[6731] Subject to deductible
[6732] Subject to waiting period
[6733] Subject to network restrictions
[6735] 7.13 BENEFIT CONSUMPTION
[6736] The system should maintain benefit utilization.
[6737] Example:
[6738] Annual Benefit Limit:
[6739] ₦500,000
[6741] Previously Used:
[6742] ₦320,000
[6744] Current Eligible Amount:
[6745] ₦100,000
[6747] Remaining:
[6748] ₦80,000
[6749] The system must prevent concurrent claims from consuming the same remaining benefit incorrectly.
[6750] This requires controlled reservation and transaction handling.
[6752] 7.14 BENEFIT RESERVATION
[6753] For claims or services requiring reservation:
[6754] AVAILABLE
[6755] ↓
[6756] RESERVED
[6757] ↓
[6758] CONSUMED
[6759] If the transaction fails:
[6760] RESERVED
[6761] ↓
[6762] RELEASED
[6763] If a claim is reversed:
[6764] CONSUMED
[6765] ↓
[6766] ADJUSTED / RESTORED
[6767] The exact restoration policy must be configurable.
[6769] 7.15 AUTHORIZATION MATCHING
[6770] Where a claim requires prior authorization, the system must compare:
[6771] Member
[6772] Provider
[6773] Service
[6774] Authorization number
[6775] Service date
[6776] Quantity
[6777] Approved amount
[6778] Diagnosis/category where applicable
[6779] Authorization status
[6780] Example:
[6781] Authorization:
[6782] MRI
[6783] Quantity: 1
[6784] Valid: 1–15 October
[6786] Claim:
[6787] MRI
[6788] Quantity: 1
[6789] Service date: 10 October
[6790] The claim can proceed to adjudication.
[6791] If:
[6792] Claim quantity: 2
[6793] the second unit should be flagged according to configured business rules.
[6795] 7.16 TARIFF AND PRICING ENGINE
[6796] The pricing engine determines the applicable financial terms.
[6797] Possible inputs:
[6798] Provider
[6799] Provider location
[6800] Service
[6801] Service date
[6802] Provider contract
[6803] Contract version
[6804] Plan
[6805] Negotiated tariff
[6806] Quantity
[6807] Discount
[6808] Co-payment
[6809] Deductible
[6810] Benefit limit
[6811] Special pricing agreement
[6812] Pricing must be effective-dated.
[6813] Example:
[6814] Provider Contract Version 3
[6815] Effective:
[6816] 01/07/2026
[6818] Service:
[6819] MRI
[6821] Agreed Tariff:
[6822] ₦120,000
[6823] A claim for the service on 20 June must not automatically use the July tariff.
[6825] 7.17 ADJUDICATION ENGINE
[6826] The adjudication engine applies deterministic business rules.
[6827] Example sequence:
[6828] 1. Validate claim
[6829] 2. Confirm member eligibility
[6830] 3. Confirm provider eligibility
[6831] 4. Confirm service
[6832] 5. Confirm authorization
[6833] 6. Determine benefit
[6834] 7. Determine tariff
[6835] 8. Determine limits
[6836] 9. Determine member responsibility
[6837] 10. Determine HMO liability
[6838] 11. Apply adjustments
[6839] 12. Generate decision
[6840] 13. Record rule versions
[6841] The order of rules must be explicitly defined.
[6843] 7.18 EXPLAINABLE ADJUDICATION
[6844] Every automated decision should be explainable.
[6845] Instead of:
[6846] Rejected
[6847] The system should produce:
[6848] Decision:
[6849] REJECTED
[6851] Reason:
[6852] Service excluded under plan benefit configuration.
[6854] Rule:
[6855] BENEFIT.EXCLUSION.001
[6857] Rule Version:
[6858] 2026.4
[6860] Applied:
[6861] 2026-10-01 14:31:05
[6863] Source:
[6864] Plan Version 7
[6865] This allows staff to understand why the decision occurred.
[6867] 7.19 CLAIM LINE ADJUDICATION
[6868] Each line should produce a financial breakdown.
[6869] Example:
[6870] Provider Charge:       ₦150,000
[6871] Contract Tariff:       ₦120,000
[6872] Allowed Amount:        ₦120,000
[6873] Benefit Covered:       ₦100,000
[6874] Member Responsibility: ₦20,000
[6875] HMO Liability:         ₦100,000
[6876] Rejected Amount:       ₦30,000
[6877] This information must flow into payment and reporting systems.
[6879] 7.20 PARTIAL APPROVAL
[6880] Claims may be partially approved.
[6881] Example:
[6882] Provider submits:
[6883] Total: ₦500,000
[6884] Adjudication determines:
[6885] Approved: ₦350,000
[6886] Rejected: ₦100,000
[6887] Member Responsibility: ₦50,000
[6888] The system must provide reason codes for every rejected or adjusted portion.
[6890] 7.21 RETURN FOR CORRECTION VS REJECTION
[6891] These are different outcomes.
[6892] Return for Correction
[6893] The claim may become valid after missing information is supplied.
[6894] Example:
[6895] Missing invoice
[6896] Incorrect service date
[6897] Missing authorization reference
[6898] Invalid attachment
[6899] Rejection
[6900] The claim fails a substantive rule.
[6901] Example:
[6902] Service excluded
[6903] Provider not contracted
[6904] Member not covered
[6905] Benefit exhausted
[6906] The system must not use rejection simply because information is missing.
[6908] 7.22 CLINICAL REVIEW
[6909] Claims requiring clinical assessment should enter a clinical review queue.
[6910] Reviewers may assess:
[6911] Medical necessity
[6912] Clinical documentation
[6913] Diagnosis
[6914] Procedure relationship
[6915] Authorization
[6916] Treatment pattern
[6917] Supporting medical records
[6918] Clinical decisions must be performed by appropriately authorized personnel.
[6919] The system should support:
[6920] APPROVE
[6921] PARTIALLY_APPROVE
[6922] REJECT
[6923] REQUEST_INFORMATION
[6924] ESCALATE
[6925] All clinical decisions must be recorded.
[6927] 7.23 FRAUD AND RISK REVIEW
[6928] The system may identify risk indicators such as:
[6929] Duplicate services
[6930] Unusual service frequency
[6931] Unusual claim amounts
[6932] Sudden provider billing increases
[6933] Repeated identical claims
[6934] Claims immediately before termination
[6935] Unusual after-hours patterns
[6936] Abnormal provider/member relationships
[6937] Repeated rejected claims followed by altered submissions
[6938] Suspicious billing combinations
[6939] These indicators should produce:
[6940] RISK_FLAG
[6941] A risk flag should not automatically mean that fraud has occurred.
[6942] The claim may be routed to an authorized investigation team.
[6944] 7.24 CLAIM APPROVAL WORKFLOW
[6945] Approval should be dynamically determined.
[6946] Inputs may include:
[6947] Claim amount
[6948] Claim type
[6949] Provider
[6950] Risk score
[6951] Clinical complexity
[6952] Department
[6953] User role
[6954] User scope
[6955] Exception status
[6956] Previous decisions
[6957] Example:
[6958] Low-value standard claim
[6959] ↓
[6960] Supervisor Review
[6962] High-value claim
[6963] ↓
[6964] Manager Review
[6966] High-risk claim
[6967] ↓
[6968] Special Review
[6970] Exceptional claim
[6971] ↓
[6972] Executive/Designated Approval
[6973] Thresholds must be configurable.
[6975] 7.25 SEGREGATION OF DUTIES
[6976] The system must prevent conflicts such as:
[6977] Claim Creator
[6978] ≠
[6979] Claim Approver
[6980] And:
[6981] Claim Approver
[6982] ≠
[6983] Payment Executor
[6984] Additional rules may include:
[6985] Provider Bank Account Modifier
[6986] ≠
[6987] Settlement Approver
[6988] The system should evaluate SoD both when assigning permissions and at transaction time.
[6990] 7.26 CLAIM CORRECTION
[6991] A claim may be returned for correction.
[6992] The corrected submission must not overwrite the original history.
[6993] Instead:
[6994] Claim Version 1
[6995] ↓
[6996] Correction Requested
[6997] ↓
[6998] Claim Version 2
[6999] The system must preserve:
[7000] Original data
[7001] Corrected data
[7002] Who changed it
[7003] Why it changed
[7004] When it changed
[7005] Supporting documents
[7006] Previous decisions
[7008] 7.27 CLAIM RESUBMISSION
[7009] A rejected or returned claim may be resubmitted if policy permits.
[7010] The system must link:
[7011] Original Claim
[7012] ↓
[7013] Correction
[7014] ↓
[7015] Resubmitted Claim
[7016] This prevents repeated submission from appearing as an unrelated claim.
[7018] 7.28 CLAIM DISPUTES AND RECONSIDERATION
[7019] Providers should be able to dispute eligible decisions.
[7020] Lifecycle:
[7021] DISPUTE_SUBMITTED
[7022] ↓
[7023] ACKNOWLEDGED
[7024] ↓
[7025] ASSIGNED
[7026] ↓
[7027] UNDER_REVIEW
[7028] ↓
[7029] DECISION
[7030] ↓
[7031] CLOSED
[7032] Possible decisions:
[7033] Decision upheld
[7034] Decision changed
[7035] Partial adjustment
[7036] Additional documentation required
[7037] The person reviewing the dispute should not automatically be the person who made the original decision where SoD rules prohibit it.
[7039] 7.29 CLAIM REVERSAL
[7040] A paid or approved claim may require reversal due to:
[7041] Duplicate payment
[7042] Incorrect adjudication
[7043] Incorrect member eligibility
[7044] Provider correction
[7045] Financial error
[7046] Fraud investigation
[7047] System processing error
[7048] Reversal must not delete the original claim.
[7049] Instead:
[7050] Original Claim
[7051] ↓
[7052] Original Payment
[7053] ↓
[7054] Reversal Event
[7055] ↓
[7056] Financial Adjustment
[7057] ↓
[7058] Audit Record
[7060] 7.30 CLAIM CANCELLATION
[7061] Cancellation should only be allowed where the claim's state permits cancellation.
[7062] For example:
[7063] DRAFT → CANCEL
[7064] may be allowed.
[7065] But:
[7066] PAID → DELETE
[7067] must never be allowed.
[7068] A paid claim requires a controlled reversal or adjustment process.
[7070] 7.31 PAYMENT READINESS
[7071] Once adjudication and approval are complete:
[7072] APPROVED
[7073] ↓
[7074] PAYMENT_PENDING
[7075] ↓
[7076] PAYMENT_BATCH
[7077] Payment readiness should verify:
[7078] Approved amount
[7079] Provider account
[7080] Provider active status
[7081] Settlement status
[7082] No outstanding hold
[7083] No unresolved investigation
[7084] No duplicate payment
[7085] Required approvals
[7086] Financial integrity
[7088] 7.32 PROVIDER REMITTANCE ADVICE
[7089] After settlement, the system should generate a provider remittance statement showing:
[7090] Claim number
[7091] Service date
[7092] Submitted amount
[7093] Approved amount
[7094] Rejected amount
[7095] Adjustments
[7096] Provider responsibility
[7097] Member responsibility where applicable
[7098] Payment amount
[7099] Payment reference
[7100] Rejection/adjustment reasons
[7102] 7.33 CLAIM BATCH PROCESSING
[7103] Providers may submit thousands of claims simultaneously.
[7104] The processing pipeline should be:
[7105] UPLOAD
[7106] ↓
[7107] SECURITY SCAN
[7108] ↓
[7109] FORMAT VALIDATION
[7110] ↓
[7111] SCHEMA VALIDATION
[7112] ↓
[7113] ROW VALIDATION
[7114] ↓
[7115] DUPLICATE CHECK
[7116] ↓
[7117] ACCEPTANCE
[7118] ↓
[7119] ASYNC PROCESSING
[7120] ↓
[7121] RESULT REPORT
[7122] One invalid claim must not necessarily cause an entire valid batch to fail.
[7123] The system should support partial batch success.
[7125] 7.34 BULK CLAIM PROCESSING
[7126] For large claim batches:
[7127] Use asynchronous processing.
[7128] Assign batch IDs.
[7129] Track individual claim states.
[7130] Support retry of failed records.
[7131] Prevent duplicate processing.
[7132] Provide progress information.
[7133] Provide downloadable result reports.
[7134] Maintain complete batch audit history.
[7135] Example:
[7136] Batch:
[7137] BATCH-20261001-00045
[7139] Total:
[7140] 10,000
[7142] Accepted:
[7143] 9,650
[7145] Rejected:
[7146] 250
[7148] Pending Review:
[7149] 100
[7151] 7.35 CLAIM DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT
[7152] Claims may include:
[7153] Invoices
[7154] Receipts
[7155] Medical reports
[7156] Lab results
[7157] Diagnostic reports
[7158] Authorization documents
[7159] Referral documents
[7160] Discharge summaries
[7161] Other supporting documents
[7162] Documents must have:
[7163] Document ID
[7164] Claim ID
[7165] Type
[7166] Version
[7167] Uploaded by
[7168] Uploaded timestamp
[7169] File hash
[7170] Security scan status
[7171] Access classification
[7172] Malware scanning must occur before documents become available for operational use.
[7174] 7.36 CLAIM NOTES
[7175] Notes should support classifications:
[7176] Internal Note
[7177] Visible only to authorized HMO employees.
[7178] Provider Note
[7179] Visible to authorized provider users.
[7180] Clinical Note
[7181] Restricted to authorized clinical personnel.
[7182] Compliance Note
[7183] Restricted to compliance/investigation personnel.
[7184] A note's visibility must be determined by authorization policy rather than UI assumptions.
[7186] 7.37 CLAIM SEARCH
[7187] Authorized users should be able to search by:
[7188] Claim number
[7189] Member ID
[7190] Provider
[7191] Employer
[7192] Service date
[7193] Claim status
[7194] Claim type
[7195] Amount
[7196] Authorization
[7197] Submission date
[7198] Payment reference
[7199] Risk status
[7200] Dispute status
[7201] Search results must respect the user's data scope.
[7202] A claims officer for Region A must not automatically see Region B claims.
[7204] 7.38 CLAIM ACCESS CONTROL
[7205] Access must combine:
[7206] ROLE
[7207] +
[7208] PERMISSION
[7209] +
[7210] DATA SCOPE
[7211] +
[7212] ATTRIBUTE
[7213] +
[7214] BUSINESS STATE
[7215] +
[7216] RISK
[7217] Example:
[7218] A Claims Officer may have:
[7219] claims.read
[7220] but may still be restricted to:
[7221] Region = Lagos
[7222] Department = Claims
[7223] Claim Types = Outpatient
[7224] A permission alone does not guarantee unrestricted data access.
[7226] 7.39 CLAIM EXPORT CONTROLS
[7227] Exporting claims should be treated as a sensitive operation.
[7228] Controls may include:
[7229] Export permission
[7230] Scope validation
[7231] Maximum record count
[7232] Date-range restriction
[7233] Sensitive-field masking
[7234] Approval requirement
[7235] Reason capture
[7236] Audit logging
[7237] Watermarking
[7238] Download expiry
[7239] Security monitoring
[7240] Bulk export activity should be monitored.
[7242] 7.40 CONCURRENCY CONTROL
[7243] Claims may be processed simultaneously by:
[7244] Automated adjudication
[7245] Claims officers
[7246] Clinical reviewers
[7247] Finance
[7248] Providers
[7249] Background jobs
[7250] The system must prevent two processes from approving or paying the same claim simultaneously.
[7251] Possible mechanisms:
[7252] Optimistic locking
[7253] Database transactions
[7254] Version numbers
[7255] State-transition validation
[7256] Distributed locks where necessary
[7257] Idempotency keys
[7258] Final state validation must occur immediately before critical execution.
[7260] 7.41 STALE DATA PROTECTION
[7261] Example:
[7262] A claims officer opens:
[7263] Claim Version: 4
[7264] Another user changes it to:
[7265] Version: 5
[7266] The first user attempts approval.
[7267] The system should detect the version conflict and prevent the stale update.
[7268] The user should receive:
[7269] “This claim has changed since you opened it. Refresh before continuing.”
[7271] 7.42 CLAIM STATE TRANSITION RULES
[7272] Every transition must be authorized.
[7273] Example:
[7274] SUBMITTED
[7275] ↓
[7276] RECEIVED
[7277] requires system authorization.
[7278] ADJUDICATION
[7279] ↓
[7280] APPROVED
[7281] requires the appropriate approval permission and SoD validation.
[7282] PAID
[7283] ↓
[7284] REVERSED
[7285] requires reversal authority.
[7286] Illegal state transitions must be rejected.
[7288] 7.43 CLAIM AUDIT TRAIL
[7289] Every important action must generate an audit event.
[7290] Minimum fields:
[7291] Audit ID
[7292] Claim ID
[7293] Actor ID
[7294] Actor Type
[7295] Action
[7296] Previous State
[7297] New State
[7298] Timestamp
[7299] Reason
[7300] IP Address
[7301] Device ID
[7302] Session ID
[7303] Correlation ID
[7304] Policy Version
[7305] Rule Version
[7306] Changed Fields
[7307] Approval Reference
[7308] Audit history must be append-only.
[7310] 7.44 CLAIM SECURITY EVENTS
[7311] Security events may include:
[7312] Unauthorized claim access
[7313] Cross-tenant access attempt
[7314] Repeated failed claim updates
[7315] Bulk claim export
[7316] Unauthorized claim approval
[7317] Repeated approval attempts
[7318] Claim manipulation
[7319] Suspicious resubmission
[7320] Privilege escalation
[7321] Unauthorized document access
[7322] Abnormal claims search activity
[7323] High-risk events should feed the security monitoring platform.
[7325] 7.45 CLAIM PERFORMANCE ARCHITECTURE
[7326] The claims engine should support:
[7327] Synchronous
[7328] Submission acknowledgement
[7329] Basic validation
[7330] Claim status lookup
[7331] Eligibility lookup
[7332] Basic claim search
[7333] Asynchronous
[7334] Large batch processing
[7335] Complex adjudication
[7336] Clinical review
[7337] Risk analysis
[7338] Fraud analytics
[7339] Large document processing
[7340] Reporting
[7341] This prevents long-running processes from blocking users.
[7343] 7.46 CLAIM SCALABILITY
[7344] The claims architecture should support horizontal scaling.
[7345] Example:
[7346] API Gateway
[7347] ↓
[7348] Claim Service
[7349] ↓
[7350] Message Queue
[7351] ↓
[7352] Validation Workers
[7353] ↓
[7354] Adjudication Workers
[7355] ↓
[7356] Clinical Review Queue
[7357] ↓
[7358] Risk Review Queue
[7359] ↓
[7360] Payment Service
[7361] Workers should be independently scalable.
[7362] If claim volume increases during monthly provider submission periods, additional processing workers can be added without redesigning the platform.
[7364] 7.47 CLAIM RELIABILITY
[7365] The system should implement:
[7366] Idempotency
[7367] Retry policies
[7368] Dead-letter queues
[7369] Transaction boundaries
[7370] Circuit breakers
[7371] Timeout controls
[7372] Outbox pattern
[7373] Event replay where appropriate
[7374] State recovery
[7375] Job checkpoints
[7376] Failure monitoring
[7377] A failed background worker must not silently lose a claim.
[7379] 7.48 UNKNOWN EDGE-CASE STRATEGY
[7380] Because not every failure can be predicted during design, the claims system must have a general safety model.
[7381] For an unknown condition:
[7382] UNKNOWN CONDITION
[7383] ↓
[7384] STOP UNSAFE ACTION
[7385] ↓
[7386] PRESERVE EXISTING STATE
[7387] ↓
[7388] CREATE CORRELATION ID
[7389] ↓
[7390] LOG TECHNICAL ERROR
[7391] ↓
[7392] CREATE OPERATIONAL/SECURITY EVENT
[7393] ↓
[7394] MOVE TO SAFE EXCEPTION STATE
[7395] ↓
[7396] NOTIFY RESPONSIBLE TEAM
[7397] ↓
[7398] ALLOW CONTROLLED RECOVERY
[7399] The system must never respond to an unknown state by silently approving a claim.
[7401] 7.49 CRITICAL CLAIM EDGE-CASE MATRIX
[7403] 7.50 CLAIM PROCESSING INTEGRITY MODEL
[7404] The central processing rule is:
[7405] AUTHENTICATE
[7406] ↓
[7407] AUTHORIZE
[7408] ↓
[7409] VALIDATE
[7410] ↓
[7411] VERIFY ELIGIBILITY
[7412] ↓
[7413] VERIFY PROVIDER
[7414] ↓
[7415] VERIFY BENEFIT
[7416] ↓
[7417] VERIFY AUTHORIZATION
[7418] ↓
[7419] PRICE
[7420] ↓
[7421] ADJUDICATE
[7422] ↓
[7423] RISK/CLINICAL REVIEW
[7424] ↓
[7425] APPROVE
[7426] ↓
[7427] FINANCIAL VALIDATION
[7428] ↓
[7429] PAY
[7430] ↓
[7431] RECONCILE
[7432] ↓
[7433] AUDIT
[7434] No stage should silently bypass a previous mandatory control.
[7436] 7.51 END-TO-END CLAIM EXAMPLE
[7437] Consider:
[7438] Member:
[7439] M-100245
[7441] Provider:
[7442] PROV-0042
[7444] Service:
[7445] MRI
[7447] Service Date:
[7448] 10 October
[7450] Authorization:
[7451] AUTH-50021
[7452] The provider submits:
[7453] Provider Charge:
[7454] ₦150,000
[7455] The system determines:
[7456] Member:
[7457] Eligible
[7459] Provider:
[7460] Contract Active
[7462] Authorization:
[7463] Valid
[7465] Contract Tariff:
[7466] ₦120,000
[7468] Benefit Available:
[7469] ₦100,000
[7470] Adjudication:
[7471] Provider Charge:       ₦150,000
[7472] Allowed Tariff:        ₦120,000
[7473] Benefit Covered:       ₦100,000
[7474] Member Responsibility: ₦20,000
[7475] HMO Liability:         ₦100,000
[7476] Excluded/Rejected:     ₦30,000
[7477] The claim proceeds to the appropriate approval workflow.
[7478] After approval:
[7479] APPROVED
[7480] ↓
[7481] PAYMENT_PENDING
[7482] ↓
[7483] SETTLEMENT_BATCH
[7484] ↓
[7485] PAYMENT_PROCESSING
[7486] ↓
[7487] PAID
[7488] ↓
[7489] RECONCILED
[7490] Every stage creates the required audit and financial records.
[7492] 7.52 CLAIM MANAGEMENT DASHBOARD
[7493] Management should have visibility into:

[7509] Average processing time

[7513] Provider
[7514] Claims by provider
[7515] Provider claim volume
[7516] Provider rejection rate
[7517] Provider adjustment rate
[7518] Risk
[7519] Claims under investigation
[7520] Duplicate indicators
[7521] High-risk claims
[7522] Unusual claim patterns
[7523] Dashboards must respect the viewer's authorization scope.
[7525] 7.53 CLAIM SLA MANAGEMENT
[7526] Each claim type may have a configurable SLA.
[7527] Example:
[7528] Standard Claim:
[7529] X business days
[7531] Urgent Claim:
[7532] Y business days
[7534] High-risk Claim:
[7535] Special review SLA
[7537] Dispute:
[7538] Z business days
[7539] The system should track:
[7540] SLA start
[7541] SLA pause
[7542] SLA resume
[7543] SLA breach
[7544] Escalation
[7545] Resolution
[7546] SLA calculations must support weekends, holidays, business hours, and organization-specific calendars.
[7548] 7.54 NOTIFICATION EVENTS
[7549] Notifications may be triggered when:
[7550] Claim submitted
[7551] Claim accepted
[7552] Claim returned
[7553] Claim rejected
[7554] Claim approved
[7555] Additional information requested
[7556] Claim disputed
[7557] Claim paid
[7558] Claim reversed
[7559] SLA breached
[7560] Notification channels may include:
[7561] In-app
[7562] Email
[7563] SMS
[7564] Provider portal
[7565] API/webhook
[7566] Notifications must never expose more sensitive information than the recipient is authorized to receive.

[7594] 7.56 CLAIM DATA INTEGRITY
[7595] The claim system must maintain referential relationships between:
[7596] Member
[7597] ↓
[7598] Enrollment
[7599] ↓

[7601] ↓
[7602] Benefit
[7603] ↓

[7607] ↓

[7609] ↓

[7613] ↓

[7615] ↓
[7616] Payment
[7617] ↓
[7618] Reconciliation
[7619] A claim should never reference an invalid or silently deleted parent record.
[7620] Historical entities should be retained according to the organization's retention policy.
[7622] 7.57 CLAIM ARCHIVAL
[7623] Archived claims must remain:
[7624] Searchable by authorized users
[7625] Auditable
[7626] Financially traceable
[7627] Immutable
[7628] Linked to their historical plan/provider/benefit versions
[7629] Archiving must not mean deletion.
[7631] 7.58 CLAIM RETENTION
[7632] Retention periods should be configurable based on:
[7633] Legal requirements
[7634] Regulatory requirements
[7635] Contractual requirements
[7636] Financial requirements
[7637] Audit requirements
[7638] Organizational policy
[7639] The platform should support legal holds.
[7640] A claim under legal or compliance hold must not be automatically destroyed.
[7642] 7.59 CLAIM QUALITY CONTROLS
[7643] The system should measure:
[7644] First-pass acceptance rate
[7645] First-pass approval rate
[7646] Rejection rate
[7647] Return-for-correction rate
[7648] Duplicate rate
[7649] Average adjudication time
[7650] Average payment time
[7651] Dispute rate
[7652] Reversal rate
[7653] Documentation completeness
[7654] Provider submission quality
[7655] These metrics can identify process weaknesses without altering individual claim decisions improperly.

[7659] Deny-by-default access.

[7673] Export controls.
[7674] Session security.
[7675] Cross-tenant isolation.
[7676] Privileged access monitoring.
[7677] Security event monitoring.
[7678] Immutable historical records.
[7680] 7.61 PART VII PERFORMANCE REQUIREMENTS
[7681] Target architecture should aim for:
[7682] Targets should be validated through performance testing rather than treated as guaranteed production figures.
[7684] 7.62 PART VII RELIABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[7685] The subsystem must support:

[7688] Transactional state changes

[7698] Reconciliation

[7702] The architecture must support:

[7708] Batch processing
[7709] Asynchronous reporting

[7712] Independent scaling of document processing
[7713] The architecture should be able to support growth in:

[7718] Documents
[7719] Employers
[7720] Transactions
[7721] Concurrent users

[7725] Clear status indicators
[7726] Explainable decisions

[7732] Keyboard-friendly workflows

[7746] Verify benefits.
[7747] Verify authorizations.

[7750] Adjudicate claims.
[7751] Explain decisions.
[7752] Perform partial approvals.

[7754] Route risk reviews.

[7756] Enforce SoD.
[7757] Support corrections.

[7759] Support disputes.
[7760] Support reversals.

[7763] Maintain complete audit history.
