[7767] Support bulk claims.
[7768] Protect sensitive data.
[7769] Handle unknown states safely.
[7771] 7.66 PART VII SUMMARY
[7772] The Claims Management architecture establishes the financial and clinical control layer of the HMO platform.
[7773] The core principle is:
[7774] A claim must never be approved merely because it was submitted.
[7775] It must pass through controlled evaluation of:
[7776] WHO
[7777] ↓
[7778] WHICH MEMBER
[7779] ↓
[7780] WHICH PLAN
[7781] ↓
[7782] WHICH PLAN VERSION
[7783] ↓
[7784] WHICH PROVIDER
[7785] ↓
[7786] WHICH CONTRACT
[7787] ↓
[7788] WHICH SERVICE
[7789] ↓
[7790] WHICH SERVICE DATE
[7791] ↓
[7792] WHICH AUTHORIZATION
[7793] ↓
[7794] WHICH BENEFIT
[7795] ↓
[7796] WHICH TARIFF
[7797] ↓
[7798] WHICH RULES
[7799] ↓
[7800] WHICH REVIEW
[7801] ↓
[7802] WHICH APPROVAL
[7803] ↓
[7804] WHICH PAYMENT
[7805] ↓
[7806] WHICH RECONCILIATION
[7807] The resulting architecture creates a claims system that is secure, explainable, auditable, scalable, reliable, and operationally controlled.
[7808] It also establishes the foundation for the next major layer of the Master Blueprint: Customer Experience, Member Services & Communication Architecture.
[7809] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[7810] Part VII — Claims Management & Adjudication Architecture
[7811] Document Type: Enterprise HMO Master Blueprint
Part: VII of the Master Blueprint
Primary Domain: Claims, Adjudication, Review, Approval & Payment Readiness
Target Market: Nigerian HMO Operations
Architecture Principles: Performance, Security, Scalability, Reliability, Usability
[7813] 7.0 PURPOSE
[7814] The Claims Management and Adjudication module is responsible for receiving, validating, evaluating, approving, rejecting, correcting, disputing, and preparing healthcare claims for settlement.
[7815] The module must establish a controlled relationship between:
[7816] Member → Coverage → Provider → Service → Authorization → Benefit → Tariff → Claim → Adjudication → Approval → Payment → Reconciliation
[7817] The platform must never treat a claim simply as an invoice.
[7818] A claim is a financial and healthcare record representing services allegedly delivered to an eligible member under a specific coverage arrangement.
[7819] Therefore, every claim decision must be:
[7820] Traceable
[7821] Explainable
[7822] Reproducible
[7823] Authorized
[7824] Auditable
[7825] Version-aware
[7826] Financially consistent
[7827] Protected against duplicate processing
[7829] 7.1 CLAIMS MANAGEMENT OBJECTIVES
[7830] The system shall:
[7831] Receive claims through multiple channels.
[7832] Validate claims automatically.
[7833] Verify member eligibility for the service date.
[7834] Verify provider eligibility.
[7835] Verify plan and benefit coverage.
[7836] Verify authorization requirements.
[7837] Detect duplicate claims.
[7838] Apply provider-specific tariffs.
[7839] Apply member benefit rules.
[7840] Calculate HMO liability.
[7841] Calculate member responsibility where applicable.
[7842] Identify claims requiring clinical review.
[7843] Identify claims requiring fraud/risk review.
[7844] Route claims through appropriate approval workflows.
[7845] Prevent unauthorized claim approval.
[7846] Support partial approvals.
[7847] Support claim corrections and resubmissions.
[7848] Support disputes and reconsiderations.
[7849] Generate payment-ready claims.
[7850] Maintain complete historical records.
[7851] Integrate approved claims with financial operations.
[7852] Prevent double payment.
[7853] Provide provider-facing claim visibility.
[7854] Provide management dashboards and analytics.
[7856] 7.2 CLAIM SOURCES
[7857] Claims may originate from:
[7858] Provider Portal
[7859] Providers manually submit claims through the provider portal.
[7860] Provider API
[7861] Large healthcare providers may submit claims programmatically.
[7862] Bulk Upload
[7863] Providers may submit large batches of claims using approved file formats.
[7864] Internal HMO Entry
[7865] Authorized HMO employees may create claims on behalf of providers under controlled permissions.
[7866] System-Generated Claims
[7867] Certain contracted arrangements may generate claims automatically.
[7868] Migration
[7869] Historical claims may be imported from legacy systems.
[7870] Migrated claims must be clearly identified as migrated records.
[7872] 7.3 CLAIM TYPES
[7873] The system should support configurable claim categories including:
[7874] Outpatient
[7875] Inpatient
[7876] Emergency
[7877] Pharmacy
[7878] Laboratory
[7879] Diagnostic
[7880] Specialist
[7881] Surgery
[7882] Maternity
[7883] Dental
[7884] Optical
[7885] Physiotherapy
[7886] Mental/behavioral healthcare where supported
[7887] Ambulance
[7888] Home healthcare where supported
[7889] Capitation-related claims
[7890] Other contracted healthcare services
[7891] The claim-type catalogue must be configurable rather than hard-coded.
[7893] 7.4 CLAIM STRUCTURE
[7894] A claim consists of multiple logical components.
[7895] Claim Header
[7896] Contains:
[7897] Claim ID
[7898] Claim number
[7899] Organization ID
[7900] Provider ID
[7901] Provider location
[7902] Member ID
[7903] Employer ID where applicable
[7904] Plan ID
[7905] Plan version
[7906] Encounter ID
[7907] Authorization ID
[7908] Referral ID
[7909] Claim type
[7910] Service start date
[7911] Service end date
[7912] Submission date
[7913] Currency
[7914] Total provider amount
[7915] Total approved amount
[7916] Total rejected amount
[7917] Member responsibility
[7918] HMO liability
[7919] Claim status
[7920] Priority
[7921] Risk status
[7922] Clinical review status
[7923] Financial status
[7925] 7.5 CLAIM LINE
[7926] Each claim may contain multiple service lines.
[7927] Example:
[7928] Every claim line should be independently traceable.
[7930] 7.6 CLAIM LIFECYCLE
[7931] The standard lifecycle should be:
[7932] DRAFT
[7933] ↓
[7934] SUBMITTED
[7935] ↓
[7936] RECEIVED
[7937] ↓
[7938] VALIDATING
[7939] ↓
[7940] VALIDATION_FAILED
[7941] ↓
[7942] ACCEPTED
[7943] ↓
[7944] ELIGIBILITY_CHECK
[7945] ↓
[7946] DUPLICATE_CHECK
[7947] ↓
[7948] BENEFIT_CHECK
[7949] ↓
[7950] AUTHORIZATION_CHECK
[7951] ↓
[7952] TARIFF_PRICING
[7953] ↓
[7954] CLINICAL_REVIEW
[7955] ↓
[7956] RISK_REVIEW
[7957] ↓
[7958] ADJUDICATION
[7959] ↓
[7960] APPROVAL_REVIEW
[7961] ↓
[7962] APPROVED / PARTIALLY_APPROVED / REJECTED
[7963] ↓
[7964] PAYMENT_PENDING
[7965] ↓
[7966] PAYMENT_PROCESSING
[7967] ↓
[7968] PAID
[7969] ↓
[7970] RECONCILED
[7971] Exception states may include:
[7972] RETURNED_FOR_CORRECTION
[7973] DISPUTED
[7974] RESUBMITTED
[7975] CANCELLED
[7976] REVERSED
[7977] ON_HOLD
[7978] UNDER_INVESTIGATION
[7979] Not every claim must pass through every stage.
[7980] The workflow should be dynamically determined by claim characteristics.
[7982] 7.7 CLAIM SUBMISSION VALIDATION
[7983] Upon submission, the system performs multiple validation layers.
[7984] Layer 1 — Structural Validation
[7985] Verify:
[7986] Required fields
[7987] Data types
[7988] Valid formats
[7989] Valid dates
[7990] Valid amounts
[7991] Valid identifiers
[7992] Layer 2 — Provider Validation
[7993] Verify:
[7994] Provider exists
[7995] Provider is active
[7996] Provider location exists
[7997] Provider is contracted
[7998] Provider contract is valid for service date
[7999] Layer 3 — Member Validation
[8000] Verify:
[8001] Member exists
[8002] Member identity is valid
[8003] Member was covered on service date
[8004] Member belongs to the appropriate plan
[8005] Layer 4 — Service Validation
[8006] Verify:
[8007] Service exists
[8008] Service code is valid
[8009] Service date is valid
[8010] Quantity is reasonable according to configured rules
[8011] Service is compatible with claim type
[8012] Layer 5 — Authorization Validation
[8013] Verify:
[8014] Authorization exists where required
[8015] Authorization belongs to the member
[8016] Authorization belongs to the provider
[8017] Authorization covers the service
[8018] Authorization covers the relevant dates
[8019] Approved quantity has not been exceeded
[8020] Layer 6 — Financial Validation
[8021] Verify:
[8022] Amount is valid
[8023] Currency is valid
[8024] Tariff exists
[8025] Provider pricing applies
[8026] No invalid negative amounts
[8027] No impossible calculations
[8029] 7.8 CLAIM DUPLICATE DETECTION
[8030] Duplicate claims represent a major operational and financial risk.
[8031] The system should compare:
[8032] Member
[8033] Provider
[8034] Service date
[8035] Service code
[8036] Quantity
[8037] Amount
[8038] Authorization
[8039] Encounter
[8040] Provider reference
[8041] Claim reference
[8042] Submission timestamp
[8043] Duplicate detection should support multiple confidence levels.
[8044] Possible Results
[8045] NO_DUPLICATE
[8046] POSSIBLE_DUPLICATE
[8047] LIKELY_DUPLICATE
[8048] CONFIRMED_DUPLICATE
[8049] A possible duplicate should normally enter a review queue rather than automatically being declared fraud.
[8051] 7.9 IDEMPOTENT CLAIM SUBMISSION
[8052] Every API submission should support an idempotency key.
[8053] Example:
[8054] Idempotency-Key:
[8055] CLAIM_PROVIDER_20261001_000123
[8056] If the provider accidentally submits the same request twice, the system must not create two independent claims.
[8057] The response should return the existing claim where appropriate.
[8059] 7.10 HISTORICAL ELIGIBILITY
[8060] Eligibility must be evaluated against the service date, not simply the current date.
[8061] For example:
[8062] Service Date: 10 March
[8063] Current Date: 20 April
[8064] Member terminated: 31 March
[8065] The member may still have been eligible on 10 March.
[8066] Therefore:
[8067] Current membership status ≠ historical eligibility.
[8068] The system must evaluate:
[8069] Coverage period
[8070] Plan version
[8071] Benefit version
[8072] Provider contract version
[8073] Network status
[8074] Authorization validity
[8075] Applicable tariff
[8076] Service date
[8078] 7.11 PLAN VERSION CONTROL
[8079] Claims must reference the plan version applicable at the time of service.
[8080] Example:
[8081] Plan A
[8082] Version 1
[8083] Effective: Jan 1 – Jun 30
[8085] Plan A
[8086] Version 2
[8087] Effective: Jul 1 – Dec 31
[8088] A service provided on June 29 must be adjudicated using Version 1 even if the claim is submitted in July.
[8089] Historical business rules must therefore remain immutable.
[8091] 7.12 BENEFIT CHECK
[8092] The adjudication engine must determine whether a service is:
[8093] Covered
[8094] Partially covered
[8095] Excluded
[8096] Subject to authorization
[8097] Subject to referral
[8098] Subject to a monetary limit
[8099] Subject to quantity/frequency limits
[8100] Subject to co-payment
[8101] Subject to deductible
[8102] Subject to waiting period
[8103] Subject to network restrictions
[8105] 7.13 BENEFIT CONSUMPTION
[8106] The system should maintain benefit utilization.
[8107] Example:
[8108] Annual Benefit Limit:
[8109] ₦500,000
[8111] Previously Used:
[8112] ₦320,000
[8114] Current Eligible Amount:
[8115] ₦100,000
[8117] Remaining:
[8118] ₦80,000
[8119] The system must prevent concurrent claims from consuming the same remaining benefit incorrectly.
[8120] This requires controlled reservation and transaction handling.
[8122] 7.14 BENEFIT RESERVATION
[8123] For claims or services requiring reservation:
[8124] AVAILABLE
[8125] ↓
[8126] RESERVED
[8127] ↓
[8128] CONSUMED
[8129] If the transaction fails:
[8130] RESERVED
[8131] ↓
[8132] RELEASED
[8133] If a claim is reversed:
[8134] CONSUMED
[8135] ↓
[8136] ADJUSTED / RESTORED
[8137] The exact restoration policy must be configurable.
[8139] 7.15 AUTHORIZATION MATCHING
[8140] Where a claim requires prior authorization, the system must compare:
[8141] Member
[8142] Provider
[8143] Service
[8144] Authorization number
[8145] Service date
[8146] Quantity
[8147] Approved amount
[8148] Diagnosis/category where applicable
[8149] Authorization status
[8150] Example:
[8151] Authorization:
[8152] MRI
[8153] Quantity: 1
[8154] Valid: 1–15 October
[8156] Claim:
[8157] MRI
[8158] Quantity: 1
[8159] Service date: 10 October
[8160] The claim can proceed to adjudication.
[8161] If:
[8162] Claim quantity: 2
[8163] the second unit should be flagged according to configured business rules.
[8165] 7.16 TARIFF AND PRICING ENGINE
[8166] The pricing engine determines the applicable financial terms.
[8167] Possible inputs:
[8168] Provider
[8169] Provider location
[8170] Service
[8171] Service date
[8172] Provider contract
[8173] Contract version
[8174] Plan
[8175] Negotiated tariff
[8176] Quantity
[8177] Discount
[8178] Co-payment
[8179] Deductible
[8180] Benefit limit
[8181] Special pricing agreement
[8182] Pricing must be effective-dated.
[8183] Example:
[8184] Provider Contract Version 3
[8185] Effective:
[8186] 01/07/2026
[8188] Service:
[8189] MRI
[8191] Agreed Tariff:
[8192] ₦120,000
[8193] A claim for the service on 20 June must not automatically use the July tariff.
[8195] 7.17 ADJUDICATION ENGINE
[8196] The adjudication engine applies deterministic business rules.
[8197] Example sequence:
[8198] 1. Validate claim
[8199] 2. Confirm member eligibility
[8200] 3. Confirm provider eligibility
[8201] 4. Confirm service
[8202] 5. Confirm authorization
[8203] 6. Determine benefit
[8204] 7. Determine tariff
[8205] 8. Determine limits
[8206] 9. Determine member responsibility
[8207] 10. Determine HMO liability
[8208] 11. Apply adjustments
[8209] 12. Generate decision
[8210] 13. Record rule versions
[8211] The order of rules must be explicitly defined.
[8213] 7.18 EXPLAINABLE ADJUDICATION
[8214] Every automated decision should be explainable.
[8215] Instead of:
[8216] Rejected
[8217] The system should produce:
[8218] Decision:
[8219] REJECTED
[8221] Reason:
[8222] Service excluded under plan benefit configuration.
[8224] Rule:
[8225] BENEFIT.EXCLUSION.001
[8227] Rule Version:
[8228] 2026.4
[8230] Applied:
[8231] 2026-10-01 14:31:05
[8233] Source:
[8234] Plan Version 7
[8235] This allows staff to understand why the decision occurred.
[8237] 7.19 CLAIM LINE ADJUDICATION
[8238] Each line should produce a financial breakdown.
[8239] Example:
[8240] Provider Charge:       ₦150,000
[8241] Contract Tariff:       ₦120,000
[8242] Allowed Amount:        ₦120,000
[8243] Benefit Covered:       ₦100,000
[8244] Member Responsibility: ₦20,000
[8245] HMO Liability:         ₦100,000
[8246] Rejected Amount:       ₦30,000
[8247] This information must flow into payment and reporting systems.
[8249] 7.20 PARTIAL APPROVAL
[8250] Claims may be partially approved.
[8251] Example:
[8252] Provider submits:
[8253] Total: ₦500,000
[8254] Adjudication determines:
[8255] Approved: ₦350,000
[8256] Rejected: ₦100,000
[8257] Member Responsibility: ₦50,000
[8258] The system must provide reason codes for every rejected or adjusted portion.
[8260] 7.21 RETURN FOR CORRECTION VS REJECTION
[8261] These are different outcomes.
[8262] Return for Correction
[8263] The claim may become valid after missing information is supplied.
[8264] Example:
[8265] Missing invoice
[8266] Incorrect service date
[8267] Missing authorization reference
[8268] Invalid attachment
[8269] Rejection
[8270] The claim fails a substantive rule.
[8271] Example:
[8272] Service excluded
[8273] Provider not contracted
[8274] Member not covered
[8275] Benefit exhausted
[8276] The system must not use rejection simply because information is missing.
[8278] 7.22 CLINICAL REVIEW
[8279] Claims requiring clinical assessment should enter a clinical review queue.
[8280] Reviewers may assess:
[8281] Medical necessity
[8282] Clinical documentation
[8283] Diagnosis
[8284] Procedure relationship
[8285] Authorization
[8286] Treatment pattern
[8287] Supporting medical records
[8288] Clinical decisions must be performed by appropriately authorized personnel.
[8289] The system should support:
[8290] APPROVE
[8291] PARTIALLY_APPROVE
[8292] REJECT
[8293] REQUEST_INFORMATION
[8294] ESCALATE
[8295] All clinical decisions must be recorded.
[8297] 7.23 FRAUD AND RISK REVIEW
[8298] The system may identify risk indicators such as:
[8299] Duplicate services
[8300] Unusual service frequency
[8301] Unusual claim amounts
[8302] Sudden provider billing increases
[8303] Repeated identical claims
[8304] Claims immediately before termination
[8305] Unusual after-hours patterns
[8306] Abnormal provider/member relationships
[8307] Repeated rejected claims followed by altered submissions
[8308] Suspicious billing combinations
[8309] These indicators should produce:
[8310] RISK_FLAG
[8311] A risk flag should not automatically mean that fraud has occurred.
[8312] The claim may be routed to an authorized investigation team.
[8314] 7.24 CLAIM APPROVAL WORKFLOW
[8315] Approval should be dynamically determined.
[8316] Inputs may include:
[8317] Claim amount
[8318] Claim type
[8319] Provider
[8320] Risk score
[8321] Clinical complexity
[8322] Department
[8323] User role
[8324] User scope
[8325] Exception status
[8326] Previous decisions
[8327] Example:
[8328] Low-value standard claim
[8329] ↓
[8330] Supervisor Review
[8332] High-value claim
[8333] ↓
[8334] Manager Review
[8336] High-risk claim
[8337] ↓
[8338] Special Review
[8340] Exceptional claim
[8341] ↓
[8342] Executive/Designated Approval
[8343] Thresholds must be configurable.
[8345] 7.25 SEGREGATION OF DUTIES
[8346] The system must prevent conflicts such as:
[8347] Claim Creator
[8348] ≠
[8349] Claim Approver
[8350] And:
[8351] Claim Approver
[8352] ≠
[8353] Payment Executor
[8354] Additional rules may include:
[8355] Provider Bank Account Modifier
[8356] ≠
[8357] Settlement Approver
[8358] The system should evaluate SoD both when assigning permissions and at transaction time.
[8360] 7.26 CLAIM CORRECTION
[8361] A claim may be returned for correction.
[8362] The corrected submission must not overwrite the original history.
[8363] Instead:
[8364] Claim Version 1
[8365] ↓
[8366] Correction Requested
[8367] ↓
[8368] Claim Version 2
[8369] The system must preserve:
[8370] Original data
[8371] Corrected data
[8372] Who changed it
[8373] Why it changed
[8374] When it changed
[8375] Supporting documents
[8376] Previous decisions
[8378] 7.27 CLAIM RESUBMISSION
[8379] A rejected or returned claim may be resubmitted if policy permits.
[8380] The system must link:
[8381] Original Claim
[8382] ↓
[8383] Correction
[8384] ↓
[8385] Resubmitted Claim
[8386] This prevents repeated submission from appearing as an unrelated claim.
[8388] 7.28 CLAIM DISPUTES AND RECONSIDERATION
[8389] Providers should be able to dispute eligible decisions.
[8390] Lifecycle:
[8391] DISPUTE_SUBMITTED
[8392] ↓
[8393] ACKNOWLEDGED
[8394] ↓
[8395] ASSIGNED
[8396] ↓
[8397] UNDER_REVIEW
[8398] ↓
[8399] DECISION
[8400] ↓
[8401] CLOSED
[8402] Possible decisions:
[8403] Decision upheld
[8404] Decision changed
[8405] Partial adjustment
[8406] Additional documentation required
[8407] The person reviewing the dispute should not automatically be the person who made the original decision where SoD rules prohibit it.
[8409] 7.29 CLAIM REVERSAL
[8410] A paid or approved claim may require reversal due to:
[8411] Duplicate payment
[8412] Incorrect adjudication
[8413] Incorrect member eligibility
[8414] Provider correction
[8415] Financial error
[8416] Fraud investigation
[8417] System processing error
[8418] Reversal must not delete the original claim.
[8419] Instead:
[8420] Original Claim
[8421] ↓
[8422] Original Payment
[8423] ↓
[8424] Reversal Event
[8425] ↓
[8426] Financial Adjustment
[8427] ↓
[8428] Audit Record
[8430] 7.30 CLAIM CANCELLATION
[8431] Cancellation should only be allowed where the claim's state permits cancellation.
[8432] For example:
[8433] DRAFT → CANCEL
[8434] may be allowed.
[8435] But:
[8436] PAID → DELETE
[8437] must never be allowed.
[8438] A paid claim requires a controlled reversal or adjustment process.
[8440] 7.31 PAYMENT READINESS
[8441] Once adjudication and approval are complete:
[8442] APPROVED
[8443] ↓
[8444] PAYMENT_PENDING
[8445] ↓
[8446] PAYMENT_BATCH
[8447] Payment readiness should verify:
[8448] Approved amount
[8449] Provider account
[8450] Provider active status
[8451] Settlement status
[8452] No outstanding hold
[8453] No unresolved investigation
[8454] No duplicate payment
[8455] Required approvals
[8456] Financial integrity
[8458] 7.32 PROVIDER REMITTANCE ADVICE
[8459] After settlement, the system should generate a provider remittance statement showing:
[8460] Claim number
[8461] Service date
[8462] Submitted amount
[8463] Approved amount
[8464] Rejected amount
[8465] Adjustments
[8466] Provider responsibility
[8467] Member responsibility where applicable
[8468] Payment amount
[8469] Payment reference
[8470] Rejection/adjustment reasons
[8472] 7.33 CLAIM BATCH PROCESSING
[8473] Providers may submit thousands of claims simultaneously.
[8474] The processing pipeline should be:
[8475] UPLOAD
[8476] ↓
[8477] SECURITY SCAN
[8478] ↓
[8479] FORMAT VALIDATION
[8480] ↓
[8481] SCHEMA VALIDATION
[8482] ↓
[8483] ROW VALIDATION
[8484] ↓
[8485] DUPLICATE CHECK
[8486] ↓
[8487] ACCEPTANCE
[8488] ↓
[8489] ASYNC PROCESSING
[8490] ↓
[8491] RESULT REPORT
[8492] One invalid claim must not necessarily cause an entire valid batch to fail.
[8493] The system should support partial batch success.
[8495] 7.34 BULK CLAIM PROCESSING
[8496] For large claim batches:
[8497] Use asynchronous processing.
[8498] Assign batch IDs.
[8499] Track individual claim states.
[8500] Support retry of failed records.
[8501] Prevent duplicate processing.
[8502] Provide progress information.
[8503] Provide downloadable result reports.
[8504] Maintain complete batch audit history.
[8505] Example:
[8506] Batch:
[8507] BATCH-20261001-00045
[8509] Total:
[8510] 10,000
[8512] Accepted:
[8513] 9,650
[8515] Rejected:
[8516] 250
[8518] Pending Review:
[8519] 100
[8521] 7.35 CLAIM DOCUMENT MANAGEMENT
[8522] Claims may include:
[8523] Invoices
[8524] Receipts
[8525] Medical reports
[8526] Lab results
[8527] Diagnostic reports
[8528] Authorization documents
[8529] Referral documents
[8530] Discharge summaries
[8531] Other supporting documents
[8532] Documents must have:
[8533] Document ID
[8534] Claim ID
[8535] Type
[8536] Version
[8537] Uploaded by
[8538] Uploaded timestamp
[8539] File hash
[8540] Security scan status
[8541] Access classification
[8542] Malware scanning must occur before documents become available for operational use.
[8544] 7.36 CLAIM NOTES
[8545] Notes should support classifications:
[8546] Internal Note
[8547] Visible only to authorized HMO employees.
[8548] Provider Note
[8549] Visible to authorized provider users.
[8550] Clinical Note
[8551] Restricted to authorized clinical personnel.
[8552] Compliance Note
[8553] Restricted to compliance/investigation personnel.
[8554] A note's visibility must be determined by authorization policy rather than UI assumptions.
[8556] 7.37 CLAIM SEARCH
[8557] Authorized users should be able to search by:
[8558] Claim number
[8559] Member ID
[8560] Provider
[8561] Employer
[8562] Service date
[8563] Claim status
[8564] Claim type
[8565] Amount
[8566] Authorization
[8567] Submission date
[8568] Payment reference
[8569] Risk status
[8570] Dispute status
[8571] Search results must respect the user's data scope.
[8572] A claims officer for Region A must not automatically see Region B claims.
[8574] 7.38 CLAIM ACCESS CONTROL
[8575] Access must combine:
[8576] ROLE
[8577] +
[8578] PERMISSION
[8579] +
[8580] DATA SCOPE
[8581] +
[8582] ATTRIBUTE
[8583] +
[8584] BUSINESS STATE
[8585] +
[8586] RISK
[8587] Example:
[8588] A Claims Officer may have:
[8589] claims.read
[8590] but may still be restricted to:
[8591] Region = Lagos
[8592] Department = Claims
[8593] Claim Types = Outpatient
[8594] A permission alone does not guarantee unrestricted data access.
[8596] 7.39 CLAIM EXPORT CONTROLS
[8597] Exporting claims should be treated as a sensitive operation.
[8598] Controls may include:
[8599] Export permission
[8600] Scope validation
[8601] Maximum record count
[8602] Date-range restriction
[8603] Sensitive-field masking
[8604] Approval requirement
[8605] Reason capture
[8606] Audit logging
[8607] Watermarking
[8608] Download expiry
[8609] Security monitoring
[8610] Bulk export activity should be monitored.
[8612] 7.40 CONCURRENCY CONTROL
[8613] Claims may be processed simultaneously by:
[8614] Automated adjudication
[8615] Claims officers
[8616] Clinical reviewers
[8617] Finance
[8618] Providers
[8619] Background jobs
[8620] The system must prevent two processes from approving or paying the same claim simultaneously.
[8621] Possible mechanisms:
[8622] Optimistic locking
[8623] Database transactions
[8624] Version numbers
[8625] State-transition validation
[8626] Distributed locks where necessary
[8627] Idempotency keys
[8628] Final state validation must occur immediately before critical execution.
[8630] 7.41 STALE DATA PROTECTION
[8631] Example:
[8632] A claims officer opens:
[8633] Claim Version: 4
[8634] Another user changes it to:
[8635] Version: 5
[8636] The first user attempts approval.
[8637] The system should detect the version conflict and prevent the stale update.
[8638] The user should receive:
[8639] “This claim has changed since you opened it. Refresh before continuing.”
[8641] 7.42 CLAIM STATE TRANSITION RULES
[8642] Every transition must be authorized.
[8643] Example:
[8644] SUBMITTED
[8645] ↓
[8646] RECEIVED
[8647] requires system authorization.
[8648] ADJUDICATION
[8649] ↓
[8650] APPROVED
[8651] requires the appropriate approval permission and SoD validation.
[8652] PAID
[8653] ↓
[8654] REVERSED
[8655] requires reversal authority.
[8656] Illegal state transitions must be rejected.
[8658] 7.43 CLAIM AUDIT TRAIL
[8659] Every important action must generate an audit event.
[8660] Minimum fields:
[8661] Audit ID
[8662] Claim ID
[8663] Actor ID
[8664] Actor Type
[8665] Action
[8666] Previous State
[8667] New State
[8668] Timestamp
[8669] Reason
[8670] IP Address
[8671] Device ID
[8672] Session ID
[8673] Correlation ID
[8674] Policy Version
[8675] Rule Version
[8676] Changed Fields
[8677] Approval Reference
[8678] Audit history must be append-only.
[8680] 7.44 CLAIM SECURITY EVENTS
[8681] Security events may include:
[8682] Unauthorized claim access
[8683] Cross-tenant access attempt
[8684] Repeated failed claim updates
[8685] Bulk claim export
[8686] Unauthorized claim approval
[8687] Repeated approval attempts
[8688] Claim manipulation
[8689] Suspicious resubmission
[8690] Privilege escalation
[8691] Unauthorized document access
[8692] Abnormal claims search activity
[8693] High-risk events should feed the security monitoring platform.
[8695] 7.45 CLAIM PERFORMANCE ARCHITECTURE
[8696] The claims engine should support:
[8697] Synchronous
[8698] Submission acknowledgement
[8699] Basic validation
[8700] Claim status lookup
[8701] Eligibility lookup
[8702] Basic claim search
[8703] Asynchronous
[8704] Large batch processing
[8705] Complex adjudication
[8706] Clinical review
[8707] Risk analysis
[8708] Fraud analytics
[8709] Large document processing
[8710] Reporting
[8711] This prevents long-running processes from blocking users.
[8713] 7.46 CLAIM SCALABILITY
[8714] The claims architecture should support horizontal scaling.
[8715] Example:
[8716] API Gateway
[8717] ↓
[8718] Claim Service
[8719] ↓
[8720] Message Queue
[8721] ↓
[8722] Validation Workers
[8723] ↓
[8724] Adjudication Workers
[8725] ↓
[8726] Clinical Review Queue
[8727] ↓
[8728] Risk Review Queue
[8729] ↓
[8730] Payment Service
[8731] Workers should be independently scalable.
[8732] If claim volume increases during monthly provider submission periods, additional processing workers can be added without redesigning the platform.
[8734] 7.47 CLAIM RELIABILITY
[8735] The system should implement:
[8736] Idempotency
[8737] Retry policies
[8738] Dead-letter queues
[8739] Transaction boundaries
[8740] Circuit breakers
[8741] Timeout controls
[8742] Outbox pattern
[8743] Event replay where appropriate
[8744] State recovery
[8745] Job checkpoints
[8746] Failure monitoring
[8747] A failed background worker must not silently lose a claim.
[8749] 7.48 UNKNOWN EDGE-CASE STRATEGY
[8750] Because not every failure can be predicted during design, the claims system must have a general safety model.
[8751] For an unknown condition:
[8752] UNKNOWN CONDITION
[8753] ↓
[8754] STOP UNSAFE ACTION
[8755] ↓
[8756] PRESERVE EXISTING STATE
[8757] ↓
[8758] CREATE CORRELATION ID
[8759] ↓
[8760] LOG TECHNICAL ERROR
[8761] ↓
[8762] CREATE OPERATIONAL/SECURITY EVENT
[8763] ↓
[8764] MOVE TO SAFE EXCEPTION STATE
[8765] ↓
[8766] NOTIFY RESPONSIBLE TEAM
[8767] ↓
[8768] ALLOW CONTROLLED RECOVERY
[8769] The system must never respond to an unknown state by silently approving a claim.
[8771] 7.49 CRITICAL CLAIM EDGE-CASE MATRIX
[8773] 7.50 CLAIM PROCESSING INTEGRITY MODEL
[8774] The central processing rule is:
[8775] AUTHENTICATE
[8776] ↓
[8777] AUTHORIZE
[8778] ↓
[8779] VALIDATE
[8780] ↓
[8781] VERIFY ELIGIBILITY
[8782] ↓
[8783] VERIFY PROVIDER
[8784] ↓
[8785] VERIFY BENEFIT
[8786] ↓
[8787] VERIFY AUTHORIZATION
[8788] ↓
[8789] PRICE
[8790] ↓
[8791] ADJUDICATE
[8792] ↓
[8793] RISK/CLINICAL REVIEW
[8794] ↓
[8795] APPROVE
[8796] ↓
[8797] FINANCIAL VALIDATION
[8798] ↓
[8799] PAY
[8800] ↓
[8801] RECONCILE
[8802] ↓
[8803] AUDIT
[8804] No stage should silently bypass a previous mandatory control.
[8806] 7.51 END-TO-END CLAIM EXAMPLE
[8807] Consider:
[8808] Member:
[8809] M-100245
[8811] Provider:
[8812] PROV-0042
[8814] Service:
[8815] MRI
[8817] Service Date:
[8818] 10 October
[8820] Authorization:
[8821] AUTH-50021
[8822] The provider submits:
[8823] Provider Charge:
[8824] ₦150,000
[8825] The system determines:
[8826] Member:
[8827] Eligible
[8829] Provider:
[8830] Contract Active
[8832] Authorization:
[8833] Valid
[8835] Contract Tariff:
[8836] ₦120,000
[8838] Benefit Available:
[8839] ₦100,000
[8840] Adjudication:
[8841] Provider Charge:       ₦150,000
[8842] Allowed Tariff:        ₦120,000
[8843] Benefit Covered:       ₦100,000
[8844] Member Responsibility: ₦20,000
[8845] HMO Liability:         ₦100,000
[8846] Excluded/Rejected:     ₦30,000
[8847] The claim proceeds to the appropriate approval workflow.
[8848] After approval:
[8849] APPROVED
[8850] ↓
[8851] PAYMENT_PENDING
[8852] ↓
[8853] SETTLEMENT_BATCH
[8854] ↓
[8855] PAYMENT_PROCESSING
[8856] ↓
[8857] PAID
[8858] ↓
[8859] RECONCILED
[8860] Every stage creates the required audit and financial records.
[8862] 7.52 CLAIM MANAGEMENT DASHBOARD
[8863] Management should have visibility into:
[8864] Volume
[8865] Claims received
[8866] Claims processed
[8867] Claims pending
[8868] Claims rejected
[8869] Claims approved
[8870] Claims disputed
[8871] Financial
[8872] Submitted value
[8873] Approved value
[8874] Rejected value
[8875] Paid value
[8876] Outstanding liability
[8877] Average claim value
[8878] Operational
[8879] Average processing time
[8880] Claims by queue
[8881] Claims breaching SLA
[8882] Claims awaiting documentation
[8883] Provider
[8884] Claims by provider
[8885] Provider claim volume
[8886] Provider rejection rate
[8887] Provider adjustment rate
[8888] Risk
[8889] Claims under investigation
[8890] Duplicate indicators
[8891] High-risk claims
[8892] Unusual claim patterns
[8893] Dashboards must respect the viewer's authorization scope.
[8895] 7.53 CLAIM SLA MANAGEMENT
[8896] Each claim type may have a configurable SLA.
[8897] Example:
[8898] Standard Claim:
[8899] X business days
[8901] Urgent Claim:
[8902] Y business days
[8904] High-risk Claim:
[8905] Special review SLA
[8907] Dispute:
[8908] Z business days
[8909] The system should track:
[8910] SLA start
[8911] SLA pause
[8912] SLA resume
[8913] SLA breach
[8914] Escalation
[8915] Resolution
[8916] SLA calculations must support weekends, holidays, business hours, and organization-specific calendars.
[8918] 7.54 NOTIFICATION EVENTS
[8919] Notifications may be triggered when:
[8920] Claim submitted
[8921] Claim accepted
[8922] Claim returned
[8923] Claim rejected
[8924] Claim approved
[8925] Additional information requested
[8926] Claim disputed
[8927] Claim paid
[8928] Claim reversed
[8929] SLA breached
[8930] Notification channels may include:
[8931] In-app
[8932] Email
[8933] SMS
[8934] Provider portal
[8935] API/webhook
[8936] Notifications must never expose more sensitive information than the recipient is authorized to receive.
[8938] 7.55 API ARCHITECTURE
[8939] Representative APIs:
[8940] POST   /claims
[8941] GET    /claims/{claimId}
[8942] PATCH  /claims/{claimId}
[8943] POST   /claims/{claimId}/submit
[8944] POST   /claims/{claimId}/adjudicate
[8945] POST   /claims/{claimId}/approve
[8946] POST   /claims/{claimId}/reject
[8947] POST   /claims/{claimId}/return
[8948] POST   /claims/{claimId}/dispute
[8949] POST   /claims/{claimId}/resubmit
[8950] POST   /claims/{claimId}/reverse
[8951] GET    /claims/{claimId}/history
[8952] GET    /claims/{claimId}/documents
[8953] POST   /claim-batches
[8954] GET    /claim-batches/{batchId}
[8955] Every API must enforce:
[8956] Authentication
[8957] Authorization
[8958] Scope validation
[8959] Input validation
[8960] Rate limiting
[8961] Idempotency where applicable
[8962] Audit logging
[8964] 7.56 CLAIM DATA INTEGRITY
[8965] The claim system must maintain referential relationships between:
[8966] Member
[8967] ↓
[8968] Enrollment
[8969] ↓
[8970] Plan Version
[8971] ↓
[8972] Benefit
[8973] ↓
[8974] Provider
[8975] ↓
[8976] Contract Version
[8977] ↓
[8978] Authorization
[8979] ↓
[8980] Claim
[8981] ↓
[8982] Claim Lines
[8983] ↓
[8984] Adjudication
[8985] ↓
[8986] Payment
[8987] ↓
[8988] Reconciliation
[8989] A claim should never reference an invalid or silently deleted parent record.
[8990] Historical entities should be retained according to the organization's retention policy.
[8992] 7.57 CLAIM ARCHIVAL
[8993] Archived claims must remain:
[8994] Searchable by authorized users
[8995] Auditable
[8996] Financially traceable
[8997] Immutable
[8998] Linked to their historical plan/provider/benefit versions
[8999] Archiving must not mean deletion.
[9001] 7.58 CLAIM RETENTION
[9002] Retention periods should be configurable based on:
[9003] Legal requirements
[9004] Regulatory requirements
[9005] Contractual requirements
[9006] Financial requirements
[9007] Audit requirements
[9008] Organizational policy
[9009] The platform should support legal holds.
[9010] A claim under legal or compliance hold must not be automatically destroyed.
[9012] 7.59 CLAIM QUALITY CONTROLS
[9013] The system should measure:
[9014] First-pass acceptance rate
[9015] First-pass approval rate
[9016] Rejection rate
[9017] Return-for-correction rate
[9018] Duplicate rate
[9019] Average adjudication time
[9020] Average payment time
[9021] Dispute rate
[9022] Reversal rate
[9023] Documentation completeness
[9024] Provider submission quality
[9025] These metrics can identify process weaknesses without altering individual claim decisions improperly.
[9027] 7.60 PART VII SECURITY REQUIREMENTS
[9028] The claims subsystem must enforce:
[9029] Deny-by-default access.
[9030] Least privilege.
[9031] RBAC.
[9032] ABAC.
[9033] Scope-based authorization.
[9034] Field-level protection.
[9035] MFA for privileged actions.
[9036] SoD.
[9037] Audit logging.
[9038] Encryption.
[9039] Secure document handling.
[9040] API authentication.
[9041] Rate limiting.
[9042] Idempotency.
[9043] Export controls.
[9044] Session security.
[9045] Cross-tenant isolation.
[9046] Privileged access monitoring.
[9047] Security event monitoring.
[9048] Immutable historical records.
[9050] 7.61 PART VII PERFORMANCE REQUIREMENTS
[9051] Target architecture should aim for:
[9052] Targets should be validated through performance testing rather than treated as guaranteed production figures.
[9054] 7.62 PART VII RELIABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[9055] The subsystem must support:
[9056] Retry-safe operations
[9057] Idempotent processing
[9058] Transactional state changes
[9059] Queue recovery
[9060] Dead-letter queues
[9061] Failure isolation
[9062] Service health checks
[9063] Database backups
[9064] Disaster recovery
[9065] Monitoring
[9066] Alerting
[9067] Operational runbooks
[9068] Reconciliation
[9069] No claim should disappear because a worker, API, network connection, or external service fails.
[9071] 7.63 PART VII SCALABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[9072] The architecture must support:
[9073] Horizontal claim-processing workers
[9074] Queue-based workloads
[9075] Read replicas where appropriate
[9076] Partitioning strategies where justified
[9077] Efficient indexing
[9078] Batch processing
[9079] Asynchronous reporting
[9080] Distributed caching where safe
[9081] Independent scaling of claim services
[9082] Independent scaling of document processing
[9083] The architecture should be able to support growth in:
[9084] Members
[9085] Providers
[9086] Claims
[9087] Claim lines
[9088] Documents
[9089] Employers
[9090] Transactions
[9091] Concurrent users
[9093] 7.64 PART VII USABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[9094] The claims interface should provide:
[9095] Clear status indicators
[9096] Explainable decisions
[9097] Guided workflows
[9098] Validation feedback
[9099] Search and filtering
[9100] Queue management
[9101] Bulk operations
[9102] Keyboard-friendly workflows
[9103] Responsive interfaces
[9104] Permission-aware navigation
[9105] Context-sensitive actions
[9106] Confirmation for high-risk actions
[9107] Clear error messages
[9108] Users should not need to understand the underlying architecture to operate the system safely.
[9110] 7.65 PART VII COMPLETION CRITERIA
[9111] Part VII is considered complete when the platform can:
[9112] Receive claims.
[9113] Validate claims.
[9114] Verify historical eligibility.
[9115] Verify providers.
[9116] Verify benefits.
[9117] Verify authorizations.
[9118] Detect duplicates.
[9119] Apply historical tariffs.
[9120] Adjudicate claims.
[9121] Explain decisions.
[9122] Perform partial approvals.
[9123] Route clinical reviews.
[9124] Route risk reviews.
[9125] Enforce approval limits.
[9126] Enforce SoD.
[9127] Support corrections.
[9128] Support resubmissions.
[9129] Support disputes.
[9130] Support reversals.
[9131] Prepare payments.
[9132] Maintain remittance information.
[9133] Maintain complete audit history.
[9134] Prevent duplicate processing.
[9135] Handle concurrency.
[9136] Recover from failures.
[9137] Support bulk claims.
[9138] Protect sensitive data.
[9139] Handle unknown states safely.
[9141] 7.66 PART VII SUMMARY
[9142] The Claims Management architecture establishes the financial and clinical control layer of the HMO platform.
[9143] The core principle is:
[9144] A claim must never be approved merely because it was submitted.
[9145] It must pass through controlled evaluation of:
[9146] WHO
[9147] ↓
[9148] WHICH MEMBER
[9149] ↓
[9150] WHICH PLAN
[9151] ↓
[9152] WHICH PLAN VERSION
[9153] ↓
[9154] WHICH PROVIDER
[9155] ↓
[9156] WHICH CONTRACT
[9157] ↓
[9158] WHICH SERVICE
[9159] ↓
[9160] WHICH SERVICE DATE
[9161] ↓
[9162] WHICH AUTHORIZATION
[9163] ↓
[9164] WHICH BENEFIT
[9165] ↓
[9166] WHICH TARIFF
[9167] ↓
[9168] WHICH RULES
[9169] ↓
[9170] WHICH REVIEW
[9171] ↓
[9172] WHICH APPROVAL
[9173] ↓
[9174] WHICH PAYMENT
[9175] ↓
[9176] WHICH RECONCILIATION
[9177] The resulting architecture creates a claims system that is secure, explainable, auditable, scalable, reliable, and operationally controlled.
[9178] It also establishes the foundation for the next major layer of the Master Blueprint: Customer Experience, Member Services & Communication Architecture.
[9179] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[9180] Part VIII — Customer Experience, Member Services & Communication Architecture
[9181] Document Type: Enterprise HMO Master Blueprint
Part: VIII of the Master Blueprint
Primary Domain: Member Experience, Employer Services, Provider Experience, Customer Support & Communications
Target Market: Nigerian HMO Operations
Architecture Principles: Performance, Security, Scalability, Reliability, Usability
[9183] 8.0 PURPOSE
[9184] The Customer Experience layer is the interaction layer between the HMO organization and its ecosystem.
[9185] It provides controlled digital access to:
[9186] Members
[9187] Dependants where applicable
[9188] Employers
[9189] Providers
[9190] HMO employees
[9191] Customer service representatives
[9192] Medical teams
[9193] Claims teams
[9194] Finance teams
[9195] Administrators
[9196] Authorized external systems
[9197] The architecture must ensure that different users see different information and different actions according to their identity, role, organization, permissions, scope, and business context.
[9198] The fundamental principle is:
[9199] A user should only see what they are authorized to see and only perform actions they are authorized to perform.
[9201] 8.1 EXPERIENCE CHANNELS
[9202] The platform should support multiple channels.
[9203] 8.1.1 Member Web Portal
[9204] Members can access:
[9205] Profile
[9206] Dependants
[9207] Membership information
[9208] Plan information
[9209] Benefits
[9210] Provider directory
[9211] Eligibility information
[9212] Preauthorization status
[9213] Claims status where applicable
[9214] Service history
[9215] Complaints
[9216] Support
[9217] Notifications
[9218] Documents
[9220] 8.1.2 Member Mobile Application
[9221] The mobile application may provide:
[9222] Secure authentication
[9223] Membership card
[9224] Digital identity
[9225] Provider search
[9226] Benefit information
[9227] Eligibility information
[9228] Authorization requests/status
[9229] Claims visibility
[9230] Notifications
[9231] Support
[9232] Complaints
[9233] Profile management
[9234] Sensitive operations must require appropriate authentication.
[9236] 8.1.3 Employer Portal
[9237] Employer administrators may manage:
[9238] Organization profile
[9239] Employees
[9240] Dependants
[9241] Enrolment
[9242] Employee status
[9243] Plan assignments

[9245] Payments
[9246] Reports
[9247] Support requests
[9248] Documents

[9263] Disputes

[9267] Provider Organization
[9268] +
[9269] Provider Location
[9270] +
[9271] Assigned Users
[9272] +
[9273] Permission
[9274] +
[9275] Data Scope

[9280] Customer service

[9355] Effective date

[9361] 8.5 DIGITAL CARD SECURITY
[9362] A membership QR code must not simply encode sensitive information.
[9363] Instead:
[9364] QR
[9365] ↓
[9366] Secure Token
[9367] ↓
[9368] Verification Service
[9369] ↓
[9370] Authorization Check
[9371] ↓
[9372] Minimum Necessary Information
[9373] Possible verification response:
[9374] Member:
[9375] M-12345
[9377] Status:
[9378] ACTIVE
[9380] Plan:
[9381] PLAN-001
[9383] Eligibility:
[9384] VALID
[9385] No unnecessary clinical history should be returned.

[9403] Laboratory

[9405] Specialist
[9407] 8.7 PROVIDER DIRECTORY SECURITY
[9408] The directory must distinguish between:
[9409] Public Provider Information
[9410] Potentially:
[9411] Name
[9412] Address
[9413] Phone
[9414] Service category
[9415] Operating hours
[9416] Member-Restricted Information
[9417] Potentially:
[9418] Network eligibility

[9420] Benefit applicability
[9421] Internal Information
[9422] Never expose publicly:
[9423] Internal contract terms
[9424] Provider risk indicators
[9425] Internal financial data
[9426] Investigation information
[9427] Internal performance notes
[9429] 8.8 MEMBER ELIGIBILITY VERIFICATION
[9430] Members and authorized providers should be able to verify eligibility.

[9436] Plan

[9439] ELIGIBLE
[9440] or:
[9441] NOT_ELIGIBLE
[9442] or:

[9444] or:
[9445] REQUIRES_REFERRAL
[9446] or:
[9447] MANUAL_REVIEW

[9464] CASE-2026-000452

[9481] ↓

[9483] ↓

[9487] CANCELLED

[9544] 8.13 INTELLIGENT CASE ASSIGNMENT
[9545] The system may recommend case assignment based on:
[9546] Team availability
[9547] Current workload
[9548] Skill
[9549] Department
[9550] Geographic scope
[9551] Case complexity
[9552] SLA risk
[9553] Automation must not override authorization boundaries.
[9554] A staff member should never receive a case outside their permitted scope simply because the assignment engine selected them.
[9556] 8.14 SLA MANAGEMENT
[9557] Each service type can have its own SLA.
[9558] Example:
[9559] The system must support:
[9560] SLA start
[9561] SLA pause
[9562] SLA resume
[9563] SLA breach
[9564] Escalation
[9565] Resolution
[9567] 8.15 SLA ESCALATION
[9568] Example:
[9569] Case Created
[9570] ↓
[9571] 50% SLA consumed
[9572] ↓
[9573] Reminder
[9574] ↓
[9575] 80% SLA consumed
[9576] ↓
[9577] Supervisor Alert
[9578] ↓
[9579] 100% SLA consumed
[9580] ↓
[9581] SLA Breach
[9582] ↓
[9583] Manager Escalation
[9584] Escalation rules should be configurable.
[9586] 8.16 CUSTOMER PRIORITY
[9587] Cases may have priority levels:
[9588] LOW
[9589] NORMAL
[9590] HIGH
[9591] URGENT
[9592] CRITICAL
[9593] Priority should be based on defined business rules rather than individual employee preference.
[9594] Possible factors:
[9595] Emergency healthcare issue
[9596] Vulnerable customer category where policy permits
[9597] Financial impact
[9598] Service disruption
[9599] Complaint escalation
[9600] Regulatory deadline
[9602] 8.17 COMPLAINT MANAGEMENT
[9603] Complaints should be separate from ordinary customer enquiries.
[9604] Lifecycle:
[9605] SUBMITTED
[9606] ↓
[9607] ACKNOWLEDGED
[9608] ↓
[9609] CLASSIFIED
[9610] ↓
[9611] ASSIGNED
[9612] ↓
[9613] INVESTIGATION
[9614] ↓
[9615] RESPONSE_PREPARATION
[9616] ↓
[9617] CUSTOMER_RESPONSE
[9618] ↓
[9619] RESOLVED
[9620] ↓
[9621] CLOSED
[9622] Possible states:
[9623] ESCALATED
[9624] REOPENED
[9625] WITHDRAWN
[9626] INVALID
[9628] 8.18 COMPLAINT CATEGORIES
[9629] Examples:
[9630] Provider conduct
[9631] Claims
[9632] Benefit access
[9633] Authorization delay
[9634] Billing
[9635] Payment
[9636] Membership
[9637] Data/privacy
[9638] Customer service
[9639] Digital platform
[9640] Other
[9641] The category list must be configurable.
[9643] 8.19 COMPLAINT INVESTIGATION
[9644] Authorized investigators may review:
[9645] Customer history
[9646] Case history
[9647] Relevant claim
[9648] Provider activity
[9649] Communication history
[9650] Authorization records
[9651] Supporting documents
[9652] They should not automatically receive unrestricted access to the customer's entire medical history.
[9654] 8.20 MINIMUM NECESSARY ACCESS
[9655] Customer-service employees may need:
[9656] Member:
[9657] Name
[9658] Member ID
[9659] Status
[9660] Plan
[9661] Relevant case
[9662] They may not need:
[9663] Complete clinical history
[9664] The platform should therefore support field-level and context-aware access.
[9666] 8.21 CUSTOMER COMMUNICATION
[9667] The platform should support:
[9668] In-App
[9669] Alerts
[9670] Case updates
[9671] Claim updates
[9672] Authorization updates
[9673] Email
[9674] Notifications
[9675] Documents
[9676] Case updates
[9677] SMS
[9678] OTP
[9679] Critical notifications
[9680] Important status updates
[9681] Push Notifications
[9682] Mobile application events
[9683] Portal Notifications
[9684] Employer
[9685] Provider
[9687] 8.22 NOTIFICATION SERVICE
[9688] A centralized notification service should receive events.
[9689] Example:
[9690] Claim Approved
[9691] ↓
[9692] Notification Event
[9693] ↓
[9694] Notification Service
[9695] ↓
[9696] Preference Check
[9697] ↓
[9698] Channel Selection
[9699] ↓
[9700] Delivery
[9701] ↓
[9702] Delivery Status
[9704] 8.23 NOTIFICATION PREFERENCES
[9705] Users should be able to configure permitted notification preferences where appropriate.
[9706] Examples:
[9707] Email
[9708] SMS
[9709] Push
[9710] In-app
[9711] Critical security and service notifications may not be suppressible.
[9713] 8.24 NOTIFICATION TEMPLATE ENGINE
[9714] Templates should support:
[9715] Subject
[9716] Body
[9717] Language
[9718] Event
[9719] Recipient type
[9720] Variables
[9721] Version
[9722] Approval status
[9723] Example variables:
[9724] {{member_name}}
[9725] {{claim_number}}
[9726] {{case_reference}}
[9727] {{status}}
[9728] {{date}}
[9729] Templates must be version-controlled.
[9731] 8.25 NOTIFICATION SECURITY
[9732] Notifications must not expose excessive sensitive information.
[9733] Bad example:
[9734] Your MRI diagnosis has been rejected.
[9735] Safer example:
[9736] Your healthcare request has an update. Please sign in to view details.
[9737] Sensitive details should be accessed through authenticated channels.
[9739] 8.26 EMAIL AND SMS FAILURE
[9740] If SMS delivery fails:
[9741] SMS FAILED
[9742] ↓
[9743] Retry Policy
[9744] ↓
[9745] Retry
[9746] ↓
[9747] Alternative Channel Where Permitted
[9748] The system must distinguish:
[9749] Delivery failed
[9750] Provider rejected
[9751] Invalid phone number
[9752] Temporary network failure
[9753] Permanent failure
[9755] 8.27 OUT-OF-ORDER NOTIFICATIONS
[9756] Suppose:
[9757] Claim Approved
[9758] arrives before:
[9759] Claim Submitted
[9760] The notification service must not blindly present contradictory information.
[9761] Events should contain:
[9762] Event ID
[9763] Event type
[9764] Entity ID
[9765] Event timestamp
[9766] Sequence/version
[9767] Correlation ID
[9768] The system should handle out-of-order events safely.
[9770] 8.28 DUPLICATE NOTIFICATIONS
[9771] A user should not receive the same notification repeatedly because an event was processed twice.
[9772] Use:
[9773] Event ID
[9774] +
[9775] Notification ID
[9776] +
[9777] Idempotency
[9778] to prevent duplicate delivery.
[9780] 8.29 CUSTOMER COMMUNICATION HISTORY
[9781] Authorized staff should be able to view communication history.
[9782] Example:
[9783] 10:00 — SMS sent
[9784] 10:01 — SMS delivered
[9785] 10:05 — Customer opened portal
[9786] 10:10 — Customer submitted case
[9787] 10:20 — Agent responded
[9788] Sensitive message contents must respect authorization.
[9790] 8.30 OMNICHANNEL CONVERSATION MODEL
[9791] The platform should unify interactions.
[9792] Example:
[9793] Customer
[9794] ↓
[9795] SMS
[9796] ↓
[9797] Case Created
[9798] ↓
[9799] Portal
[9800] ↓
[9801] Agent
[9802] ↓
[9803] Email
[9804] The system should maintain a single case/customer context rather than creating disconnected records.
[9806] 8.31 MEMBER PROFILE SELF-SERVICE
[9807] Members may request changes to permitted information.
[9808] Examples:
[9809] Phone number
[9810] Email
[9811] Address
[9812] Emergency contact
[9813] High-risk changes should require verification.
[9814] For example:
[9815] Change Phone Number
[9816] ↓
[9817] Authentication
[9818] ↓
[9819] OTP Verification
[9820] ↓
[9821] Risk Check
[9822] ↓
[9823] Update
[9824] ↓
[9825] Audit
[9827] 8.32 SENSITIVE PROFILE CHANGES
[9828] Certain changes may require additional controls:
[9829] Name
[9830] Date of birth
[9831] Identification number
[9832] Bank information
[9833] Employer relationship
[9834] Dependant relationship
[9835] The system may require:
[9836] Supporting documentation
[9837] Manual verification
[9838] Step-up authentication
[9839] Approval
[9841] 8.33 MEMBER DEPENDANT MANAGEMENT
[9842] Members may submit dependant requests.
[9843] Workflow:
[9844] REQUEST
[9845] ↓
[9846] VALIDATION
[9847] ↓
[9848] DOCUMENT REVIEW
[9849] ↓
[9850] DUPLICATE CHECK
[9851] ↓
[9852] APPROVAL
[9853] ↓
[9854] ENROLLMENT
[9855] The system must prevent:
[9856] Duplicate dependants
[9857] Invalid relationships
[9858] Overlapping coverage
[9859] Unauthorized dependant creation
[9861] 8.34 EMPLOYER CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE
[9862] Employer administrators should have dashboards for:
[9863] Active employees
[9864] Pending enrolments
[9865] Dependants
[9866] Plan assignments
[9867] Billing
[9868] Outstanding invoices
[9869] Claims summaries where authorized
[9870] Service requests
[9871] Reports
[9872] Documents
[9873] Employer users must be isolated to their organization.
[9875] 8.35 EMPLOYER USER ROLES
[9876] Example roles:
[9877] Employer Administrator
[9878] Broad organization-level access.
[9879] HR Officer
[9880] Employee/enrollment management.
[9881] Finance Officer
[9882] Invoices and payment information.
[9883] Viewer
[9884] Read-only access.
[9885] Integration User
[9886] API/service access.
[9887] Each role should have configurable permissions.
[9889] 8.36 PROVIDER CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE
[9890] Provider users may have:
[9891] Reception/Front Desk
[9892] Eligibility
[9893] Member verification
[9894] Basic authorization status
[9895] Claims Officer
[9896] Claim creation
[9897] Claim submission
[9898] Claim status
[9899] Clinical User
[9900] Clinical documents
[9901] Authorization information
[9902] Provider Finance
[9903] Settlement
[9904] Remittance
[9905] Payment information
[9906] Provider Administrator
[9907] Provider users
[9908] Profile
[9909] Documents
[9910] Provider users must not automatically inherit every provider permission.
[9912] 8.37 PROVIDER USER ISOLATION
[9913] If a provider operates multiple locations:
[9914] Provider Organization
[9915] ↓
[9916] Location A
[9917] ↓
[9918] Location B
[9919] ↓
[9920] Location C
[9921] A user assigned only to Location A should not automatically access Location B.
[9922] Scope must be explicitly defined.
[9924] 8.38 CUSTOMER SERVICE ROLE MODEL
[9925] Customer service roles may include:
[9926] Customer Service Agent
[9927] Senior Customer Service Agent
[9928] Team Lead
[9929] Customer Service Manager
[9930] Escalation Officer
[9931] Complaint Officer
[9932] Quality Assurance Officer
[9933] Permissions should be separated.
[9934] For example:
[9935] A customer service agent may:
[9936] case.read
[9937] case.create
[9938] case.update
[9939] member.read_limited
[9940] but may not:
[9941] claim.approve
[9942] payment.execute
[9943] provider.contract.modify
[9945] 8.39 KNOWLEDGE BASE
[9946] The platform should provide an internal knowledge base.
[9947] Content may include:
[9948] Benefit explanations
[9949] Service procedures
[9950] Provider procedures
[9951] Claims procedures
[9952] Escalation rules
[9953] Customer-service scripts
[9954] Frequently asked questions
[9955] Internal policies
[9956] Knowledge articles should support:
[9957] Versioning
[9958] Ownership
[9959] Approval
[9960] Publication
[9961] Expiration
[9962] Search
[9964] 8.40 KNOWLEDGE BASE ACCESS
[9965] Articles may be classified:
[9966] PUBLIC
[9967] MEMBER
[9968] PROVIDER
[9969] EMPLOYER
[9970] INTERNAL
[9971] RESTRICTED
[9972] A restricted internal policy must never accidentally appear in member search results.
[9974] 8.41 SEARCH ARCHITECTURE
[9975] Search should support:
[9976] Members
[9977] Providers
[9978] Claims
[9979] Cases
[9980] Employers
[9981] Plans
[9982] Documents
[9983] Knowledge articles
[9984] Search results must apply authorization before returning records.
[9985] The system must never:
[9986] Search all records
[9987] ↓
[9988] Filter unauthorized records in UI
[9989] Instead:
[9990] Authorization-aware query
[9991] ↓
[9992] Only authorized records returned
[9994] 8.42 CUSTOMER DATA PRIVACY
[9995] Customer data should be classified.
[9996] Example:
[9997] Public
[9998] Provider public directory information.
[9999] Internal
[10000] Operational information.
[10001] Confidential
[10002] Customer contact information.
[10003] Restricted
[10004] Healthcare and financial information.
[10005] Highly Restricted
[10006] Sensitive clinical, investigation, security, or privileged information.
[10007] The classification system must integrate with authorization policies.
[10009] 8.43 CUSTOMER DATA MASKING
[10010] Examples:
[10011] Phone:
[10012] 080****1234
[10013] Bank Account:
[10014] ****5678
[10015] Sensitive fields should only be unmasked when the user's role and context permit it.
[10017] 8.44 CUSTOMER SUPPORT AUDIT
[10018] The system should record:
[10019] Who viewed a case
[10020] Who changed a case
[10021] Who reassigned it
[10022] Who escalated it
[10023] Who accessed documents
[10024] Who changed customer information
[10025] Who closed it
[10026] Who reopened it
[10027] Who exported information
[10029] 8.45 CUSTOMER SUPPORT FRAUD/ABUSE CONTROLS
[10030] Monitor for:
[10031] Excessive customer lookups
[10032] Mass member searches
[10033] Unauthorized profile changes
[10034] Excessive data exports
[10035] Repeated sensitive-data access
[10036] Unusual after-hours access
[10037] Repeated access to unrelated customers
[10038] These should generate risk/security events where appropriate.
[10040] 8.46 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE API
[10041] Representative APIs:
[10042] GET    /member/profile
[10043] PATCH  /member/profile
[10044] GET    /member/benefits
[10045] GET    /member/providers
[10046] GET    /member/claims
[10047] GET    /member/authorizations
[10049] POST   /cases
[10050] GET    /cases/{caseId}
[10051] PATCH  /cases/{caseId}
[10052] POST   /cases/{caseId}/assign
[10053] POST   /cases/{caseId}/escalate
[10054] POST   /cases/{caseId}/resolve
[10055] POST   /cases/{caseId}/reopen
[10057] GET    /notifications
[10058] PATCH  /notifications/preferences
[10060] GET    /knowledge-base
[10061] GET    /knowledge-base/{articleId}
[10062] Every endpoint must enforce authorization independently.
[10064] 8.47 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE PERFORMANCE
[10065] Target architecture:
[10066] Performance must be measured under realistic production-like loads.
[10068] 8.48 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE RELIABILITY
[10069] The platform should support:
[10070] Retry-safe requests
[10071] Idempotent case creation
[10072] Notification retry
[10073] Queue recovery
[10074] Dead-letter queues
[10075] Offline mobile handling
[10076] Temporary service degradation
[10077] Graceful error messages
[10078] Service health monitoring
[10079] If the notification service fails, core claims and eligibility operations should continue where technically possible.
[10081] 8.49 MOBILE CONNECTIVITY EDGE CASES
[10082] Because users may operate under unstable network conditions, mobile applications must handle:
[10083] Slow connections
[10084] Network loss
[10085] Duplicate submissions
[10086] Request timeout
[10087] App termination during submission
[10088] Offline state
[10089] Expired authentication
[10090] Token refresh failure
[10091] Example:
[10092] User submits request
[10093] ↓
[10094] Network disappears
[10095] ↓
[10096] Client does not know whether request succeeded
[10097] ↓
[10098] Retry using idempotency key
[10099] ↓
[10100] Server returns original result
[10101] This prevents duplicate cases.
[10103] 8.50 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE EDGE-CASE MATRIX
[10105] 8.51 UNKNOWN CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE EDGE CASE STRATEGY
[10106] For unexpected customer-service conditions:
[10107] UNKNOWN CONDITION
[10108] ↓
[10109] DO NOT GUESS
[10110] ↓
[10111] PRESERVE DATA
[10112] ↓
[10113] DO NOT GRANT EXTRA ACCESS
[10114] ↓
[10115] SAFE ERROR
[10116] ↓
[10117] CORRELATION ID
[10118] ↓
[10119] AUDIT/LOG
[10120] ↓
[10121] MONITOR
[10122] ↓
[10123] CONTROLLED RECOVERY
[10124] The most important rule is:
[10125] An unknown condition must never become an authorization bypass.
[10127] 8.52 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE OBSERVABILITY
[10128] Monitor:
[10129] Operational
[10130] Cases created
[10131] Cases resolved
[10132] Cases pending
[10133] SLA breaches
[10134] Average resolution time
[10135] Technical
[10136] API latency
[10137] Error rate
[10138] Notification delivery
[10139] Queue depth
[10140] Mobile crash rate
[10141] Authentication failures
[10142] Security
[10143] Unauthorized access
[10144] Sensitive data access
[10145] Export activity
[10146] Suspicious searches
[10147] Privilege violations
[10149] 8.53 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE ANALYTICS
[10150] Management dashboards may show:
[10151] Case volume
[10152] Case categories
[10153] Resolution times
[10154] SLA performance
[10155] Complaint trends
[10156] Provider complaints
[10157] Claims-related complaints
[10158] Benefit-related complaints
[10159] Member satisfaction indicators
[10160] Channel usage
[10161] Notification delivery rates
[10162] Analytics must use appropriate access controls and privacy protections.
[10164] 8.54 CUSTOMER FEEDBACK
[10165] The system may collect:
[10166] Case satisfaction
[10167] Service feedback
[10168] Provider experience
[10169] Portal experience
[10170] Mobile experience
[10171] Feedback should be associated with the relevant service/case where possible.
[10172] The system should distinguish:
[10173] SERVICE FEEDBACK
[10174] from:
[10175] FORMAL COMPLAINT
[10176] They should not automatically have the same workflow.
[10178] 8.55 ACCESSIBILITY
[10179] The experience layer should target accessible design.
[10180] Requirements should include:
[10181] Keyboard navigation
[10182] Clear labels
[10183] Accessible forms
[10184] Appropriate contrast
[10185] Screen-reader compatibility
[10186] Error explanations
[10187] Consistent navigation
[10188] Responsive layouts
[10189] Accessible authentication flows
[10190] Accessibility requirements should be tested rather than treated as documentation only.
[10192] 8.56 MULTI-LANGUAGE ARCHITECTURE
[10193] The platform should be designed for localization.
[10194] At minimum, the architecture should avoid hard-coding user-facing text.
[10195] Support should be possible for:
[10196] English
[10197] Future additional languages
[10198] Templates, notifications, error messages, labels, and knowledge articles should support localization.
[10200] 8.57 CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE GOVERNANCE
[10201] Every customer-facing feature should have:
[10202] Product owner
[10203] Business owner
[10204] Technical owner
[10205] Security classification
[10206] Data owner
[10207] SLA
[10208] Audit requirements
[10209] Support procedure
[10210] Incident procedure
[10211] Version history
[10213] 8.58 PART VIII SECURITY REQUIREMENTS
[10214] The customer-experience layer must enforce:
[10215] Authentication.
[10216] Authorization.
[10217] Tenant isolation.
[10218] Role restrictions.
[10219] Data-scope restrictions.
[10220] Field-level masking.
[10221] Secure sessions.
[10222] MFA where required.
[10223] Secure notifications.
[10224] Export controls.
[10225] Audit logging.
[10226] Document protection.
[10227] API security.
[10228] Rate limiting.
[10229] Abuse monitoring.
[10230] Privacy controls.
[10232] 8.59 PART VIII PERFORMANCE REQUIREMENTS
[10233] The experience layer should target:
[10234] Fast dashboards
[10235] Efficient search
[10236] Lightweight mobile responses
[10237] Asynchronous document processing
[10238] Cached non-sensitive reference data
[10239] Paginated lists
[10240] Lazy loading
[10241] Background notification processing
[10242] Large datasets should never be loaded unnecessarily into the browser or mobile device.
[10244] 8.60 PART VIII SCALABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[10245] The platform should support:
[10246] Thousands of Members
[10247] ↓
[10248] Thousands of Employers
[10249] ↓
[10250] Thousands of Providers
[10251] ↓
[10252] Large Employee Populations
[10253] ↓
[10254] Large Case Volumes
[10255] ↓
[10256] Large Notification Volumes
[10257] ↓
[10258] Large Concurrent User Population
[10259] Services should be independently scalable.
[10261] 8.61 PART VIII RELIABILITY REQUIREMENTS
[10262] Customer-facing services should implement:
[10263] Retry mechanisms
[10264] Circuit breakers
[10265] Queue-based notifications
[10266] Idempotent submissions
[10267] Graceful degradation
[10268] Health checks
[10269] Disaster recovery
[10270] Backup systems
[10271] Monitoring
[10272] Alerting
[10273] Critical HMO operations should not depend on a single communication channel.
[10275] 8.62 PART VIII USABILITY PRINCIPLES
[10276] The system should follow:
[10277] Principle 1 — Simple
[10278] Users should understand what to do.
[10279] Principle 2 — Contextual
[10280] Show actions relevant to the current situation.
[10281] Principle 3 — Safe
[10282] Warn before high-impact actions.
[10283] Principle 4 — Transparent
[10284] Explain statuses and next steps.
[10285] Principle 5 — Accessible
[10286] Support different abilities and devices.
[10287] Principle 6 — Permission-Aware
[10288] Never display actions that the user cannot legitimately perform.
[10290] 8.63 END-TO-END CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE EXAMPLE
[10291] A member visits a hospital.
[10292] Member
[10293] ↓
[10294] Provider scans membership QR
[10295] ↓
[10296] Secure verification
[10297] ↓
[10298] Eligibility check
[10299] ↓
[10300] Member eligible
[10301] ↓
[10302] Service requires authorization
[10303] ↓
[10304] Provider submits authorization
[10305] ↓
[10306] Medical team reviews
[10307] ↓
[10308] Authorization approved
[10309] ↓
[10310] Healthcare service delivered
[10311] ↓
[10312] Provider submits claim
[10313] ↓
[10314] Claim adjudicated
[10315] ↓
[10316] Payment processed
[10317] ↓
[10318] Member receives appropriate notification
[10319] The customer-experience layer connects all these events without exposing information the member or provider is not authorized to see.
[10321] 8.64 PART VIII COMPLETION CRITERIA
[10322] Part VIII is complete when the platform can:
[10323] Support member self-service.
[10324] Support employer self-service.
[10325] Support provider self-service.
[10326] Provide secure digital membership cards.
[10327] Provide provider search.
[10328] Provide eligibility verification.
[10329] Create and manage customer cases.
[10330] Route cases.
[10331] Manage SLAs.
[10332] Manage complaints.
[10333] Support escalations.
[10334] Send notifications.
[10335] Track communication history.
[10336] Protect sensitive information.
[10337] Support field-level masking.
[10338] Prevent cross-tenant access.
[10339] Support mobile connectivity failures.
[10340] Prevent duplicate submissions.
[10341] Provide knowledge management.
[10342] Provide accessible interfaces.
[10343] Monitor customer experience.
[10344] Audit customer interactions.
[10346] 8.65 PART VIII SUMMARY
[10347] The customer-experience architecture transforms the HMO platform from an internal administrative system into an integrated digital ecosystem.
[10348] The complete interaction model becomes:
[10349] MEMBER
[10350] ↕
[10351] MEMBER PORTAL / MOBILE
[10352] ↕
[10353] CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE SERVICES
[10354] ↕
[10355] IDENTITY + ACCESS CONTROL
[10356] ↕
[10357] CASE MANAGEMENT
[10358] ↕
[10359] HEALTHCARE SERVICES
[10360] ↕
[10361] ELIGIBILITY
[10362] ↕
[10363] PREAUTHORIZATION
[10364] ↕
[10365] CLAIMS
[10366] ↕
[10367] FINANCE
[10368] ↕
[10369] NOTIFICATIONS
[10370] ↕
[10371] AUDIT + SECURITY
[10372] The critical design principle remains:
[10373] Every interaction must be useful to the customer while remaining constrained by identity, authorization, scope, privacy, and business rules.
[10374] This completes Part VIII of the Complete HMO Master Blueprint.
[10375] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[10376] Part IX — Data Architecture & Master Data Management
[10377] Document Type: Enterprise HMO Master Blueprint
Part: IX of the Master Blueprint
Primary Domain: Enterprise Data Architecture, Master Data, Data Governance, Data Quality, Privacy & Historical Integrity
Target Market: Nigerian HMO Operations
Architecture Principles: Performance, Security, Scalability, Reliability, Usability
[10379] 9.0 PURPOSE
[10380] The HMO platform requires a centralized and governed data architecture capable of supporting:
[10381] Members
[10382] Dependants
[10383] Employers
[10384] Employer employees
[10385] Providers
[10386] Provider locations
[10387] Provider users
[10388] Plans
[10389] Benefits
[10390] Enrolments
[10391] Eligibility
[10392] Authorizations
[10393] Referrals
[10394] Claims
[10395] Claim lines
[10396] Clinical reviews
[10397] Finance
[10398] Payments
[10399] Complaints
[10400] Customer cases
[10401] Documents
[10402] Notifications
[10403] Users
[10404] Roles
[10405] Permissions
[10406] Audit records
[10407] Security events
[10408] Analytics
[10409] The data architecture must ensure:
[10410] One trusted source of truth, controlled access, historical integrity, clear ownership, strong data quality, and safe scalability.
[10412] 9.1 DATA ARCHITECTURE PRINCIPLES
[10413] The platform shall follow these principles:
[10414] Single source of truth for core master data.
[10415] Explicit ownership of every critical dataset.
[10416] Strong referential integrity.
[10417] Historical records must remain traceable.
[10418] Sensitive data must be classified.
[10419] Data access must be authorization-controlled.
[10420] Data should be immutable where historical integrity requires it.
[10421] Financial records must never be silently overwritten.
[10422] Clinical records must preserve historical context.
[10423] Personally identifiable information must be protected.
[10424] Data changes must be auditable.
[10425] Duplicate master records must be controlled.
[10426] Soft deletion should be preferred for important business records.
[10427] Hard deletion must be restricted.
[10428] Data retention must be policy-driven.
[10429] Cross-organization data isolation must be enforced.
[10430] Data quality must be continuously monitored.
[10432] 9.2 HIGH-LEVEL DATA ARCHITECTURE
[10433] The platform should use multiple logical data layers.
[10434] ┌──────────────────────┐
[10435] │   Experience Layer   │
[10436] │ Web / Mobile / API   │
[10437] └──────────┬───────────┘
[10438] ↓
[10439] ┌──────────────────────┐
[10440] │ Application Services │
[10441] └──────────┬───────────┘
[10442] ↓
[10443] ┌──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐
[10444] ↓                      ↓                      ↓
[10445] ┌──────────────┐       ┌──────────────┐       ┌──────────────┐
[10446] │ Transactional│       │ Cache/Search │       │ Event Store  │
[10447] │   Database   │       │    Layer     │       │ / Messaging  │
[10448] └──────────────┘       └──────────────┘       └──────────────┘
[10449] ↓                      ↓                      ↓
[10450] └──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┘
[10451] ↓
[10452] ┌──────────────────────┐
[10453] │ Data / Analytics     │
[10454] │ Warehouse/Lakehouse  │
[10455] └──────────────────────┘
[10456] The transactional database remains authoritative for operational transactions.
[10457] The analytics platform should not become the authoritative source for operational decisions.
[10459] 9.3 DATA DOMAINS

[10461] Identity Domain
[10462] Users

[10464] Sessions

[10470] HMO organization
[10471] Regions
[10472] Branches
[10473] Departments
[10474] Teams

[10476] Employers
[10477] Employer contacts
[10478] Employer users
[10479] Employees
[10480] Employer contracts
[10481] Member Domain
[10482] Members
[10483] Dependants
[10484] Member identifiers
[10485] Member relationships
[10486] Provider Domain
[10487] Providers
[10488] Locations
[10489] Provider users
[10490] Credentials
[10491] Contracts
[10492] Networks
[10493] Plan Domain
[10494] Plans
[10495] Plan versions
[10496] Benefits
[10497] Exclusions
[10498] Limits
[10499] Pricing
[10500] Enrollment Domain
[10501] Enrollments
[10502] Coverage periods
[10503] Eligibility
[10504] Healthcare Domain
[10505] Services
[10506] Authorizations
[10507] Referrals
[10508] Clinical reviews
[10509] Utilization
[10510] Claims Domain
[10511] Claims

[10514] Decisions

[10521] Reconciliation

[10533] Audit events
[10534] Security events
[10535] Compliance records
[10537] 9.4 CORE ENTITY MODEL
[10538] The primary relationship can be represented as:
[10539] ORGANIZATION
[10540] │
[10541] ├── REGIONS
[10542] │     └── BRANCHES
[10543] │
[10544] ├── EMPLOYERS
[10545] │     └── EMPLOYEES
[10546] │             └── MEMBERS
[10547] │                    └── DEPENDANTS
[10548] │
[10549] ├── PROVIDERS
[10550] │     ├── LOCATIONS
[10551] │     ├── USERS
[10552] │     ├── CREDENTIALS
[10553] │     └── CONTRACTS
[10554] │
[10555] ├── PLANS
[10556] │     └── PLAN VERSIONS
[10557] │            └── BENEFITS
[10558] │
[10559] └── USERS
[10560] └── ROLES
[10561] └── PERMISSIONS
[10562] Operational relationships:
[10563] MEMBER
[10564] ↓
[10565] ENROLLMENT
[10566] ↓
[10567] PLAN VERSION
[10568] ↓
[10569] ELIGIBILITY
[10570] ↓
[10571] AUTHORIZATION
[10572] ↓

[10574] ↓

[10578] ↓

[10580] ↓
[10581] PAYMENT
[10582] ↓
[10583] RECONCILIATION
[10585] 9.5 ORGANIZATION ENTITY
[10586] The organization entity represents an HMO organization or another controlled business entity.
[10587] Core fields:
[10588] organization_id
[10589] organization_code
[10590] organization_name
[10591] organization_type
[10592] status
[10593] country
[10594] state
[10595] timezone
[10596] currency
[10597] created_at
[10598] updated_at
[10599] Organization IDs must be immutable.
[10601] 9.6 ORGANIZATIONAL HIERARCHY
[10602] The system should support:
[10603] HMO
[10604] ↓
[10605] Region
[10606] ↓
[10607] Branch
[10608] ↓
[10609] Department
[10610] ↓
[10611] Team
[10612] Not every organization must use every level.
[10613] Hierarchy should therefore be configurable.
[10615] 9.7 EMPLOYER DATA MODEL
[10616] Employer information may include:

[10626] status

[10628] created_at
[10629] updated_at
[10630] Sensitive financial and contractual information should be separately protected where necessary.
[10632] 9.8 EMPLOYEE DATA MODEL
[10633] An employer employee record should include:
[10634] employee_id

[10636] employee_reference
[10637] member_id
[10638] employment_status
[10639] employment_start_date
[10640] employment_end_date
[10641] department
[10642] job_title
[10643] created_at
[10644] updated_at
[10645] Important distinction:
[10646] Employer Employee ID is not automatically the same as HMO Member ID.
[10647] A person may also have a separate application identity.
[10649] 9.9 MEMBER DATA MODEL
[10650] Core member fields:
[10651] member_id
[10652] member_number
[10653] member_type
[10654] primary_member_id
[10655] employer_id
[10656] plan_id
[10657] current_enrollment_id
[10658] status
[10659] effective_date
[10660] termination_date
[10661] created_at
[10662] updated_at
[10663] Personal identity information should be stored according to data classification requirements.
[10665] 9.10 MEMBER IDENTIFIERS
[10666] The system should support separate identifiers for:
[10667] Internal Member ID
[10668] Member Number
[10669] Employer Employee Number
[10670] Provider Patient Reference
[10671] National identifier where legally appropriate
[10672] External system reference
[10673] Identifiers should never be assumed interchangeable.
[10675] 9.11 DEPENDANT DATA MODEL
[10676] A dependant record should contain:
[10677] dependant_id
[10678] primary_member_id
[10679] relationship_type
[10680] status
[10681] effective_date
[10682] termination_date
[10683] verification_status
[10684] created_at
[10685] updated_at
[10686] The relationship must be explicit.
[10688] 9.12 PROVIDER DATA MODEL
[10689] Provider master data:
[10690] provider_id
[10691] provider_code
[10692] provider_type
[10693] legal_name
[10694] display_name
[10695] registration_reference
[10696] status
[10697] network_status
[10698] created_at
[10699] updated_at
[10700] Separate entities should exist for:
[10701] Provider
[10702] Provider location
[10703] Provider user
[10704] Provider credential
[10705] Provider contract
[10706] Provider bank account
[10707] This prevents one large provider record from becoming unmanageable.
[10709] 9.13 PROVIDER LOCATION
[10710] Fields may include:
[10711] provider_location_id
[10712] provider_id
[10713] location_name
[10714] address
[10715] state
[10716] city
[10717] phone
[10718] email
[10719] operating_hours
[10720] status
[10721] A provider may have multiple locations.
[10723] 9.14 PROVIDER CREDENTIALS
[10724] Credentials should be independently managed.
[10725] Examples:
[10726] License
[10727] Accreditation
[10728] Certification
[10729] Professional registration
[10730] Operating permit
[10731] Fields:
[10732] credential_id
[10733] provider_id
[10734] credential_type
[10735] credential_number
[10736] issue_date
[10737] expiry_date
[10738] verification_status
[10739] verified_by
[10740] verified_at
[10741] document_id
[10742] Expiry dates should trigger automated monitoring.
[10744] 9.15 PROVIDER CONTRACT DATA
[10745] Provider contracts should be versioned.
[10746] provider_contract
[10747] ↓
[10748] contract_version
[10749] ↓
[10750] tariffs
[10751] ↓
[10752] services
[10753] A contract change must not rewrite the historical terms used by previous claims.
[10755] 9.16 PLAN DATA MODEL
[10756] The plan hierarchy:
[10757] PLAN
[10758] ↓
[10759] PLAN VERSION
[10760] ↓
[10761] BENEFIT
[10762] ↓
[10763] BENEFIT RULE
[10764] ↓
[10765] LIMIT
[10766] ↓
[10767] EXCLUSION
[10768] ↓
[10769] NETWORK
[10770] This allows plans to evolve without destroying historical information.
[10772] 9.17 PLAN VERSIONING
[10773] Every plan version must have:
[10774] plan_version_id
[10775] plan_id
[10776] version_number
[10777] effective_from
[10778] effective_to
[10779] status
[10780] created_by
[10781] approved_by
[10782] created_at
[10783] Effective periods should not overlap unless explicitly supported.
[10785] 9.18 BENEFIT DATA MODEL
[10786] A benefit may contain:
[10787] benefit_id

[10790] benefit_name
[10791] service_category
[10792] coverage_type

[10794] monetary_limit
[10795] frequency_limit
[10796] authorization_required
[10797] referral_required
[10798] copayment
[10799] deductible
[10800] status
[10801] The actual business rules may be stored in a configurable rule structure.
[10803] 9.19 SERVICE MASTER DATA
[10804] Healthcare services should use a controlled service catalogue.
[10805] Example fields:
[10806] service_id
[10807] service_code
[10808] service_name
[10809] category
[10810] description
[10811] unit
[10812] status
[10813] effective_from
[10814] effective_to
[10815] The service catalogue should support versioning where necessary.
[10817] 9.20 ENROLLMENT DATA
[10818] Enrollment represents a member's coverage relationship.
[10819] enrollment_id
[10820] member_id
[10821] employer_id

[10824] effective_from
[10825] effective_to
[10826] status
[10827] enrollment_source
[10828] created_at
[10829] updated_at
[10830] The enrollment record must reference the appropriate plan version.
[10832] 9.21 ELIGIBILITY DATA
[10833] Eligibility decisions may include:
[10834] eligibility_check_id

[10843] authorization_required
[10844] checked_at
[10845] Eligibility results should be traceable.
[10847] 9.22 AUTHORIZATION DATA

[10867] 9.23 CLAIM DATA

[10869] claim_id

[10879] service_start_date

[10884] rejected_amount
[10885] member_responsibility
[10886] hmo_liability
[10887] status
[10888] version
[10889] created_at
[10890] updated_at
[10892] 9.24 CLAIM LINE DATA
[10893] claim_line_id
[10894] claim_id
