[13589] 10.85 Part X Summary
[13590] The enterprise HMO platform should be engineered as a secure, modular, observable and highly resilient system.
[13591] The architecture should support:
[13592] USERS
[13593] ↓
[13594] IDENTITY
[13595] ↓
[13596] AUTHENTICATION
[13597] ↓
[13598] AUTHORIZATION
[13599] ↓
[13600] DATA SCOPE
[13601] ↓
[13602] BUSINESS DOMAIN
[13603] ↓
[13604] TRANSACTION
[13605] ↓
[13606] DATABASE
[13607] ↓
[13608] EVENT
[13609] ↓
[13610] AUDIT
[13611] ↓
[13612] MONITORING
[13613] ↓
[13614] ANALYTICS
[13615] The most important architectural principle remains:
[13616] Every layer must enforce the correct boundary.
[13617] The frontend protects the user experience.
[13618] The API protects the application boundary.
[13619] The domain service protects business rules.
[13620] The data layer protects information boundaries.
[13621] The authorization platform protects privileges.
[13622] The audit platform provides accountability.
[13623] The infrastructure protects availability and resilience.
[13624] Together, these form the technical foundation of the Complete HMO Master Blueprint.
[13625] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[13626] PART XI — SECURITY ARCHITECTURE, CYBERSECURITY, PRIVACY, COMPLIANCE & THREAT MANAGEMENT
[13628] 11.0 Purpose
[13629] Part XI defines the security architecture for the enterprise HMO platform.
[13630] The HMO system handles highly sensitive information, including:
[13631] Member identity information
[13632] Dependants
[13633] Employer information
[13634] Healthcare information
[13635] Medical records
[13636] Claims
[13637] Financial transactions
[13638] Provider information
[13639] Employee information
[13640] Authentication credentials
[13641] Documents
[13642] Audit records
[13643] Payment information
[13644] Organizational information
[13645] Security therefore cannot be treated as a standalone module.
[13646] It must be embedded across:
[13647] IDENTITY
[13648] AUTHENTICATION
[13649] AUTHORIZATION
[13650] APPLICATION
[13651] DATA
[13652] DATABASE
[13653] NETWORK
[13654] INFRASTRUCTURE
[13655] INTEGRATIONS
[13656] DOCUMENTS
[13657] DEVICES
[13658] OPERATIONS
[13659] AUDIT
[13660] MONITORING
[13661] The fundamental security principle is:
[13662] Every request must be authenticated, authorized, scoped, validated, monitored and auditable according to its risk.
[13664] 11.1 Security Objectives
[13665] The security architecture must protect:
[13666] Confidentiality
[13667] Prevent unauthorized access to information.
[13668] Integrity
[13669] Prevent unauthorized or accidental modification of information.
[13670] Availability
[13671] Ensure authorized users can access required services.
[13672] Accountability
[13673] Ensure sensitive actions can be traced to an identity.
[13674] Privacy
[13675] Ensure personal and healthcare information is accessed and processed appropriately.
[13676] Resilience
[13677] Ensure the platform can continue operating or recover safely after attacks or failures.
[13679] 11.2 Security Principles
[13680] The platform shall follow:
[13681] Zero-trust principles
[13682] Least privilege
[13683] Deny-by-default
[13684] Defense in depth
[13685] Separation of duties
[13686] Privileged access management
[13687] Strong authentication
[13688] Data minimization
[13689] Secure-by-design development
[13690] Secure-by-default configuration
[13691] Continuous monitoring
[13692] Complete auditability
[13693] Explicit tenant isolation
[13694] Fail-safe behavior
[13695] Controlled emergency access
[13697] 11.3 Zero-Trust Architecture
[13698] The platform must not automatically trust a request because it originates from:
[13699] an employee
[13700] an internal network
[13701] a known device
[13702] a corporate IP
[13703] a previously authenticated session
[13704] an administrator
[13705] Instead:
[13706] REQUEST
[13707] ↓
[13708] WHO?
[13709] ↓
[13710] AUTHENTICATED?
[13711] ↓
[13712] WHAT DEVICE?
[13713] ↓
[13714] WHAT ROLE?
[13715] ↓
[13716] WHAT PERMISSION?
[13717] ↓
[13718] WHAT RESOURCE?
[13719] ↓
[13720] WHAT DATA SCOPE?
[13721] ↓
[13722] WHAT CONTEXT?
[13723] ↓
[13724] WHAT RISK?
[13725] ↓
[13726] ALLOWED?
[13728] 11.4 Security Architecture
[13729] High-level security architecture:
[13730] INTERNET
[13731] │
[13732] ▼
[13733] CDN / WAF
[13734] │
[13735] ▼
[13736] API GATEWAY
[13737] │
[13738] ▼
[13739] IDENTITY PROVIDER
[13740] │
[13741] ┌────────┴────────┐
[13742] ▼                 ▼
[13743] Authentication     MFA / Risk
[13744] │                 │
[13745] └────────┬────────┘
[13746] ▼
[13747] AUTHORIZATION
[13748] ┌─────────┼─────────┐
[13749] ▼         ▼         ▼
[13750] RBAC       ABAC       SoD
[13751] │         │         │
[13752] └─────────┼─────────┘
[13753] ▼
[13754] APPLICATIONS
[13755] │
[13756] ┌────────────┼────────────┐
[13757] ▼            ▼            ▼
[13758] Database      Services     Storage
[13759] │            │            │
[13760] └────────────┼────────────┘
[13761] ▼
[13762] SECURITY MONITORING
[13763] │
[13764] ▼
[13765] SIEM / SOC
[13767] 11.5 Identity Security
[13768] Every human and machine identity must have a unique identifier.
[13769] The system should distinguish:
[13770] User ID
[13771] Employee ID
[13772] Member ID
[13773] Provider User ID
[13774] Employer User ID
[13775] Service Account ID
[13776] Device ID
[13777] Session ID
[13778] One person may have several business relationships, but these must not become uncontrolled identities.
[13780] 11.6 Identity Lifecycle
[13781] Identity states:
[13782] REQUESTED
[13783] ↓
[13784] PENDING_VERIFICATION
[13785] ↓
[13786] PENDING_APPROVAL
[13787] ↓
[13788] ACTIVE
[13789] ↓
[13790] SUSPENDED
[13791] ↓
[13792] REACTIVATED
[13793] ↓
[13794] DEACTIVATED
[13795] ↓
[13796] ARCHIVED
[13797] Additional security states:
[13798] LOCKED
[13799] COMPROMISED
[13800] PENDING_MFA
[13801] PENDING_TERMINATION
[13803] 11.7 Joiner-Mover-Leaver Security
[13804] The platform must automatically support:
[13805] Joiner
[13806] New employee:
[13807] Employee Created
[13808] ↓
[13809] Identity Created
[13810] ↓
[13811] Manager Approval
[13812] ↓
[13813] Role Assignment
[13814] ↓
[13815] Scope Assignment
[13816] ↓
[13817] MFA Enrollment
[13818] ↓
[13819] Access Activated
[13820] Mover
[13821] Employee changes department:
[13822] Old Role
[13823] ↓
[13824] Access Reassessment
[13825] ↓
[13826] Old Privileges Removed
[13827] ↓
[13828] New Role Assigned
[13829] ↓
[13830] New Scope Assigned
[13831] Leaver
[13832] Employee leaves:
[13833] Employment Terminated
[13834] ↓
[13835] Access Revoked
[13836] ↓
[13837] Sessions Revoked
[13838] ↓
[13839] Tokens Invalidated
[13840] ↓
[13841] Privileged Access Removed
[13842] ↓
[13843] Devices / Credentials Revoked
[13844] ↓
[13845] Audit Record Preserved
[13847] 11.8 Authentication Architecture
[13848] Supported authentication methods may include:
[13849] username/password
[13850] email/password
[13851] phone/OTP
[13852] authenticator applications
[13853] passkeys
[13854] biometric authentication
[13855] enterprise SSO
[13856] identity federation
[13857] service credentials
[13858] certificates
[13859] Authentication requirements should depend on risk.
[13861] 11.9 Password Security
[13862] Passwords must never be stored in plaintext.
[13863] Use a modern password hashing mechanism such as:
[13864] Argon2id
[13865] bcrypt
[13866] another approved password hashing algorithm
[13867] Passwords should not be reversible.
[13869] 11.10 Multi-Factor Authentication
[13870] MFA should be required for:
[13871] administrators
[13872] privileged users
[13873] finance users
[13874] security users
[13875] high-risk medical access
[13876] sensitive exports
[13877] bank account changes
[13878] high-value financial transactions
[13879] security configuration changes
[13880] break-glass access
[13881] Possible factors:
[13882] Something You Know
[13883] +
[13884] Something You Have
[13885] +
[13886] Something You Are
[13888] 11.11 Step-Up Authentication
[13889] A user may already be authenticated but still require stronger verification.
[13890] Example:
[13891] User Login
[13892] ↓
[13893] Normal Authentication
[13894] ↓
[13895] User Opens Claim
[13896] ↓
[13897] Normal Access
[13898] ↓
[13899] User Requests High-Risk Export
[13900] ↓
[13901] STEP-UP MFA
[13902] ↓
[13903] Additional Authorization
[13905] 11.12 Session Security
[13906] Sessions should include:
[13907] session ID
[13908] user ID
[13909] device ID
[13910] creation time
[13911] last activity
[13912] expiry
[13913] authentication strength
[13914] IP metadata where appropriate
[13915] risk information
[13916] Sessions must support:
[13917] timeout
[13918] revocation
[13919] logout
[13920] forced logout
[13921] suspicious-session detection
[13923] 11.13 Session Revocation
[13924] When a security event occurs:
[13925] Account Compromised
[13926] ↓
[13927] Revoke Sessions
[13928] ↓
[13929] Invalidate Tokens
[13930] ↓
[13931] Require Reauthentication
[13932] ↓
[13933] Require MFA
[13935] 11.14 Device Security
[13936] Devices should be tracked where appropriate.
[13937] Device records may include:
[13938] device ID
[13939] platform
[13940] browser
[13941] operating system
[13942] registration time
[13943] last activity
[13944] trust state
[13945] risk state
[13946] Possible states:
[13947] UNKNOWN
[13948] REGISTERED
[13949] TRUSTED
[13950] RESTRICTED
[13951] BLOCKED
[13952] REVOKED
[13954] 11.15 Suspicious Device Detection
[13955] Potential signals:
[13956] new device
[13957] impossible travel indicators
[13958] unusual login time
[13959] unusual geographic pattern
[13960] repeated failed authentication
[13961] abnormal session behavior
[13962] known compromised device indicators
[13963] Risk signals should trigger appropriate controls rather than automatically determining malicious intent.
[13965] 11.16 Account Lockout
[13966] Repeated authentication failures should trigger controlled protection.
[13967] However, the design must avoid allowing attackers to permanently lock legitimate users out through repeated login attempts.
[13968] Possible controls:
[13969] progressive delays
[13970] temporary lock
[13971] CAPTCHA/risk challenge where appropriate
[13972] MFA
[13973] security notification
[13974] administrator-assisted recovery
[13976] 11.17 Account Recovery
[13977] Account recovery is a high-risk operation.
[13978] Recovery should require stronger verification than normal login where appropriate.
[13979] Controls include:
[13980] identity verification
[13981] MFA recovery
[13982] recovery codes
[13983] trusted channels
[13984] administrator approval for privileged accounts
[13985] session revocation after recovery
[13986] audit logging
[13988] 11.18 Authorization Security
[13989] Authorization must follow:
[13990] IDENTITY
[13991] +
[13992] ROLE
[13993] +
[13994] PERMISSION
[13995] +
[13996] RESOURCE
[13997] +
[13998] DATA SCOPE
[13999] +
[14000] ATTRIBUTES
[14001] +
[14002] BUSINESS RULES
[14003] +
[14004] SoD
[14005] +
[14006] RISK
[14008] 11.19 RBAC Security
[14009] Roles should represent business responsibilities.
[14010] Examples:
[14011] Customer Service Officer
[14012] Claims Officer
[14013] Claims Manager
[14014] Medical Officer
[14015] Provider Manager
[14016] Finance Officer
[14017] Finance Manager
[14018] Compliance Officer
[14019] Auditor
[14020] Security Administrator
[14021] Platform Administrator
[14022] Roles should not be created simply because an employee asks for additional permissions.
[14024] 11.20 Permission Model
[14025] Permissions should follow a consistent naming scheme.
[14026] Example:
[14027] member.read
[14028] member.create
[14029] member.update
[14030] member.suspend
[14032] claim.read
[14033] claim.create
[14034] claim.submit
[14035] claim.review
[14036] claim.approve
[14037] claim.reject
[14039] finance.payment.read
[14040] finance.payment.create
[14041] finance.payment.approve
[14042] finance.payment.execute
[14043] finance.payment.reverse
[14045] 11.21 ABAC
[14046] Attribute-Based Access Control can evaluate:
[14047] User attributes
[14048] department
[14049] job function
[14050] organization
[14051] employment status
[14052] risk level
[14053] Resource attributes
[14054] sensitivity
[14055] owner
[14056] organization
[14057] claim value
[14058] provider
[14059] member
[14060] Environment attributes
[14061] device trust
[14062] location
[14063] time
[14064] network
[14065] authentication strength
[14066] Example:
[14067] User:
[14068] Claims Officer
[14070] Resource:
[14071] High-value claim
[14073] Amount:
[14074] Above approval threshold
[14076] Authentication:
[14077] Normal session
[14079] Decision:
[14080] Additional approval / step-up required
[14082] 11.22 Tenant Isolation
[14083] The platform must isolate:
[14084] HMO internal data
[14085] employer data
[14086] provider data
[14087] member data
[14088] A user belonging to Employer A must not access Employer B's data unless explicitly authorized.
[14089] Tenant isolation must exist at:
[14090] API
[14091] service
[14092] query
[14093] database
[14094] cache
[14095] search
[14096] document
[14097] report
[14098] export
[14099] event
[14100] analytics
[14102] 11.23 Cross-Tenant Attack Protection
[14103] Every request involving tenant-specific data must validate:
[14104] Authenticated User
[14105] ↓
[14106] User Organization
[14107] ↓
[14108] Requested Tenant
[14109] ↓
[14110] Allowed Scope?
[14111] ↓
[14112] Resource Belongs to Tenant?
[14113] If the answer is uncertain:
[14114] DENY.
[14116] 11.24 IDOR Protection
[14117] The platform must prevent attacks where a user changes:
[14118] /member/123
[14119] to:
[14120] /member/124
[14121] and gains access to another member.
[14122] Object IDs are not authorization.
[14123] The server must verify ownership and scope for every resource.
[14125] 11.25 Mass Assignment Protection
[14126] The API must not blindly accept fields supplied by clients.
[14127] Unsafe example:
[14128] {
[14129] "name": "John",
[14130] "role": "SYSTEM_ADMIN"
[14131] }
[14132] The API must explicitly define writable fields.
[14133] Sensitive fields such as:
[14134] role
[14135] approval status
[14136] account status
[14137] financial status
[14138] ownership
[14139] security state
[14140] must have dedicated controlled operations.
[14142] 11.26 Privileged Access Management
[14143] Privileged access should be tightly controlled.
[14144] Privileged roles include:
[14145] System Administrator
[14146] Security Administrator
[14147] Database Administrator
[14148] Cloud Administrator
[14149] Identity Administrator
[14150] Privileged users should receive:
[14151] MFA
[14152] stronger authentication
[14153] limited sessions
[14154] additional logging
[14155] privileged approval where appropriate
[14156] periodic access review
[14158] 11.27 Just-in-Time Access
[14159] Highly privileged access should be temporary where possible.
[14160] Example:
[14161] Administrator
[14162] ↓
[14163] Access Request
[14164] ↓
[14165] Reason
[14166] ↓
[14167] Approval
[14168] ↓
[14169] Temporary Privilege
[14170] ↓
[14171] Expiration
[14172] ↓
[14173] Privilege Removed
[14175] 11.28 Break-Glass Access
[14176] Emergency access is necessary in some healthcare environments.
[14177] However, it must be highly controlled.
[14178] Break-glass access requires:
[14179] explicit action
[14180] reason
[14181] MFA
[14182] elevated audit logging
[14183] limited duration
[14184] restricted scope
[14185] post-event review
[14186] Example:
[14187] Emergency Medical Situation
[14188] ↓
[14189] Break-Glass Request
[14190] ↓
[14191] Identity Verified
[14192] ↓
[14193] Reason Required
[14194] ↓
[14195] Temporary Access
[14196] ↓
[14197] Enhanced Monitoring
[14198] ↓
[14199] Automatic Expiry
[14200] ↓
[14201] Review
[14202] Break-glass must never become a shortcut around normal permissions.
[14204] 11.29 Security Event Architecture
[14205] Security events should include:
[14206] event ID
[14207] timestamp
[14208] actor
[14209] organization
[14210] IP metadata where appropriate
[14211] device
[14212] session
[14213] action
[14214] resource
[14215] result
[14216] reason
[14217] risk score if applicable
[14218] correlation ID
[14219] Examples:
[14220] LOGIN_FAILED
[14221] LOGIN_SUCCESS
[14222] MFA_FAILED
[14223] MFA_RESET
[14224] ROLE_ASSIGNED
[14225] ROLE_REMOVED
[14226] PERMISSION_CHANGED
[14227] PRIVILEGE_ESCALATED
[14228] BREAK_GLASS_USED
[14229] EXPORT_CREATED
[14230] EXPORT_DOWNLOADED
[14231] SESSION_REVOKED
[14232] ACCOUNT_LOCKED
[14233] SUSPICIOUS_ACCESS
[14235] 11.30 Security Event Severity
[14236] Events may be classified as:
[14237] Informational
[14238] Normal security activity.
[14239] Low
[14240] Minor suspicious activity.
[14241] Medium
[14242] Potential security issue requiring investigation.
[14243] High
[14244] Likely security incident or serious policy violation.
[14245] Critical
[14246] Potential compromise of highly sensitive systems or data.
[14248] 11.31 Security Monitoring
[14249] Security monitoring should identify:
[14250] repeated failed logins
[14251] abnormal login patterns
[14252] privilege escalation
[14253] unauthorized exports
[14254] unusual data access
[14255] mass record access
[14256] repeated denied requests
[14257] suspicious API behavior
[14258] unusual administrative activity
[14259] unexpected configuration changes
[14261] 11.32 Security Information & Event Management
[14262] A centralized security monitoring platform should aggregate:
[14263] Application Logs
[14264] +
[14265] Authentication Events
[14266] +
[14267] Authorization Events
[14268] +
[14269] Infrastructure Logs
[14270] +
[14271] Network Events
[14272] +
[14273] Database Security Events
[14274] +
[14275] Endpoint Signals
[14276] +
[14277] Cloud Security Events
[14278] This enables correlation across systems.
[14280] 11.33 Example Detection Rule
[14281] Potential mass data access:
[14282] User accesses:
[14283] 5 members → normal
[14285] User accesses:
[14286] 500 members in 10 minutes → investigate
[14288] User accesses:
[14289] 10,000 member records in 10 minutes → high-risk alert
[14290] Thresholds must be tuned using actual business behaviour to reduce false positives.
[14292] 11.34 Data Classification
[14293] Data should be classified.
[14294] PUBLIC
[14295] Information intended for public distribution.
[14296] INTERNAL
[14297] Normal internal business information.
[14298] CONFIDENTIAL
[14299] Sensitive business information.
[14300] RESTRICTED
[14301] Sensitive personal, financial or operational information.
[14302] HIGHLY RESTRICTED
[14303] Highly sensitive healthcare, identity, security or privileged information.
[14305] 11.35 Data Handling Rules
[14306] Each classification should define:
[14307] who can access
[14308] where it can be stored
[14309] encryption requirement
[14310] export rules
[14311] retention
[14312] sharing rules
[14313] logging requirement
[14315] 11.36 Encryption
[14316] Sensitive data should be encrypted:
[14317] In transit
[14318] Use modern TLS.
[14319] At rest
[14320] Use strong encryption such as AES-256 where appropriate.
[14321] Backups
[14322] Backups must also be encrypted.
[14323] Documents
[14324] Sensitive files must be encrypted in storage.
[14326] 11.37 Key Management
[14327] Encryption keys must be centrally managed.
[14328] Requirements:
[14329] secure key storage
[14330] rotation
[14331] access control
[14332] audit
[14333] separation of duties
[14334] revocation
[14335] versioning
[14336] Keys should not be hardcoded into applications.
[14338] 11.38 Field-Level Protection
[14339] Some highly sensitive fields may require additional protection.
[14340] Examples:
[14341] national identifiers
[14342] bank account information
[14343] sensitive healthcare information
[14344] authentication recovery data
[14345] Possible techniques:
[14346] encryption
[14347] tokenization
[14348] masking
[14349] hashing where appropriate
[14351] 11.39 Data Masking
[14352] Different roles may see different representations.
[14353] Example:
[14354] Finance Officer:
[14355] **** **** 1234
[14357] Customer Service:
[14358] Limited financial information
[14360] Security Administrator:
[14361] Access only when specifically authorized
[14362] Technical administrators should not automatically receive plaintext access to sensitive business data.
[14364] 11.40 Healthcare Data Protection
[14365] Medical information requires additional controls.
[14366] Access should be:
[14367] role-based
[14368] purpose-aware where appropriate
[14369] scope-controlled
[14370] logged
[14371] monitored
[14372] A customer service agent may need:
[14373] Eligibility:
[14374] YES
[14375] without needing access to:
[14376] Detailed clinical history
[14378] 11.41 Data Minimization
[14379] The system should only expose the information required for the task.
[14380] For example:
[14381] A provider verifying eligibility may need:
[14382] member identifier
[14383] coverage status
[14384] plan
[14385] relevant benefit information
[14386] but not necessarily the member's complete medical history.
[14388] 11.42 Secure Document Access
[14389] Documents must be accessed through authorization-controlled workflows.
[14390] Example:
[14391] User
[14392] ↓
[14393] Document Request
[14394] ↓
[14395] Authorization
[14396] ↓
[14397] Scope Check
[14398] ↓
[14399] Classification Check
[14400] ↓
[14401] Purpose / Business Rule
[14402] ↓
[14403] Temporary Access
[14404] ↓
[14405] Audit
[14406] ↓
[14407] Document Delivery
[14409] 11.43 Secure File Upload
[14410] Every uploaded file should pass through:
[14411] Upload
[14412] ↓
[14413] Size Validation
[14414] ↓
[14415] Extension Validation
[14416] ↓
[14417] MIME Validation
[14418] ↓
[14419] Malware Scan
[14420] ↓
[14421] Content Validation
[14422] ↓
[14423] Metadata Processing
[14424] ↓
[14425] Encrypted Storage
[14426] Potentially dangerous file types should be restricted.
[14428] 11.44 Secure File Download
[14429] Downloads should support:
[14430] authorization check
[14431] temporary URLs/tokens
[14432] expiry
[14433] download audit
[14434] rate limiting
[14435] classification restrictions
[14437] 11.45 Application Security
[14438] The application must protect against common threats including:
[14439] injection
[14440] broken access control
[14441] authentication failures
[14442] insecure direct object references
[14443] cross-site scripting
[14444] CSRF where applicable
[14445] insecure deserialization
[14446] SSRF
[14447] file upload attacks
[14448] security misconfiguration
[14449] vulnerable dependencies
[14450] excessive API access
[14451] business logic abuse
[14453] 11.46 Input Validation
[14454] All external inputs must be validated.
[14455] Inputs include:
[14456] JSON
[14457] query parameters
[14458] path parameters
[14459] file uploads
[14460] headers
[14461] webhook payloads
[14462] bulk imports
[14463] API requests
[14464] Never trust client-side validation alone.
[14466] 11.47 Output Encoding
[14467] User-controlled content must be safely encoded before being rendered.
[14468] This is especially important for:
[14469] comments
[14470] notes
[14471] complaint messages
[14472] provider descriptions
[14473] employer names
[14474] member names
[14475] uploaded metadata
[14477] 11.48 API Rate Limiting
[14478] Rate limits should apply based on:
[14479] IP
[14480] user
[14481] API key
[14482] tenant
[14483] endpoint
[14484] risk level
[14485] Sensitive endpoints should have stricter controls.
[14487] 11.49 Abuse Prevention
[14488] Potential abuse patterns include:
[14489] mass member lookup
[14490] repeated eligibility requests
[14491] automated scraping
[14492] repeated OTP requests
[14493] bulk claim submissions
[14494] excessive exports
[14495] API credential abuse
[14496] Controls include:
[14497] rate limiting
[14498] anomaly detection
[14499] quotas
[14500] throttling
[14501] temporary restrictions
[14502] security alerts
[14504] 11.50 Fraud and Security Relationship
[14505] Fraud and cybersecurity overlap but are not identical.
[14506] Security
[14507] Focuses on:
[14508] Is the system or account being accessed or manipulated improperly?
[14509] Fraud
[14510] Focuses on:
[14511] Is a business transaction potentially being manipulated for improper financial or operational benefit?
[14512] Signals may be shared between both systems.
[14514] 11.51 Fraud Risk Signals
[14515] Examples:
[14516] unusual claim frequency
[14517] unusual provider billing
[14518] repeated identical claims
[14519] unusual refund patterns
[14520] split transactions
[14521] unusual provider bank account changes
[14522] abnormal approval patterns
[14523] sudden benefit utilization
[14524] suspicious member-provider relationships
[14525] These should be treated as risk signals for review, not automatic conclusions of wrongdoing.
[14527] 11.52 Secure Development Lifecycle
[14528] Every software change should pass through:
[14529] Requirement
[14530] ↓
[14531] Threat Assessment
[14532] ↓
[14533] Architecture Review
[14534] ↓
[14535] Secure Design
[14536] ↓
[14537] Development
[14538] ↓
[14539] Code Review
[14540] ↓
[14541] Automated Security Testing
[14542] ↓
[14543] Integration Testing
[14544] ↓
[14545] Security Testing
[14546] ↓
[14547] Deployment
[14548] ↓
[14549] Monitoring
[14551] 11.53 Secure Coding Standards
[14552] Development teams should follow standards covering:
[14553] authentication
[14554] authorization
[14555] input validation
[14556] secrets
[14557] cryptography
[14558] database access
[14559] API security
[14560] logging
[14561] error handling
[14562] file handling
[14564] 11.54 Dependency Security
[14565] All third-party dependencies should be monitored.
[14566] Requirements:
[14567] dependency inventory
[14568] vulnerability scanning
[14569] patching
[14570] version management
[14571] license review
[14572] emergency patch process
[14574] 11.55 Container Security
[14575] If containers are used:
[14576] minimal images
[14577] non-root execution
[14578] image scanning
[14579] signed images where appropriate
[14580] secrets outside images
[14581] read-only filesystem where practical
[14582] resource limits
[14583] runtime monitoring
[14585] 11.56 Infrastructure Security
[14586] Infrastructure must use:
[14587] private networks
[14588] security groups/firewalls
[14589] least-privilege IAM
[14590] network segmentation
[14591] secure secrets
[14592] encryption
[14593] vulnerability management
[14594] patching
[14595] logging
[14597] 11.57 Cloud IAM
[14598] Cloud permissions must follow least privilege.
[14599] Avoid:
[14600] AdministratorAccess
[14601] for ordinary application workloads.
[14602] Use narrowly defined permissions such as:
[14603] Read specific bucket
[14604] Write specific queue
[14605] Read specific secret
[14606] Write specific logs
[14608] 11.58 Service Account Security
[14609] Service accounts must:
[14610] have unique identities
[14611] have limited permissions
[14612] use short-lived credentials where possible
[14613] rotate credentials
[14614] be monitored
[14615] never be shared between unrelated applications
[14617] 11.59 Database Security
[14618] Database security should include:
[14619] encryption
[14620] restricted network access
[14621] strong authentication
[14622] least privilege
[14623] audit logging
[14624] backup protection
[14625] query monitoring where appropriate
[14626] separation of application and administrative accounts
[14628] 11.60 Database Administrative Access
[14629] Database administrators should not automatically have unrestricted business access.
[14630] Technical access and business authorization should remain separate.
[14631] Where highly sensitive access is necessary:
[14632] Request
[14633] ↓
[14634] Reason
[14635] ↓
[14636] Approval
[14637] ↓
[14638] Temporary Access
[14639] ↓
[14640] Audit
[14641] ↓
[14642] Expiry
[14644] 11.61 Secrets in Source Code
[14645] The following must never be committed to source control:
[14646] passwords
[14647] API keys
[14648] private keys
[14649] database credentials
[14650] cloud credentials
[14651] encryption secrets
[14652] production tokens
[14653] Automated secret scanning should be part of CI/CD.
[14655] 11.62 Incident Response Architecture
[14656] The platform should have a formal incident response process.
[14657] DETECT
[14658] ↓
[14659] TRIAGE
[14660] ↓
[14661] CONTAIN
[14662] ↓
[14663] INVESTIGATE
[14664] ↓
[14665] ERADICATE
[14666] ↓
[14667] RECOVER
[14668] ↓
[14669] VALIDATE
[14670] ↓
[14671] LESSONS LEARNED
[14673] 11.63 Incident Severity
[14674] P1 — Critical
[14675] Examples:
[14676] confirmed major data breach
[14677] widespread account compromise
[14678] production-wide security compromise
[14679] major financial security incident
[14680] P2 — High
[14681] Examples:
[14682] privileged account compromise
[14683] significant unauthorized access
[14684] serious application vulnerability
[14685] P3 — Medium
[14686] Examples:
[14687] limited unauthorized access
[14688] suspicious activity requiring investigation
[14689] P4 — Low
[14690] Examples:
[14691] minor security configuration issue
[14692] informational security event
[14694] 11.64 Incident Containment
[14695] Possible containment actions:
[14696] revoke sessions
[14697] disable account
[14698] disable API credential
[14699] block IP/risk source
[14700] disable compromised integration
[14701] rotate secrets
[14702] revoke certificates
[14703] isolate service
[14704] temporarily disable functionality
[14705] Containment must preserve evidence.
[14707] 11.65 Evidence Preservation
[14708] Security investigations should preserve:
[14709] audit events
[14710] security events
[14711] relevant application logs
[14712] timestamps
[14713] request IDs
[14714] correlation IDs
[14715] affected identities
[14716] affected resources
[14717] system state where appropriate
[14718] Logs required for investigations should have appropriate protection against unauthorized alteration.
[14720] 11.66 Security Incident Example
[14721] Suppose a privileged account is compromised.
[14722] Suspicious Login
[14723] ↓
[14724] Risk Detection
[14725] ↓
[14726] Security Alert
[14727] ↓
[14728] Session Revocation
[14729] ↓
[14730] Account Suspension
[14731] ↓
[14732] Credential Rotation
[14733] ↓
[14734] Affected Resource Identification
[14735] ↓
[14736] Investigation
[14737] ↓
[14738] Recovery
[14739] ↓
[14740] Post-Incident Review
[14742] 11.67 Vulnerability Management
[14743] Vulnerabilities should be classified by:
[14744] severity
[14745] exploitability
[14746] affected systems
[14747] data sensitivity
[14748] exposure
[14749] business impact
[14750] Critical vulnerabilities require accelerated remediation.
[14752] 11.68 Patch Management
[14753] Patch management should cover:
[14754] operating systems
[14755] containers
[14756] databases
[14757] libraries
[14758] application frameworks
[14759] cloud services
[14760] network devices
[14761] Emergency patches should have an expedited process.
[14763] 11.69 Security Configuration Management
[14764] Configuration must be continuously evaluated.
[14765] Examples:
[14766] public storage bucket
[14767] unrestricted firewall
[14768] excessive IAM permission
[14769] disabled encryption
[14770] expired certificate
[14771] weak authentication configuration
[14772] exposed database
[14773] logging disabled
[14774] Security configuration drift should generate alerts.
[14776] 11.70 Privacy Architecture
[14777] Privacy must be embedded into product design.
[14778] Principles include:
[14779] purpose limitation
[14780] data minimization
[14781] access control
[14782] retention management
[14783] transparency
[14784] controlled sharing
[14785] secure processing
[14786] appropriate correction mechanisms
[14787] The exact legal obligations should be confirmed against the HMO's applicable Nigerian and other relevant regulatory requirements before production deployment.
[14789] 11.71 Privacy by Design
[14790] For every feature ask:
[14791] What personal data is required?
[14792] Why is it required?
[14793] Who needs access?
[14794] How long should it be retained?
[14795] Can the data be minimized?
[14796] Can the field be masked?
[14797] Is consent or another legal basis relevant?
[14798] What happens when the purpose ends?
[14799] How is access audited?
[14801] 11.72 Data Retention
[14802] Retention policies should be defined by data category.
[14803] Examples:
[14804] member records
[14805] claims
[14806] financial transactions
[14807] medical documents
[14808] audit events
[14809] security events
[14810] support cases
[14811] contracts
[14812] Retention periods must be established based on applicable legal, regulatory, contractual and business requirements.
[14814] 11.73 Legal Hold
[14815] When records are subject to investigation or legal hold:
[14816] Normal Retention
[14817] ↓
[14818] Legal Hold
[14819] ↓
[14820] Deletion Suspended
[14821] ↓
[14822] Investigation
[14823] ↓
[14824] Hold Released
[14825] ↓
[14826] Normal Retention Resumes
[14828] 11.74 Data Correction
[14829] Correction of inaccurate personal data must not silently destroy historical truth.
[14830] The system should preserve:
[14831] Original Value
[14832] ↓
[14833] Correction Request
[14834] ↓
[14835] Authorized Change
[14836] ↓
[14837] New Value
[14838] ↓
[14839] Audit History
[14841] 11.75 Data Deletion
[14842] Deletion must distinguish between:
[14843] active operational records
[14844] historical records
[14845] legally retained records
[14846] audit records
[14847] financial records
[14848] healthcare records
[14849] Where deletion is restricted, the system may use controlled anonymization or archival strategies subject to applicable requirements.
[14851] 11.76 Privacy Access Requests
[14852] Where applicable, the platform should support controlled workflows for requests involving:
[14853] access
[14854] correction
[14855] restriction
[14856] deletion
[14857] data export
[14858] other applicable privacy rights
[14859] The exact rights and process must be aligned with applicable law and organizational policy.
[14861] 11.77 Third-Party Security
[14862] External vendors must be evaluated according to risk.
[14863] Assessment may include:
[14864] security controls
[14865] data handling
[14866] encryption
[14867] authentication
[14868] incident response
[14869] availability
[14870] subcontractors
[14871] data location
[14872] retention
[14873] contractual obligations
[14875] 11.78 Third-Party Credential Management
[14876] Every external credential must have:
[14877] owner
[14878] purpose
[14879] creation date
[14880] expiration
[14881] rotation policy
[14882] access scope
[14883] last-used timestamp
[14884] emergency revocation mechanism
[14886] 11.79 Security Governance
[14887] Security governance should include representatives from:
[14888] executive management
[14889] information security
[14890] IT
[14891] compliance
[14892] legal/privacy
[14893] finance
[14894] healthcare/medical operations
[14895] claims
[14896] operations
[14898] 11.80 Security Roles
[14899] Suggested security responsibilities:
[14900] CISO / Security Lead
[14901] Overall security strategy.
[14902] Security Operations
[14903] Monitoring and incident response.
[14904] IAM Administrator
[14905] Identity and access administration.
[14906] Application Security
[14907] Secure development and testing.
[14908] Infrastructure Security
[14909] Cloud/network/infrastructure protection.
[14910] Privacy Officer / Responsible Function
[14911] Privacy governance.
[14912] Compliance
[14913] Regulatory and policy oversight.
[14914] Internal Audit
[14915] Independent assurance.
[14917] 11.81 Access Certification
[14918] Privileged and sensitive access should be periodically reviewed.
[14919] Review:
[14920] User
[14921] ↓
[14922] Role
[14923] ↓
[14924] Permissions
[14925] ↓
[14926] Scope
[14927] ↓
[14928] Last Used
[14929] ↓
[14930] Business Need
[14931] ↓
[14932] Manager Approval
[14933] ↓
[14934] Security Review
[14935] Unused privileges should be removed.
[14937] 11.82 Dormant Accounts
[14938] Accounts inactive beyond defined thresholds should be:
[14939] flagged
[14940] reviewed
[14941] suspended where policy requires
[14942] eventually deactivated
[14943] Exceptions must be documented.
[14945] 11.83 Security Edge-Case Matrix
[14947] 11.84 Unknown Security Edge Cases
[14948] Unknown threats are inevitable.
[14949] Therefore the system must follow:
[14950] UNKNOWN CONDITION
[14951] ↓
[14952] IS ACCESS SAFE?
[14953] ↓
[14954] NO / UNKNOWN
[14955] ↓
[14956] DENY OR RESTRICT
[14957] ↓
[14958] PRESERVE EVIDENCE
[14959] ↓
[14960] GENERATE SECURITY EVENT
[14961] ↓
[14962] ALERT
[14963] ↓
[14964] INVESTIGATE
[14965] ↓
[14966] RECOVER
[14967] ↓
[14968] UPDATE SECURITY CONTROL
[14969] The architecture must prefer:
[14970] False denial over unauthorized disclosure or unsafe privileged action.
[14971] However, this principle must be balanced with availability requirements for legitimate healthcare emergencies through controlled mechanisms such as break-glass access.
[14973] 11.85 Security Testing Matrix
[14974] The following must be tested regularly.
[14975] Authentication
[14976] password attacks
[14977] MFA bypass
[14978] session fixation
[14979] token theft
[14980] recovery bypass
[14981] Authorization
[14982] IDOR
[14983] privilege escalation
[14984] cross-tenant access
[14985] role bypass
[14986] scope bypass
[14987] approval bypass
[14988] API
[14989] injection
[14990] rate-limit bypass
[14991] malformed requests
[14992] mass assignment
[14993] replay attacks
[14994] Documents
[14995] malicious uploads
[14996] unauthorized downloads
[14997] metadata manipulation
[14998] path traversal
[14999] Infrastructure
[15000] exposed ports
[15001] weak IAM
[15002] public storage
[15003] vulnerable packages
[15004] configuration drift
[15006] 11.86 Security Metrics
[15007] Management dashboards should track:
[15008] failed login attempts
[15009] MFA failures
[15010] privileged access events
[15011] authorization denials
[15012] security incidents
[15013] critical vulnerabilities
[15014] patch compliance
[15015] dormant accounts
[15016] excessive permissions
[15017] security alerts
[15018] suspicious exports
[15019] security response time
[15020] incident recovery time
[15022] 11.87 Security KPIs
[15023] Examples:
[15024] Mean Time to Detect
[15025] How quickly security issues are identified.
[15026] Mean Time to Respond
[15027] How quickly incidents receive a response.
[15028] Mean Time to Contain
[15029] How quickly an active incident is contained.
[15030] Critical Vulnerability Remediation
[15031] Percentage remediated within defined SLA.
[15032] Privileged Access Review
[15033] Percentage of privileged accounts reviewed on schedule.
[15034] MFA Coverage
[15035] Percentage of required users protected by MFA.
[15037] 11.88 Security Architecture Golden Rules
[15038] The HMO platform should enforce these rules:
[15039] Rule 1
[15040] Never trust the client.
[15041] Rule 2
[15042] Never authorize based solely on UI visibility.
[15043] Rule 3
[15044] Never trust an object ID as proof of ownership.
[15045] Rule 4
[15046] Never give administrators automatic business privileges.
[15047] Rule 5
[15048] Never store plaintext passwords or secrets.
[15049] Rule 6
[15050] Never silently modify historical financial or healthcare records.
[15051] Rule 7
[15052] Never expose more data than required.
[15053] Rule 8
[15054] Never allow unknown authorization states to become ALLOW.
[15055] Rule 9
[15056] Never allow emergency access without accountability.
[15057] Rule 10
[15058] Never assume internal networks are trusted.
[15060] 11.89 Security Architecture Completion Criteria
[15061] Part XI is complete when the HMO platform has defined:
[15062] identity security
[15063] authentication
[15064] MFA
[15065] session management
[15066] device management
[15067] RBAC
[15068] ABAC
[15069] SoD
[15070] privileged access
[15071] break-glass access
[15072] tenant isolation
[15073] data classification
[15074] encryption
[15075] key management
[15076] data masking
[15077] secure documents
[15078] application security
[15079] API security
[15080] infrastructure security
[15081] vulnerability management
[15082] security monitoring
[15083] security events
[15084] incident response
[15085] privacy architecture
[15086] retention
[15087] legal hold
[15088] third-party security
[15089] access certification
[15090] security testing
[15091] security metrics
[15092] security edge cases
[15093] unknown threat handling

[15096] The security architecture of the HMO platform can be summarized as:
[15097] ZERO TRUST

[15116] │

[15132] INCIDENT RESPONSE
[15133] The security architecture must make unauthorized access difficult, detect suspicious activity quickly, limit the impact of compromise, preserve evidence, and provide controlled recovery.
[15134] Most importantly, roles and privileges remain a first-class architectural concern rather than merely an administrative feature.
[15135] The next part of the Complete HMO Master Blueprint should move into Part XII — Reporting, Business Intelligence, Analytics, Dashboards & Decision Intelligence, covering executive dashboards, operational reporting, claims analytics, utilization analytics, provider performance, employer analytics, financial intelligence, fraud/risk analytics, data warehouse architecture, KPI definitions, scheduled reports, export controls, and role-based analytics access.
[15136] COMPLETE HMO MASTER BLUEPRINT
[15137] PART XII — REPORTING, BUSINESS INTELLIGENCE, ANALYTICS & DECISION INTELLIGENCE
[15139] 12.0 Purpose
[15140] Part XII defines the reporting, analytics and decision-intelligence architecture of the enterprise HMO platform.
[15141] The objective is to transform operational data into:
[15142] Executive intelligence
[15143] Operational visibility
[15144] Financial intelligence
[15145] Claims intelligence
[15146] Healthcare utilization insights
[15147] Provider performance insights
[15148] Employer insights
[15149] Member experience insights
[15150] Risk indicators
[15151] Fraud signals
[15152] Workforce intelligence
[15153] Compliance reporting
[15154] Strategic decision support
[15155] The analytics platform must answer four fundamental questions:
[15156] What happened?
[15157] Why did it happen?
[15158] What is happening now?
[15159] What requires attention?
[15160] Where sufficient quality and validated models exist, the system may also support:
[15161] What could happen next?
[15162] The platform must distinguish clearly between:
[15163] factual metrics
[15164] calculated indicators
[15165] statistical predictions
[15166] risk signals
[15167] human decisions
[15168] Analytics must inform decision-making without silently replacing authorized business or clinical judgment.
[15170] 12.1 Analytics Principles
[15171] The analytics architecture shall follow:
[15172] Single source of truth
[15173] Consistent KPI definitions
[15174] Data lineage
[15175] Role-based access
[15176] Tenant isolation
[15177] Minimum necessary data
[15178] Historical accuracy
[15179] Reproducible calculations
[15180] Versioned business rules
[15181] Explainable analytics
[15182] Secure exports
[15183] Data quality monitoring
[15184] Separation of operational and analytical workloads
[15185] Human oversight for high-impact decisions
[15187] 12.2 Analytics Architecture
[15188] High-level architecture:
[15189] OPERATIONAL SYSTEMS
[15190] │
[15191] ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐
[15192] │                │                │
[15193] ▼                ▼                ▼
[15194] Members          Claims           Finance
[15195] Providers        Healthcare       Employers
[15196] Enrollment       Customer         Identity
[15197] │                │                │
[15198] └────────────────┼────────────────┘
[15199] ▼
[15200] EVENT / ETL LAYER
[15201] │
[15202] ▼
[15203] DATA WAREHOUSE / LAKE
[15204] │
[15205] ┌───────────┼───────────┐
[15206] ▼           ▼           ▼
[15207] KPI Engine   Analytics   ML/Risk
[15208] │           │           │
[15209] └───────────┼───────────┘
[15210] ▼
[15211] SEMANTIC LAYER
[15212] │
[15213] ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐
[15214] ▼               ▼               ▼
[15215] Dashboards        Reports         Alerts
[15216] │               │               │
[15217] └───────────────┼───────────────┘
[15218] ▼
[15219] DECISION SUPPORT
[15221] 12.3 Operational vs Analytical Systems
[15222] Operational databases should support:
[15223] transactions
[15224] workflows
[15225] eligibility
[15226] claims
[15227] payments
[15228] member management
[15229] Analytics systems should support:
[15230] aggregation
[15231] historical analysis
[15232] trends
[15233] dashboards
[15234] reporting
[15235] forecasting
[15236] large analytical queries
[15237] Large analytical queries must not unnecessarily consume resources from transactional systems.
[15239] 12.4 Data Warehouse
[15240] The HMO should maintain a centralized analytical environment.
[15241] Possible architecture:
[15242] Operational Databases
[15243] ↓
[15244] CDC / Events / ETL
[15245] ↓
[15246] Raw Data Layer
[15247] ↓
[15248] Validated Data Layer
[15249] ↓
[15250] Curated Data Warehouse
[15251] ↓
[15252] Semantic / KPI Layer
[15253] ↓
[15254] Dashboards & Reports
[15255] The specific technology may be selected based on:
[15256] expected data volume
[15257] cost
[15258] team capability
[15259] reporting requirements
[15260] cloud environment
[15262] 12.5 Data Layers
[15263] Raw Layer
[15264] Stores source data with minimal transformation.
[15265] Validated Layer
[15266] Data is checked for:
[15267] format
[15268] completeness
[15269] duplicates
[15270] referential integrity
[15271] source consistency
[15272] Curated Layer
[15273] Business-ready datasets.
[15274] Semantic Layer
[15275] Defines consistent business metrics.
[15277] 12.6 Data Lineage
[15278] Every important KPI should be traceable.
[15279] Example:
[15280] Executive Dashboard
[15281] ↓
[15282] Claims Cost KPI
[15283] ↓
[15284] Claims Fact Table
[15285] ↓
[15286] Claim Line Data
[15287] ↓
[15288] Operational Claim
[15289] ↓
[15290] Original Provider Submission
[15291] A user should be able to understand where an important number originated.
[15293] 12.7 KPI Governance
[15294] A KPI must have a formal definition.
[15295] Example:
[15296] Claims Approval Rate
[15297] Approved Claims
[15298] ------------------------
[15299] Eligible Adjudicated Claims
[15300] The system must define:
[15301] numerator
[15302] denominator
[15303] exclusions
[15304] time period
[15305] data source
[15306] calculation version
[15307] owner
[15309] 12.8 KPI Dictionary
[15310] The platform should maintain a centralized KPI dictionary.
[15311] Example:
[15312] Definitions must be version-controlled.
[15314] 12.9 Executive Dashboard

[15316] Possible sections:
[15317] Membership

[15335] claims value
[15336] pending claims
[15337] approved claims
[15338] rejected claims
[15339] claims aging
[15340] Finance
[15341] premium billed

[15343] outstanding receivables

[15363] 12.10 Executive Dashboard Access
[15364] Executive dashboards must still obey authorization.
[15365] An executive should not automatically receive unrestricted access to:
[15366] individual medical records
[15367] individual claims
[15368] employee HR records
[15369] sensitive security information
[15370] Executive access should normally emphasize aggregated information unless specific detailed access is authorized.
[15372] 12.11 Role-Based Dashboards
[15373] Different roles should receive different dashboards.
[15374] Claims Officer
[15375] Pending Claims
[15376] Assigned Claims
[15377] SLA
[15378] Validation Errors
[15379] Review Queue
[15380] Claims Manager

[15382] Team Productivity
[15383] Approval Rate
[15384] Rejection Rate
[15385] Aging
[15386] High-Risk Claims

[15390] Settlements

[15392] Reconciliation
[15393] Outstanding Liabilities

[15395] Utilization
[15396] Preauthorization
[15397] High-Cost Services
[15398] Provider Patterns
[15399] Clinical Review Queue
[15400] Customer Service Manager
[15401] Open Cases
[15402] SLA Breaches
[15403] Complaint Trends
[15404] Escalations
[15405] Resolution Time
[15407] 12.12 Scope-Aware Analytics
[15408] A user's analytics scope must follow their authorization scope.
[15409] Example:
[15410] A regional manager may see:
[15411] Region A
[15412] but not:
[15413] Region B
[15414] unless explicitly authorized.
[15415] A provider manager may see:
[15416] Authorized Provider Network
[15417] but not unrelated providers.
[15419] 12.13 Analytics Tenant Isolation
[15420] Analytics must enforce the same tenant boundaries as operational systems.
[15421] This applies to:
[15422] dashboards
[15423] reports
[15424] exports
[15425] data warehouse queries
[15426] APIs
[15427] scheduled reports
[15428] BI tools
[15429] ad hoc queries
[15430] A reporting system must never become a backdoor around application authorization.
[15432] 12.14 Claims Analytics
[15433] Claims analytics should include:
[15434] Volume
[15435] claims per day
[15436] claims per provider
[15437] claims per employer
[15438] claims per plan
[15439] Financial
[15440] submitted value
[15441] approved value
[15442] rejected value
[15443] provider liability
[15444] HMO liability
[15445] member responsibility
[15446] Operational
[15447] processing time
[15448] queue aging
[15449] correction rate
[15450] resubmission rate
[15451] dispute rate
[15452] Quality
[15453] incomplete claims
[15454] duplicate claims
[15455] documentation issues
[15456] coding errors
[15458] 12.15 Claims Aging
[15459] Claims may be grouped by age:
[15460] 0–7 days
[15461] 8–14 days
[15462] 15–30 days
[15463] 31–60 days
[15464] 61–90 days
[15465] 90+ days
[15466] Exact SLA categories should be configurable.
[15468] 12.16 Claims Cost Analytics
[15469] The system should support analysis by:
[15470] employer
[15471] provider
[15472] plan
[15473] service type
[15474] geography
[15475] member population
[15476] age bands where permitted
[15477] claim type
[15478] treatment category
[15479] time period
[15480] Sensitive demographic analytics must be subject to applicable privacy and governance controls.
[15482] 12.17 Provider Analytics
[15483] Provider performance dashboards may include:

[15495] Risk indicators should be presented as signals requiring appropriate review, not as automatic conclusions.
[15497] 12.18 Provider Network Analytics

[15499] provider distribution

[15506] High Member Demand
[15507] +
[15508] Low Provider Availability
[15509] ↓
[15510] Network Coverage Gap
[15512] 12.19 Utilization Analytics

[15514] outpatient visits
[15515] inpatient admissions
[15516] procedures

[15518] imaging
[15519] pharmacy
[15520] specialist referrals
[15521] emergency services
[15522] high-frequency services
[15524] 12.20 Benefit Utilization
[15525] For each plan:
[15526] Benefit Limit
[15527] ↓
[15528] Used
[15529] ↓
[15530] Reserved
[15531] ↓
[15532] Remaining
[15533] Analytics should show:
[15534] utilization percentage
[15535] remaining benefit
[15536] utilization trends
[15537] members approaching limits
[15538] employer-level utilization
[15540] 12.21 High-Cost Service Analytics
[15541] The system should identify high-cost categories.
[15542] Examples:
[15543] major surgery
[15544] prolonged inpatient care
[15545] advanced diagnostics
[15546] specialized treatment
[15547] high-cost medications
[15548] These are analytical categories, not automatically abnormal activities.
[15550] 12.22 Preauthorization Analytics
[15551] Track:
[15552] authorization requests

[15555] partial approval rate

[15559] requests by service
[15560] requests by plan
[15562] 12.23 Medical Review Analytics
[15563] Metrics may include:
[15564] cases reviewed

[15566] escalations
[15567] clinical criteria exceptions
[15568] reconsiderations
[15569] workload by reviewer
[15570] Sensitive clinical details must be restricted according to role and purpose.
[15572] 12.24 Employer Analytics
[15573] Employer dashboards may include:
[15574] enrolled employees
[15575] dependants
[15576] active members
[15577] utilization
[15578] claims volume
[15579] claims value
[15580] employee additions/removals
[15581] renewal status
[15582] outstanding invoices
[15583] payment history
[15584] Employer users must only access their own organization.
[15586] 12.25 Employer Benchmarking
[15587] Benchmarking may compare employer-level metrics against appropriate aggregated groups.
[15588] Example:
[15589] Employer Utilization
[15590] ↓
[15591] Aggregated Peer Benchmark
[15592] ↓
[15593] Variance
[15594] Small populations should be handled carefully to reduce the risk of identifying individuals.
[15596] 12.26 Member Analytics
[15597] Member analytics may include:
[15598] enrollment trends
[15599] service usage
[15600] benefit utilization
[15601] support interactions
[15602] claims activity
[15603] Individual-level analytics must remain subject to strict access controls.
[15605] 12.27 Customer Experience Analytics
[15606] Track:
[15607] cases opened

[15609] average resolution time
[15610] SLA compliance
[15611] complaints
[15612] escalations
[15613] reopened cases
[15614] contact channels
[15615] recurring complaint categories
[15617] 12.28 Customer Complaint Intelligence

[15619] Complaint Category
[15620] ↓
[15621] Frequency
[15622] ↓
[15623] Trend
[15624] ↓

[15626] ↓
[15627] Affected Provider / Region
[15628] ↓
[15629] Management Action
[15630] The system should not automatically infer misconduct from complaint volume alone.

[15677] Forecasts must clearly identify:

[15680] model version
[15681] confidence/uncertainty
[15682] source data
[15683] A forecast must not be presented as a guaranteed outcome.
[15685] 12.32 Risk Analytics
[15686] Risk analytics can identify patterns requiring investigation.
[15687] Examples:
[15688] unusual provider billing
[15689] unusual claims frequency
[15690] repeated claim corrections
[15691] unusual payment patterns
[15692] unusual refund activity
[15693] unusual account activity
[15694] Risk analytics should produce:
[15695] Signal
[15696] +
[15697] Evidence
[15698] +
[15699] Confidence / Severity
[15700] +
[15701] Recommended Review Queue
[15702] rather than silently making irreversible decisions.
[15704] 12.33 Fraud Analytics
[15705] Potential fraud signals may include:
[15706] Provider
[15707] unusual billing volume
[15708] abnormal service combinations
[15709] sudden activity increase
[15710] unusual claim timing
[15711] Member
[15712] repeated similar claims
[15713] unusual service frequency
[15714] unusual provider patterns
[15715] Financial
[15716] unusual refunds
[15717] repeated adjustments
[15718] split transactions
[15719] bank account changes
[15720] These signals must be reviewed using appropriate investigation processes.
[15722] 12.34 Risk Scoring
[15723] A risk engine may calculate a score.
[15724] Example:
[15725] Risk Score = 0–100
[15726] But the score must be accompanied by:
[15727] contributing signals
[15728] rule/model version
[15729] timestamp
[15730] source data
[15731] decision threshold
[15732] A score without explanation should not automatically determine a high-impact outcome.
[15734] 12.35 Analytics Explainability
[15735] For important decisions, the system should answer:
[15736] Why was this item flagged?
[15737] Example:
[15738] Claim Flagged
[15740] Signals:
[15741] - Unusual frequency
[15742] - Amount above historical pattern
[15743] - Service combination requires review
[15745] Rule Version:
[15746] RISK-2026-04
[15748] Result:
[15749] Manual Review
[15751] 12.36 Machine Learning Governance
[15752] If ML models are introduced, each model should have:
[15753] model ID
[15754] version
[15755] owner
[15756] training data description
[15757] validation
[15758] deployment date
[15759] monitoring
[15760] drift detection
[15761] rollback capability
[15762] approval status
[15764] 12.37 Model Drift
[15765] Models can become less accurate as behaviour changes.
[15766] Monitor:
[15767] prediction distribution
[15768] input distribution
[15769] error rates
[15770] false positives
[15771] false negatives
[15772] data quality
[15773] If performance deteriorates:
[15774] Detect Drift
[15775] ↓
[15776] Alert
[15777] ↓
[15778] Review
[15779] ↓
[15780] Retrain / Rollback
[15782] 12.38 Human-in-the-Loop
[15783] High-impact decisions should support human review.
[15784] Examples:
[15785] fraud investigation
[15786] high-value claim review
[15787] clinical exception
[15788] account suspension
[15789] provider suspension
[15790] financial exception
[15791] The system should provide evidence to the reviewer.
[15793] 12.39 Reporting Engine
[15794] The reporting engine should support:
[15795] predefined reports
[15796] parameterized reports
[15797] scheduled reports
[15798] ad hoc reports for authorized users
[15799] exports
[15800] dashboards
[15802] 12.40 Standard Report Lifecycle
[15803] REPORT REQUEST
[15804] ↓
[15805] AUTHORIZATION
[15806] ↓
[15807] PARAMETER VALIDATION
[15808] ↓
[15809] DATA SCOPE
[15810] ↓
[15811] QUERY
[15812] ↓
[15813] DATA MASKING
[15814] ↓
[15815] REPORT GENERATION
[15816] ↓
[15817] AUDIT
[15818] ↓
[15819] DELIVERY
[15821] 12.41 Scheduled Reports
[15822] Reports may be scheduled:
[15823] daily
[15824] weekly
[15825] monthly
[15826] quarterly
[15827] annually
[15828] Scheduling itself requires permission.
[15829] A user should not be able to create a scheduled report containing data they are not authorized to access.
[15831] 12.42 Report Delivery
[15832] Delivery channels may include:
[15833] in-app
[15834] secure portal
[15835] email notification
[15836] secure download
[15837] controlled API
[15838] Sensitive reports should not be sent as unrestricted email attachments.
[15840] 12.43 Report Export Controls
[15841] Exports must enforce:
[15842] role
[15843] permission
[15844] data scope
[15845] classification
[15846] maximum records
[15847] field masking
[15848] expiration
[15849] audit
[15851] 12.44 Report Watermarking
[15852] Highly sensitive reports may include:
[15853] generated by
[15854] timestamp
[15855] organization
[15856] report ID
[15857] classification
[15858] confidentiality notice
[15859] This supports accountability.
[15861] 12.45 Ad Hoc Analytics
[15862] Authorized analysts may need more flexible queries.
[15863] However:
[15864] Analyst access must not bypass normal data governance.
[15865] Analysts should work against controlled analytical datasets.
[15866] Direct production database access should be restricted.
[15868] 12.46 Self-Service BI
[15869] Self-service BI can allow authorized users to:
[15870] filter
[15871] group
[15872] compare
[15873] visualize
[15874] drill down
[15875] export
[15876] But datasets should be pre-scoped.
[15877] Example:
[15878] Finance Dataset
[15879] ↓
[15880] Finance User Scope
[15881] ↓
[15882] Allowed Dimensions
[15883] ↓
[15884] Allowed Measures
[15886] 12.47 Analytics Semantic Layer
[15887] A semantic layer should standardize:
[15888] member
[15889] claim
[15890] provider
[15891] employer
[15892] plan
[15893] premium
[15894] payment
[15895] settlement
[15896] utilization
[15897] This prevents different departments from calculating the same KPI differently.
[15899] 12.48 Dashboard Refresh
[15900] Dashboards should indicate data freshness.
[15901] Example:
[15902] Last Updated:
[15903] 10:30 PM
[15905] Data Through:
[15906] 10:15 PM
[15907] Users must not assume that an analytical dashboard represents real-time operational state unless explicitly designed that way.
[15909] 12.49 Real-Time Analytics
[15910] Some metrics may require near-real-time processing.
[15911] Examples:
[15912] active system incidents
[15913] current claims queue
[15914] payment processing
[15915] authorization workload
[15916] security events
[15917] Architecture:
[15918] Operational Event
[15919] ↓
[15920] Event Stream
[15921] ↓
[15922] Stream Processing
[15923] ↓
[15924] Real-Time Metric
[15925] ↓
[15926] Dashboard
[15928] 12.50 Historical Reporting
[15929] Historical reports must use the business rules applicable to the relevant period.
[15930] For example:
[15931] A claim processed under Plan Version 2026-A should not be recalculated using Plan Version 2027-B simply because the report was generated in 2027.
[15932] Historical reporting requires:
[15933] effective dates
[15934] versioned plans
[15935] versioned tariffs
[15936] versioned rules
[15937] versioned KPI definitions where necessary
[15939] 12.51 Analytics Data Quality
[15940] Every analytical pipeline should monitor:
[15941] completeness
[15942] accuracy
[15943] consistency
[15944] uniqueness
[15945] timeliness
[15946] validity
[15947] Example:
[15948] Claims Received Today: 20,000
[15950] Warehouse Claims:
[15951] 19,850
[15953] Variance:
[15954] 150
[15956] Status:
[15957] DATA QUALITY ALERT
[15959] 12.52 Reconciliation
[15960] Critical analytical data should be reconciled against source systems.
[15961] Examples:
[15962] Operational Claims
[15963] ↕
[15964] Warehouse Claims
[15966] Operational Payments
[15967] ↕
[15968] Finance Analytics
[15970] Active Members
[15971] ↕
[15972] Membership Analytics
[15973] Material differences should generate alerts.
[15975] 12.53 Analytics Audit Trail
[15976] Important analytical activities should be auditable.
[15977] Record:
[15978] user
[15979] report
[15980] dataset
[15981] parameters
[15982] timestamp
[15983] export action
[15984] format
[15985] scope
[15986] result status
[15988] 12.54 Data Access Governance
[15989] Every analytical dataset should have:
[15990] owner
[15991] classification
[15992] approved users
[15993] purpose
[15994] retention
[15995] refresh frequency
[15996] source
[15997] sensitivity
[15999] 12.55 Executive Alerts
[16000] The platform may generate management alerts.
[16001] Examples:
[16002] Claims Cost ↑ 25%
[16003] Provider Credential Expiring
[16004] Receivables Aging Increased
[16005] Complaint SLA Breach
[16006] Settlement Backlog Increased
[16007] Eligibility Service Degraded
[16008] Critical Security Event
[16009] Alerts must include:
[16010] metric
[16011] threshold
[16012] actual value
[16013] period
[16014] source
[16015] timestamp
[16016] severity
[16017] responsible owner
[16019] 12.56 Alert Fatigue Prevention
[16020] Too many alerts make alerts ineffective.
[16021] Therefore:
[16022] group duplicates
[16023] suppress repeated alerts
[16024] set escalation rules
[16025] define severity
[16026] provide ownership
[16027] automatically resolve when conditions normalize where appropriate
[16029] 12.57 Analytics Notification Workflow
[16030] Metric
[16031] ↓
[16032] Threshold
[16033] ↓
[16034] Rule Evaluation
[16035] ↓
[16036] Alert Created
[16037] ↓
[16038] Owner Identified
[16039] ↓
[16040] Notification
[16041] ↓
[16042] Acknowledgement
[16043] ↓
[16044] Investigation
[16045] ↓
[16046] Resolution
[16048] 12.58 Decision Intelligence
[16049] Decision intelligence combines:
[16050] DATA
[16051] +
[16052] RULES
[16053] +
[16054] CONTEXT
[16055] +
[16056] ANALYTICS
[16057] +
[16058] RISK SIGNALS
[16059] +
[16060] HUMAN JUDGMENT
[16061] to support decisions.
[16062] Examples:
[16063] which claims require additional review
[16064] which providers require credential review
[16065] which employers have increasing receivables
[16066] which benefits are highly utilized
[16067] where provider network capacity is insufficient
[16069] 12.59 Decision Recommendation Architecture
[16070] The system may produce:
[16071] Recommendation
[16072] ↓
[16073] Evidence
[16074] ↓
[16075] Reason
[16076] ↓
[16077] Confidence
[16078] ↓
[16079] Authorized Human Review
[16080] The system should not silently execute high-impact decisions unless explicitly authorized by approved business rules.
[16082] 12.60 Analytics Edge-Case Matrix
[16084] 12.61 Unknown Analytics Edge Cases
[16085] Unknown analytics conditions must not silently produce misleading results.
[16086] The safe strategy is:
[16087] Unexpected Analytics Condition
[16088] ↓
[16089] Validate Data
[16090] ↓
[16091] Confidence Sufficient?
[16092] ┌───┴───┐
[16093] YES      NO
[16094] │        │
[16095] ▼        ▼
[16096] Publish    Mark Uncertain
[16097] / Incomplete
[16098] ↓
[16099] Alert
[16100] ↓
[16101] Review
[16102] The platform should prefer:
[16103] “Data unavailable”
[16104] over:
[16105] “False certainty.”
[16107] 12.62 Analytics Security Edge Cases
[16108] Particularly sensitive scenarios include:
[16109] Executive exports
[16110] Require authorization and audit.
[16111] Analyst access
[16112] Must remain scoped.
[16113] Cross-employer comparisons
[16114] Must avoid exposing another employer's confidential information.
[16115] Provider benchmarking
[16116] Must respect provider confidentiality and business rules.
[16117] Individual medical analytics
[16118] Must be restricted to authorized purposes.
[16119] Security analytics
[16120] Security teams may access sensitive security events, but not automatically unrelated medical data.
[16122] 12.63 Analytics Performance
[16123] Analytics systems should use:
[16124] pre-aggregations
[16125] columnar storage where appropriate
[16126] partitioning
[16127] caching
[16128] incremental processing
[16129] materialized views
[16130] scheduled computations
[16131] Large analytical queries should run asynchronously where required.
[16133] 12.64 Analytics Scalability
[16134] The analytics platform should scale independently from transactional services.
[16135] Operational Traffic ↑
[16136] │
[16137] ├──► Operational Infrastructure
[16138] │
[16139] └──► Event Stream
[16140] ↓
[16141] Analytics Layer
[16142] This prevents heavy reporting from degrading claims or eligibility services.
[16144] 12.65 Analytics Reliability
[16145] Analytics failures should not normally stop operational HMO services.
[16146] For example:
[16147] If the warehouse is unavailable:
[16148] Eligibility
[16149] Claims
[16150] Finance Transactions
[16151] Member Portal
[16152] ↓
[16153] Continue Operating
[16154] while:
[16155] Analytics
[16156] Reports
[16157] Dashboards
[16158] ↓
[16159] Degraded / Delayed
[16161] 12.66 Data Freshness Indicators
[16162] Each dashboard should clearly communicate whether data is:
[16163] real-time
[16164] near-real-time
[16165] hourly
[16166] daily
[16167] monthly
[16168] Example:
[16169] Claims Dashboard
[16170] Data through: 14:00
[16171] Last refresh: 14:05
[16173] 12.67 Analytics Governance Board
[16174] A governance group should oversee:
[16175] KPI definitions
[16176] data ownership
[16177] sensitive datasets
[16178] report standards
[16179] analytical models
