[16305] 12.73 Part XII Summary
[16306] The HMO platform's intelligence architecture should transform operational information into trustworthy decision support:
[16307] OPERATIONAL DATA
[16308] ↓
[16309] DATA QUALITY
[16310] ↓
[16311] DATA WAREHOUSE
[16312] ↓
[16313] SEMANTIC / KPI LAYER
[16314] ↓
[16315] ANALYTICS
[16316] ↓
[16317] RISK SIGNALS
[16318] ↓
[16319] DASHBOARDS
[16320] ↓
[16321] ALERTS
[16322] ↓
[16323] DECISION SUPPORT
[16324] ↓
[16325] AUTHORIZED HUMAN ACTION
[16326] ↓
[16327] AUDIT
[16328] The central principle is:
[16329] Analytics should make the organization more informed, not less accountable.
[16330] Every important number must have a definition.
[16331] Every sensitive report must have an access boundary.
[16332] Every risk signal must have traceable evidence.
[16333] Every analytical decision must be distinguishable from an established business fact.
[16334] And every analytical system must preserve the same security boundaries as the operational HMO platform.
[16337] PART XIII — INTEGRATION ARCHITECTURE, EXTERNAL SYSTEMS, APIs, WEBHOOKS & INTEROPERABILITY
[16338] 13.0 Purpose
[16339] The HMO platform cannot operate as an isolated system.
[16340] It must securely communicate with:
[16341] Banks
[16342] Payment gateways
[16343] Healthcare providers
[16344] Hospitals
[16345] Pharmacies
[16346] Laboratories
[16347] Employers
[16348] Enterprise customers
[16349] SMS providers
[16350] Email providers
[16351] Push notification services
[16352] Identity providers
[16353] Accounting/ERP systems
[16354] Analytics platforms
[16355] Document systems
[16356] External healthcare platforms
[16357] Approved government or regulatory systems where applicable
[16358] Other authorized third-party systems
[16359] The integration architecture therefore becomes a critical part of the platform.
[16360] The platform must be designed so that an external system failing, becoming slow, sending duplicate messages, sending malformed data, or becoming unavailable does not corrupt the HMO's internal records.
[16361] Core principle
[16362] External systems may influence the HMO platform, but they must never be allowed to compromise its data integrity, authorization model, security boundaries, or financial controls.
[16364] 13.1 Integration Goals
[16365] The integration architecture shall provide:
[16366] Secure system-to-system communication.
[16367] Standardized API interfaces.
[16368] Reliable asynchronous processing.
[16369] Webhook support.
[16370] Batch/file integration.
[16371] Real-time eligibility verification.
[16372] Provider integration.
[16373] Employer integration.
[16374] Payment and banking integration.
[16375] Notification integration.
[16376] Identity federation.
[16377] Accounting integration.
[16378] Healthcare interoperability.
[16379] Integration monitoring.
[16380] Integration reconciliation.
[16381] Partner isolation.
[16382] Credential lifecycle management.
[16383] Failure recovery.
[16384] Idempotent processing.
[16385] Full auditability.
[16387] 13.2 Integration Principles
[16388] Every integration must follow these principles.
[16389] Principle 1 — Never trust external input
[16390] Every external payload must be:
[16391] authenticated
[16392] authorized
[16393] validated
[16394] sanitized
[16395] schema-checked
[16396] business-rule checked
[16397] monitored
[16398] Principle 2 — External failure must not corrupt internal state
[16399] A failed external request must not automatically roll back unrelated completed business operations.
[16400] Principle 3 — Every integration must be observable
[16401] The platform must know:
[16402] what was sent
[16403] when it was sent
[16404] who/what initiated it
[16405] where it was sent
[16406] what response was received
[16407] how long it took
[16408] whether it succeeded
[16409] whether it was retried
[16410] whether it was reconciled
[16411] Principle 4 — Idempotency everywhere
[16412] Repeated requests must not create duplicate:
[16413] payments
[16414] claims
[16415] enrollments
[16416] members
[16417] invoices
[16418] notifications
[16419] provider transactions
[16420] financial entries
[16421] Principle 5 — Authorization remains internal
[16422] External systems cannot grant themselves permissions inside the HMO platform.
[16423] Principle 6 — Integration credentials are identities
[16424] Every external connection must have its own identity and credentials.
[16425] Principle 7 — Version everything
[16426] APIs, schemas, events, mappings and business contracts must be versioned.
[16428] 13.3 High-Level Integration Architecture
[16429] EXTERNAL ECOSYSTEM
[16430] |
[16431] +---------------+----------------+
[16432] |               |                |
[16433] Banks          Providers        Employers
[16434] |               |                |
[16435] +---------------+----------------+
[16436] |
[16437] INTEGRATION LAYER
[16438] |
[16439] +---------------+----------------+
[16440] |               |                |
[16441] API Gateway     Integration      File Gateway
[16442] Services
[16443] |               |                |
[16444] +---------------+----------------+
[16445] |
[16446] Message/Event Platform
[16447] |
[16448] +---------------+----------------+
[16449] |               |                |
[16450] Domain          Workflow         Notification
[16451] Services        Services          Services
[16452] |
[16453] +----+------------------------------------+
[16454] |             HMO PLATFORM                |
[16455] |                                          |
[16456] | Member | Claims | Finance | Healthcare  |
[16457] | Provider | Employer | Plan | Eligibility|
[16458] +------------------------------------------+
[16460] 13.4 Integration Types
[16461] The platform shall support multiple integration patterns.
[16462] 13.4.1 Synchronous APIs
[16463] Used when an immediate response is required.
[16464] Examples:
[16465] eligibility verification
[16466] member lookup
[16467] provider lookup
[16468] payment initiation
[16469] claim submission acknowledgement
[16470] authorization status
[16471] Typical flow:
[16472] Client
[16473] ↓
[16474] API Gateway
[16475] ↓
[16476] Authentication
[16477] ↓
[16478] Authorization
[16479] ↓
[16480] Validation
[16481] ↓
[16482] Domain Service
[16483] ↓
[16484] Database
[16485] ↓
[16486] Response
[16488] 13.4.2 Asynchronous Events
[16489] Used for long-running or non-blocking operations.
[16490] Examples:
[16491] claim processing
[16492] bulk enrollment
[16493] payment reconciliation
[16494] notification delivery
[16495] document processing
[16496] analytics processing
[16497] fraud/risk analysis
[16498] Request
[16499] ↓
[16500] Domain Service
[16501] ↓
[16502] Transaction
[16503] ↓
[16504] Outbox Event
[16505] ↓
[16506] Message Broker
[16507] ↓
[16508] Consumer
[16509] ↓
[16510] Processing
[16512] 13.5 Integration Domains
[16513] The platform should maintain separate integration boundaries for:
[16514] Financial
[16515] Banks
[16516] Payment gateways
[16517] Treasury platforms
[16518] Accounting systems
[16519] Healthcare
[16520] Hospitals
[16521] Clinics
[16522] Pharmacies
[16523] Laboratories
[16524] Diagnostic providers
[16525] Healthcare information systems
[16526] Employer
[16527] HR systems
[16528] Payroll systems
[16529] Employee databases
[16530] ERP systems
[16531] Communication
[16532] SMS
[16533] Email
[16534] Push notifications
[16535] WhatsApp or other approved channels where applicable
[16536] Identity
[16537] Enterprise SSO
[16538] Identity providers
[16539] MFA providers
[16540] Directory systems
[16541] Analytics
[16542] Data warehouse
[16543] BI platforms
[16544] ML platforms
[16545] Regulatory
[16546] Approved external regulatory/reporting systems where applicable.
[16548] 13.6 API Management
[16549] The API Gateway shall provide centralized controls for external APIs.
[16550] Responsibilities include:
[16551] authentication
[16552] authorization
[16553] routing
[16554] rate limiting
[16555] throttling
[16556] request validation
[16557] response validation where required
[16558] API versioning
[16559] logging
[16560] correlation IDs
[16561] abuse protection
[16562] IP controls
[16563] quota management
[16564] API analytics
[16565] Example:
[16566] POST /api/v1/eligibility/check
[16567] POST /api/v1/claims
[16568] GET  /api/v1/members/{memberId}
[16569] GET  /api/v1/providers/{providerId}
[16570] POST /api/v1/preauthorizations
[16571] GET  /api/v1/claims/{claimId}
[16573] 13.7 API Security
[16574] External APIs should support appropriate combinations of:
[16575] OAuth 2.0
[16576] OpenID Connect
[16577] mTLS
[16578] signed requests
[16579] API keys where appropriate
[16580] short-lived access tokens
[16581] scoped permissions
[16582] IP allowlisting
[16583] rate limits
[16584] request timestamps
[16585] replay protection
[16586] API keys alone should not be used as the primary security mechanism for highly sensitive operations when stronger authentication is practical.
[16588] 13.8 API Authorization
[16589] Authentication answers:
[16590] Who are you?
[16591] Authorization answers:
[16592] What are you allowed to do?
[16593] For example:
[16594] A provider may have:
[16595] provider.claim.create
[16596] provider.claim.read
[16597] provider.member.eligibility.check
[16598] provider.preauthorization.create
[16599] but must not automatically receive:
[16600] finance.payment.execute
[16601] finance.refund.execute
[16602] admin.user.create
[16603] security.role.assign
[16604] Authorization must also consider:
[16605] provider identity
[16606] provider location
[16607] organization
[16608] role
[16609] permission
[16610] member relationship
[16611] data scope
[16612] service type
[16613] environment
[16614] request risk
[16616] 13.9 API Contracts
[16617] Every production API must have a defined contract.
[16618] Minimum contract information:
[16619] endpoint
[16620] method
[16621] request schema
[16622] response schema
[16623] authentication
[16624] authorization
[16625] required headers
[16626] error responses
[16627] idempotency behavior
[16628] pagination
[16629] rate limits
[16630] version
[16631] deprecation policy
[16632] SLA expectations
[16633] OpenAPI can be used as the contract format.
[16635] 13.10 API Versioning
[16636] The platform should avoid breaking existing integrations without controlled migration.
[16637] Example:
[16638] /api/v1/claims
[16639] /api/v2/claims
[16640] A version should only be retired after:
[16641] migration notice
[16642] partner communication
[16643] usage analysis
[16644] migration support
[16645] deprecation period
[16646] final shutdown
[16647] audit record
[16649] 13.11 Idempotency
[16650] Every operation capable of creating financial, healthcare, enrollment or claim records should support idempotency.
[16651] Example:
[16652] Idempotency-Key:
[16653] CLAIM-PROVIDER123-20260930-000145
[16654] If the same request arrives twice:
[16655] Request 1 → Claim Created
[16656] Request 2 → Existing Claim Returned
[16657] Not:
[16658] Request 1 → Claim A
[16659] Request 2 → Claim B
[16661] 13.12 Correlation IDs
[16662] Every integration transaction should receive a correlation ID.
[16663] Example:
[16664] CORR-2026-0000009182
[16665] The ID should follow the transaction across:
[16666] API gateway
[16667] integration service
[16668] domain service
[16669] database transaction
[16670] message broker
[16671] external provider
[16672] webhook
[16673] reconciliation
[16674] audit
[16675] monitoring
[16676] This allows operations teams to reconstruct an entire transaction.
[16678] 13.13 Webhook Architecture
[16679] External systems may notify the HMO platform through webhooks.
[16680] Example:
[16681] Payment Gateway
[16682] |
[16683] | Webhook
[16684] ↓
[16685] Webhook Gateway
[16686] ↓
[16687] Signature Validation
[16688] ↓
[16689] Timestamp Validation
[16690] ↓
[16691] Replay Detection
[16692] ↓
[16693] Schema Validation
[16694] ↓
[16695] Idempotency Check
[16696] ↓
[16697] Event Queue
[16698] ↓
[16699] Business Processing
[16700] ↓
[16701] Audit + Reconciliation
[16703] 13.14 Webhook Security
[16704] Every webhook should support appropriate protections such as:
[16705] signature verification
[16706] timestamp validation
[16707] event ID
[16708] replay protection
[16709] TLS
[16710] source validation
[16711] schema validation
[16712] rate limiting
[16713] idempotency
[16714] The system must never trust a webhook merely because it contains a familiar reference number.
[16716] 13.15 Webhook Processing
[16717] Recommended lifecycle:
[16718] RECEIVED
[16719] ↓
[16720] AUTHENTICATION_CHECK
[16721] ↓
[16722] SIGNATURE_VALIDATED
[16723] ↓
[16724] SCHEMA_VALIDATED
[16725] ↓
[16726] DUPLICATE_CHECK
[16727] ↓
[16728] ACCEPTED
[16729] ↓
[16730] QUEUED
[16731] ↓
[16732] PROCESSING
[16733] ↓
[16734] PROCESSED
[16735] Failure states:
[16736] INVALID
[16737] REJECTED
[16738] DUPLICATE
[16739] PROCESSING_FAILED
[16740] DEAD_LETTER
[16741] MANUAL_REVIEW
[16743] 13.16 Webhook Replay Protection
[16744] An attacker or faulty provider may resend an old valid webhook.
[16745] Therefore:
[16746] Event ID
[16747] +
[16748] Timestamp
[16749] +
[16750] Signature
[16751] +
[16752] Previously Processed Event Check
[16753] must be evaluated.
[16754] A valid signature does not automatically mean the event should be processed again.
[16756] 13.17 Payment and Banking Integrations
[16757] Financial integrations require the highest reliability and reconciliation controls.
[16758] Possible integrations:
[16759] bank transfer systems
[16760] payment gateways
[16761] card processors
[16762] direct debit systems where supported
[16763] corporate payment platforms
[16764] settlement systems
[16765] Typical payment flow:
[16766] User/Finance Officer
[16767] ↓
[16768] Payment Request
[16769] ↓
[16770] Authorization
[16771] ↓
[16772] SoD Check
[16773] ↓
[16774] Approval
[16775] ↓
[16776] Payment Gateway
[16777] ↓
[16778] Gateway Response
[16779] ↓
[16780] Payment Status
[16781] ↓
[16782] Ledger
[16783] ↓
[16784] Reconciliation
[16786] 13.18 Unknown Payment Status
[16787] A critical case occurs when the HMO sends a payment but receives no response.
[16788] Example:
[16789] HMO → Bank
[16790] Bank → Payment Received
[16791] Network → Timeout
[16792] HMO → UNKNOWN
[16793] The platform must not automatically mark the payment as failed.
[16794] Correct state:
[16795] UNKNOWN
[16796] ↓
[16797] STATUS QUERY
[16798] ↓
[16799] WEBHOOK CHECK
[16800] ↓
[16801] BANK RECONCILIATION
[16802] ↓
[16803] CONFIRMED SUCCESS / CONFIRMED FAILURE
[16804] This prevents duplicate payments.
[16806] 13.19 Provider Integrations
[16807] Provider systems may exchange:
[16808] eligibility requests
[16809] preauthorization requests
[16810] referrals
[16811] service requests
[16812] claims
[16813] claim corrections
[16814] remittance information
[16815] provider documents
[16816] clinical information where authorized
[16817] Provider integrations must respect provider-level and location-level authorization.
[16818] A provider user at Location A must not automatically access records belonging to Location B.
[16820] 13.20 Provider Eligibility API
[16821] Example:
[16822] POST /api/v1/provider/eligibility/check
[16823] Request:
[16824] memberReference
[16825] providerReference
[16826] serviceCode
[16827] serviceDate
[16828] Response:
[16829] eligibilityStatus
[16830] coverageStatus
[16831] planReference
[16832] benefitStatus
[16833] authorizationRequired
[16834] reasonCode
[16835] verificationReference
[16836] timestamp
[16837] The response must disclose only the minimum information necessary for the provider's workflow.
[16839] 13.21 Provider Offline Scenario
[16840] A provider may lose connectivity.
[16841] The platform should support controlled offline workflows where operationally necessary.
[16842] Possible design:
[16843] Online Verification
[16844] ↓
[16845] Temporary Verification Token
[16846] ↓
[16847] Provider Offline
[16848] ↓
[16849] Service Delivered
[16850] ↓
[16851] Connection Restored
[16852] ↓
[16853] Transaction Submitted
[16854] ↓
[16855] Server Revalidation
[16856] Offline authorization must have:
[16857] expiration
[16858] scope
[16859] provider binding
[16860] member binding
[16861] service binding
[16862] replay protection
[16863] Offline capability must never become an unlimited authorization bypass.
[16865] 13.22 Employer Integrations
[16866] Employers may provide:
[16867] employee onboarding
[16868] employee termination
[16869] employee changes
[16870] dependant information
[16871] payroll information
[16872] plan selection
[16873] premium information
[16874] billing data
[16875] Integration options:
[16876] REST API
[16877] SFTP
[16878] secure file upload
[16879] scheduled batch
[16880] approved HR/ERP connector
[16882] 13.23 Employer Bulk Enrollment
[16883] Recommended pipeline:
[16884] Employer
[16885] ↓
[16886] Upload
[16887] ↓
[16888] Malware Scan
[16889] ↓
[16890] File Validation
[16891] ↓
[16892] Schema Validation
[16893] ↓
[16894] Field Validation
[16895] ↓
[16896] Business Validation
[16897] ↓
[16898] Duplicate Detection
[16899] ↓
[16900] Preview
[16901] ↓
[16902] Employer Confirmation
[16903] ↓
[16904] Processing
[16905] ↓
[16906] Result Report
[16907] ↓
[16908] Reconciliation
[16909] The employer must be able to see:
[16910] accepted records
[16911] rejected records
[16912] warnings
[16913] duplicates
[16914] corrected records
[16915] processing status
[16917] 13.24 Partial Success
[16918] If an employer uploads 10,000 employees and 9,850 are valid:
[16919] The system should not necessarily reject the entire file.
[16920] It should support controlled partial processing:
[16921] 10,000 Submitted
[16923] 9,850 Accepted
[16924] 100 Rejected
[16925] 50 Require Review
[16926] Each record must retain its processing result.
[16928] 13.25 Communication Integrations
[16929] The notification platform may integrate with:
[16930] SMS providers
[16931] email providers
[16932] push notification services
[16933] approved messaging channels
[16934] Notification lifecycle:
[16935] CREATED
[16936] ↓
[16937] QUEUED
[16938] ↓
[16939] SENT
[16940] ↓
[16941] DELIVERED
[16942] Failure states:
[16943] FAILED
[16944] BOUNCED
[16945] EXPIRED
[16946] SUPPRESSED
[16947] CANCELLED
[16949] 13.26 Notification Reliability
[16950] A message must not be sent repeatedly simply because the provider response was delayed.
[16951] Use:
[16952] notification ID
[16953] delivery ID
[16954] idempotency key
[16955] retry policy
[16956] delivery status
[16957] provider message ID
[16958] Example:
[16959] HMO Notification ID
[16960] ↓
[16961] Provider Message ID
[16962] ↓
[16963] Delivery Status
[16965] 13.27 Communication Failover
[16966] If the primary SMS provider fails:
[16967] Primary Provider
[16968] ↓
[16969] Timeout
[16970] ↓
[16971] Retry Policy
[16972] ↓
[16973] Secondary Provider
[16974] However, failover must consider duplicate delivery.
[16975] The system should verify whether the original request may already have been accepted before sending again.
[16977] 13.28 Identity Provider Integration
[16978] Enterprise customers may require:
[16979] SSO
[16980] identity federation
[16981] directory integration
[16982] MFA federation
[16983] user lifecycle synchronization
[16984] Potential standards:
[16985] OAuth 2.0
[16986] OpenID Connect
[16987] SAML
[16988] SCIM where appropriate
[16989] The HMO platform must still maintain its internal authorization model.
[16990] External SSO authentication does not automatically grant internal privileges.
[16992] 13.29 Employer SSO Example
[16993] Employer User
[16994] ↓
[16995] Employer Identity Provider
[16996] ↓
[16997] Authentication
[16998] ↓
[16999] Identity Assertion
[17000] ↓
[17001] HMO Identity Service
[17002] ↓
[17003] Organization Mapping
[17004] ↓
[17005] Role Mapping
[17006] ↓
[17007] Permission Evaluation
[17008] ↓
[17009] Session
[17010] If the employer says:
[17011] User = HR Manager
[17012] the HMO platform must map that external identity to an explicitly configured internal role.
[17014] 13.30 Accounting / ERP Integration
[17015] The platform may integrate with accounting or ERP systems for:
[17016] invoices
[17017] receivables
[17018] provider settlements
[17019] payments
[17020] refunds
[17021] journal entries
[17022] chart of accounts
[17023] financial reporting
[17024] The HMO platform should retain the authoritative transaction reference needed for reconciliation.
[17026] 13.31 Accounting Export
[17027] Example:
[17028] HMO Transaction
[17029] ↓
[17030] Financial Validation
[17031] ↓
[17032] Approved Accounting Event
[17033] ↓
[17034] Integration Queue
[17035] ↓
[17036] ERP
[17037] ↓
[17038] ERP Reference
[17039] ↓
[17040] Reconciliation
[17041] An ERP acknowledgement must not automatically mean the HMO financial transaction is fully reconciled.
[17043] 13.32 Healthcare Interoperability
[17044] Where partner requirements justify it, the platform may support healthcare interoperability standards such as:
[17045] HL7
[17046] FHIR
[17047] standardized healthcare coding systems
[17048] partner-specific healthcare APIs
[17049] The selected standard must depend on the actual integration requirements.
[17050] The architecture must therefore support:
[17051] Internal HMO Data Model
[17052] ↓
[17053] Mapping Layer
[17054] ↓
[17055] External Healthcare Standard
[17056] ↓
[17057] Partner System
[17058] The internal data model must not be tightly coupled to a single external partner's representation.
[17060] 13.33 Data Mapping Architecture
[17061] External systems rarely use exactly the same data definitions.
[17062] Example:
[17063] External:
[17064] "MemberNo"
[17066] Internal:
[17067] "member_reference"
[17068] Mapping must be explicit.
[17069] External Field
[17070] ↓
[17071] Transformation
[17072] ↓
[17073] Validation
[17074] ↓
[17075] Internal Field
[17076] Mappings must be:
[17077] versioned
[17078] tested
[17079] documented
[17080] auditable
[17082] 13.34 Coding and Reference Data Mapping
[17083] Healthcare and financial partners may use different:
[17084] service codes
[17085] diagnosis codes
[17086] provider identifiers
[17087] plan identifiers
[17088] currency representations
[17089] status values
[17090] The platform should maintain mapping tables.
[17091] Example:
[17092] Partner Service Code
[17093] ↓
[17094] Mapping Table
[17095] ↓
[17096] Internal Service Code
[17097] ↓
[17098] Business Rule
[17099] A missing mapping must not silently default to an unrelated service.
[17101] 13.35 File Exchange Architecture
[17102] For partners unable to use APIs, secure file exchange should be supported.
[17103] Possible methods:
[17104] SFTP
[17105] encrypted object storage
[17106] managed file transfer
[17107] Pipeline:
[17108] File Received
[17109] ↓
[17110] Source Verification
[17111] ↓
[17112] Checksum
[17113] ↓
[17114] Malware Scan
[17115] ↓
[17116] Encryption Validation
[17117] ↓
[17118] Schema Validation
[17119] ↓
[17120] Business Validation
[17121] ↓
[17122] Processing
[17123] ↓
[17124] Result
[17125] ↓
[17126] Archive
[17128] 13.36 File Security
[17129] Files may contain highly sensitive information.
[17130] Controls include:
[17131] encryption in transit
[17132] encryption at rest
[17133] malware scanning
[17134] access restrictions
[17135] retention rules
[17136] checksum verification
[17137] file size limits
[17138] file type restrictions
[17139] secure deletion/retention controls
[17140] audit logging
[17142] 13.37 Batch Integration Architecture
[17143] Every batch process should have:
[17144] batch ID
[17145] source
[17146] destination
[17147] start time
[17148] end time
[17149] record count
[17150] successful count
[17151] failed count
[17152] warning count
[17153] retry count
[17154] status
[17155] reconciliation status
[17156] Example:
[17157] BATCH-2026-00091
[17159] Submitted: 50,000
[17160] Processed: 50,000
[17161] Success:   49,700
[17162] Failed:       200
[17163] Review:       100
[17165] 13.38 Batch State Machine
[17166] CREATED
[17167] ↓
[17168] UPLOADED
[17169] ↓
[17170] VALIDATING
[17171] ↓
[17172] READY
[17173] ↓
[17174] PROCESSING
[17175] ↓
[17176] PARTIALLY_PROCESSED / COMPLETED
[17177] ↓
[17178] RECONCILIATION
[17179] ↓
[17180] RECONCILED
[17181] Failure states:
[17182] FAILED
[17183] CANCELLED
[17184] QUARANTINED
[17185] MANUAL_REVIEW
[17187] 13.39 Integration Gateway
[17188] The platform should centralize common integration capabilities rather than allowing every service to implement them independently.
[17189] Integration Gateway responsibilities:
[17190] partner authentication
[17191] credential management
[17192] routing
[17193] transformation
[17194] validation
[17195] rate limiting
[17196] retry handling
[17197] circuit breaking
[17198] webhook handling
[17199] monitoring
[17200] audit
[17201] reconciliation support
[17203] 13.40 Credential Lifecycle
[17204] Every integration credential should have a lifecycle.
[17205] REQUESTED
[17206] ↓
[17207] APPROVED
[17208] ↓
[17209] PROVISIONED
[17210] ↓
[17211] ACTIVE
[17212] ↓
[17213] ROTATION_PENDING
[17214] ↓
[17215] ROTATED
[17216] ↓
[17217] REVOKED
[17218] ↓
[17219] ARCHIVED
[17220] Credentials must not be hard-coded into applications.
[17221] They should be stored using secure secret-management infrastructure.
[17223] 13.41 Credential Rotation
[17224] Rotation must support:
[17225] old credential overlap where necessary
[17226] new credential validation
[17227] controlled cutover
[17228] monitoring
[17229] rollback
[17230] final old credential revocation
[17231] Example:
[17232] Old Credential ACTIVE
[17233] New Credential CREATED
[17234] ↓
[17235] Test
[17236] ↓
[17237] Activate New
[17238] ↓
[17239] Monitor
[17240] ↓
[17241] Revoke Old
[17243] 13.42 Third-Party Vendor Lifecycle
[17244] Every external integration partner should pass through:
[17245] PROSPECT
[17246] ↓
[17247] SECURITY ASSESSMENT
[17248] ↓
[17249] TECHNICAL ASSESSMENT
[17250] ↓
[17251] CONTRACT
[17252] ↓
[17253] SANDBOX
[17254] ↓
[17255] CERTIFICATION
[17256] ↓
[17257] PRODUCTION
[17258] ↓
[17259] MONITORING
[17260] ↓
[17261] REVIEW
[17262] ↓
[17263] SUSPENSION / TERMINATION
[17265] 13.43 Partner Suspension
[17266] A partner may need to be suspended because of:
[17267] security incident
[17268] repeated invalid requests
[17269] credential compromise
[17270] abnormal traffic
[17271] contract expiration
[17272] system instability
[17273] data-quality problems
[17274] Suspension should be scoped where possible.
[17275] Example:
[17276] Partner
[17277] ├── Eligibility API → ACTIVE
[17278] ├── Claims API → SUSPENDED
[17279] └── Remittance API → ACTIVE
[17281] 13.44 Integration Observability
[17282] The integration platform should monitor:
[17283] Availability
[17284] uptime
[17285] endpoint availability
[17286] partner availability
[17287] Performance
[17288] response latency
[17289] timeout rate
[17290] queue latency
[17291] Reliability
[17292] retry rate
[17293] failure rate
[17294] DLQ volume
[17295] duplicate rate
[17296] Data quality
[17297] rejected payloads
[17298] mapping failures
[17299] schema errors
[17300] Security
[17301] invalid signatures
[17302] unauthorized requests
[17303] suspicious traffic
[17304] credential failures
[17306] 13.45 Integration Metrics
[17307] Example metrics:
[17308] API Success Rate
[17309] API Error Rate
[17310] Average Latency
[17311] P95 Latency
[17312] P99 Latency
[17313] Timeout Rate
[17314] Webhook Failure Rate
[17315] Duplicate Event Rate
[17316] DLQ Messages
[17317] Integration Availability
[17318] Batch Completion Rate
[17319] Reconciliation Exceptions
[17320] Credential Failure Rate
[17322] 13.46 Integration Reliability
[17323] External systems are inherently unreliable.
[17324] The platform must support:
[17325] Timeout
[17326] Prevent indefinite waiting.
[17327] Retry
[17328] Use controlled retries with exponential backoff and jitter.
[17329] Circuit breaker
[17330] Stop repeatedly calling a failing dependency.
[17331] Bulkhead
[17332] Prevent one failing integration from consuming all system resources.
[17333] Queueing
[17334] Move non-urgent operations to asynchronous processing.
[17335] Dead-letter queue
[17336] Capture messages that cannot be processed automatically.
[17338] 13.47 Backpressure
[17339] If an external system can only process:
[17340] 100 requests/sec
[17341] but the HMO generates:
[17342] 1,000 requests/sec
[17343] the integration layer must not simply forward all requests.
[17344] It should:
[17345] Requests
[17346] ↓
[17347] Queue
[17348] ↓
[17349] Rate-controlled Consumer
[17350] ↓
[17351] External System
[17353] 13.48 Integration Reconciliation Framework
[17354] Every important integration should have reconciliation.
[17355] Examples:
[17356] Payment
[17357] HMO Payment Records
[17358] ↕
[17359] Bank Records
[17360] Claims
[17361] HMO Claims
[17362] ↕
[17363] Provider Submission Records
[17364] Employer Enrollment
[17365] HMO Members
[17366] ↕
[17367] Employer Employee File
[17368] Accounting
[17369] HMO Ledger
[17370] ↕
[17371] ERP Ledger
[17372] Differences must become reconciliation exceptions.
[17374] 13.49 Reconciliation States
[17375] MATCHED
[17376] PARTIALLY_MATCHED
[17377] UNMATCHED
[17378] CONFLICT
[17379] MISSING_EXTERNAL
[17380] MISSING_INTERNAL
[17381] PENDING
[17382] MANUAL_REVIEW
[17383] RESOLVED
[17384] No reconciliation exception should silently disappear.
[17386] 13.50 Integration Access Control
[17387] Integration permissions should follow the same authorization architecture used internally.
[17388] Example:
[17389] integration.claim.submit
[17390] integration.claim.read
[17391] integration.eligibility.check
[17392] integration.payment.initiate
[17393] integration.payment.status
[17394] integration.remittance.read
[17395] A partner should only receive the permissions explicitly assigned to it.
[17397] 13.51 Tenant Isolation
[17398] External integrations must respect organization boundaries.
[17399] For example:
[17400] Employer A
[17401] ↓
[17402] Employer A Integration Credential
[17403] ↓
[17404] Employer A Data
[17405] Employer A must never be able to manipulate Employer B data.
[17406] This isolation must exist at:
[17407] API
[17408] service
[17409] authorization
[17410] database
[17411] cache
[17412] search
[17413] file
[17414] reporting
[17415] export
[17416] event
[17417] webhook
[17418] levels.
[17420] 13.52 Integration Testing
[17421] Every integration must have:
[17422] Unit tests
[17423] For transformation and validation.
[17424] Contract tests
[17425] To ensure both sides agree on API behavior.
[17426] Integration tests
[17427] For actual system interaction.
[17428] Failure tests
[17429] For:
[17430] timeout
[17431] invalid response
[17432] duplicate response
[17433] malformed payload
[17434] unavailable service
[17435] Security tests
[17436] For:
[17437] invalid token
[17438] expired token
[17439] replay
[17440] signature failure
[17441] unauthorized scope
[17442] cross-tenant access
[17443] Performance tests
[17444] For:
[17445] expected volume
[17446] peak volume
[17447] burst traffic
[17449] 13.53 Partner Certification
[17450] Before production access, a partner should pass certification scenarios such as:
[17451] Valid request.
[17452] Invalid request.
[17453] Duplicate request.
[17454] Timeout.
[17455] Retry.
[17456] Invalid authentication.
[17457] Expired credential.
[17458] Unauthorized resource.
[17459] Large request.
[17460] Malformed request.
[17461] Unexpected response.
[17462] Webhook replay.
[17463] Partial failure.
[17464] Reconciliation mismatch.
[17466] 13.54 Sandbox Environment
[17467] External partners should have a controlled sandbox.
[17468] The sandbox should support:
[17469] test credentials
[17470] test members
[17471] test providers
[17472] test claims
[17473] simulated payment results
[17474] simulated webhook events
[17475] simulated failures
[17476] No real production personal or financial information should be used for testing unless specifically governed and protected.
[17478] 13.55 Integration Failure Matrix
[17480] 13.56 Unknown Integration Edge Cases
[17481] The system must assume that not every failure can be predicted.
[17482] For any unknown condition:
[17483] UNKNOWN CONDITION
[17484] ↓
[17485] STOP UNSAFE ACTION
[17486] ↓
[17487] PRESERVE CURRENT STATE
[17488] ↓
[17489] CREATE CORRELATION ID
[17490] ↓
[17491] LOG TECHNICAL ERROR
[17492] ↓
[17493] CREATE OPERATIONAL/SECURITY EVENT
[17494] ↓
[17495] MOVE TO SAFE EXCEPTION STATE
[17496] ↓
[17497] ALERT APPROPRIATE TEAM
[17498] ↓
[17499] RETRY OR MANUAL RECOVERY
[17500] ↓
[17501] RECONCILE
[17502] The platform must never attempt to "guess" a financial or healthcare outcome when the external state is uncertain.
[17504] 13.57 Integration Security Events
[17505] The following should generate security events where appropriate:
[17506] invalid API token
[17507] repeated authentication failure
[17508] invalid webhook signature
[17509] replay attempt
[17510] abnormal request volume
[17511] cross-tenant access attempt
[17512] unexpected geographic/source pattern
[17513] credential misuse
[17514] unauthorized endpoint access
[17515] suspicious file upload
[17516] malware detection
[17517] unusual API data extraction
[17518] privilege escalation attempt
[17520] 13.58 Integration Audit Trail
[17521] Every important integration transaction should record:
[17522] Integration ID
[17523] Partner ID
[17524] Credential/Client ID
[17525] Request ID
[17526] Correlation ID
[17527] Event ID
[17528] Direction
[17529] Endpoint
[17530] Operation
[17531] Timestamp
[17532] Actor/Service
[17533] Source
[17534] Destination
[17535] Request Hash
[17536] Response Status
[17537] Processing Status
[17538] Retry Count
[17539] Error Code
[17540] Reconciliation Status
[17541] Sensitive payloads should not automatically be stored in plaintext in logs.
[17543] 13.59 Integration Data Privacy
[17544] Integration data should follow minimum-necessary principles.
[17545] For example, a provider eligibility response may need:
[17546] Member Reference
[17547] Eligibility Status
[17548] Plan
[17549] Benefit
[17550] Authorization Requirement
[17551] It may not need unrestricted access to:
[17552] unrelated claims
[17553] financial records
[17554] internal employee information
[17555] security events
[17556] unrelated clinical information
[17558] 13.60 Integration Rate Limits
[17559] Different partners may have different limits.
[17560] Example configuration:
[17561] Partner A
[17562] Eligibility: 100 req/sec
[17564] Partner B
[17565] Eligibility: 20 req/sec
[17567] Employer C
[17568] Bulk API: 5 req/sec
[17569] Rate limits must be configurable rather than hard-coded.
[17571] 13.61 Integration Quotas
[17572] Quota dimensions may include:
[17573] requests/minute
[17574] requests/day
[17575] file size
[17576] records/batch
[17577] concurrent requests
[17578] storage
[17579] webhook volume
[17580] Quota exhaustion should produce an explicit response rather than an unexplained failure.
[17582] 13.62 Integration Lifecycle Management
[17583] Every integration should have:
[17584] REQUEST
[17585] ↓
[17586] ASSESS
[17587] ↓
[17588] DESIGN
[17589] ↓
[17590] BUILD
[17591] ↓
[17592] TEST
[17593] ↓
[17594] CERTIFY
[17595] ↓
[17596] DEPLOY
[17597] ↓
[17598] MONITOR
[17599] ↓
[17600] REVIEW
[17601] ↓
[17602] VERSION
[17603] ↓
[17604] DEPRECATE
[17605] ↓
[17606] RETIRE
[17608] 13.63 Integration Change Management
[17609] Changes to an integration must evaluate:
[17610] API compatibility
[17611] security
[17612] data mapping
[17613] authorization
[17614] performance
[17615] failure behavior
[17616] reconciliation
[17617] audit
[17618] partner impact
[17619] High-risk changes require controlled approval.

[17814] 13.69 Performance Requirements
[17815] Integration performance targets should be configurable by integration type.
[17816] Example targets:
[17817] Webhook endpoints should acknowledge receipt quickly and perform expensive processing asynchronously.
[17819] 13.70 Scalability Requirements
[17820] Integration infrastructure must scale independently.
[17821] Examples:
[17822] API Traffic
[17823] ↓
[17824] API Gateway
[17825] ↓
[17826] Autoscaling Integration Services
[17827] and:
[17828] 10,000 Claims
[17829] ↓
[17830] Message Queue
[17831] ↓
[17832] Claim Consumers
[17833] ↓
[17834] Horizontal Scaling
[17835] A large batch should not make real-time eligibility unavailable.
[17837] 13.71 Reliability Requirements
[17838] The integration layer must support:
[17839] retries
[17840] exponential backoff
[17841] jitter
[17842] circuit breakers
[17843] queues
[17844] dead-letter queues
[17845] idempotency
[17846] timeout controls
[17847] bulkheads
[17848] graceful degradation
[17849] reconciliation
[17850] replay where safe
[17851] controlled manual recovery
[17853] 13.72 Usability Requirements
[17854] Integration operations must be visible to authorized staff.
[17855] An integration dashboard should show:
[17856] Integration Health
[17857] -------------------------
[17858] Bank API              HEALTHY
[17859] Provider API          DEGRADED
[17860] Employer API          HEALTHY
[17861] SMS Provider          DOWN
[17862] Email Provider        HEALTHY
[17863] Claims Queue          2,431 pending
[17864] Webhook DLQ           17
[17865] Reconciliation        4 exceptions
[17866] Authorized administrators should be able to inspect:
[17867] failures
[17868] retries
[17869] queues
[17870] partner status
[17871] reconciliation exceptions
[17872] certificates
[17873] credential status
[17874] API versions
[17875] without exposing sensitive payloads unnecessarily.
[17877] 13.73 Security Requirements
[17878] The integration architecture must enforce:
[17879] zero-trust principles
[17880] least privilege
[17881] strong authentication
[17882] scoped authorization
[17883] tenant isolation
[17884] encryption
[17885] secret management
[17886] credential rotation
[17887] replay protection
[17888] API abuse protection
[17889] secure logging
[17890] auditability
[17891] malware scanning
[17892] secure file exchange
[17893] partner security monitoring
[17895] 13.74 Integration Governance
[17896] An Integration Governance Board or designated architecture/security function should oversee high-risk integrations.
[17897] Responsibilities:
[17898] approve new integrations
[17899] review security
[17900] approve data exchange
[17901] review architecture
[17902] approve credentials
[17903] define ownership
[17904] define SLAs
[17905] review incidents
[17906] review deprecated integrations
[17907] approve production access
[17909] 13.75 Integration Ownership
[17910] Every integration must have a clear owner.
[17911] Minimum ownership model:
[17912] No production integration should exist without a named owner.
[17914] 13.76 Integration SLA Management
[17915] Each critical integration should have:
[17916] availability target
[17917] latency target
[17918] support contact
[17919] escalation procedure
[17920] maintenance window
[17921] incident procedure
[17922] recovery expectation
[17923] reconciliation procedure
[17924] External SLA breaches should generate operational visibility.
[17926] 13.77 Five Invisible Pillars — Integration Implementation
[17927] Performance

[17932] efficient serialization
[17933] queue-based workloads

[17960] understandable reconciliation exceptions

[17963] The platform must explicitly test for:
[17964] Authentication

[17969] credential rotation during active requests
[17970] Network

[17980] unexpected fields
[17981] wrong data types
[17982] invalid encoding
[17983] invalid dates
[17984] invalid currency
[17985] invalid identifiers
[17986] Transactions

[17990] conflicting status

[18022] 13.79 Unknown Failure Principle
[18023] The most important integration rule is:
[18024] When the system does not know what happened, it must not pretend that it knows.
[18025] For example:
[18026] UNKNOWN PAYMENT
[18027] must remain:
[18028] UNKNOWN
[18029] until evidence determines:
[18030] SUCCESS
[18031] or:
[18032] FAILED
[18033] The same principle applies to:
[18034] claims
[18035] enrollments
[18036] authorizations
[18037] refunds
[18038] provider transactions
[18039] financial transfers
[18040] external healthcare transactions.
[18042] 13.80 Part XIII Completion Criteria
[18043] Part XIII is considered complete when the HMO platform has:
[18044] centralized integration architecture
[18045] API gateway
[18046] secure partner authentication
[18047] authorization
[18048] API versioning
[18049] idempotency
[18050] correlation IDs
[18051] webhook architecture
[18052] webhook replay protection
[18053] payment integration architecture
[18054] provider integration
[18055] employer integration
[18056] communication integration
[18057] identity integration
[18058] accounting integration
[18059] healthcare interoperability strategy
[18060] secure file exchange
[18061] batch processing
[18062] integration monitoring
[18063] reconciliation
[18064] credential lifecycle management
[18065] partner lifecycle management
[18066] tenant isolation
[18067] integration testing
[18068] sandbox/certification
[18069] failure recovery
[18070] DLQ handling
[18071] integration audit
[18072] integration security events
[18073] performance controls
[18074] scalability controls
[18075] reliability controls
[18076] unknown-edge-case handling
[18077] operational ownership
[18078] integration governance.
[18080] 13.81 Final Integration Principle
[18081] The HMO platform should be designed as an integration-safe enterprise system.
[18082] The architecture must assume that:
[18083] banks can fail
[18084] providers can go offline
[18085] employers can upload bad files
[18086] APIs can timeout
[18087] webhooks can arrive twice
[18088] messages can arrive out of order
[18089] credentials can expire
[18090] partners can send unexpected data
[18091] networks can fail
[18092] systems can disagree
[18093] external transactions can succeed while internal systems are temporarily unavailable.
[18094] The platform must therefore combine:
[18095] SECURITY
[18096] +
[18097] AUTHORIZATION
[18098] +
[18099] VALIDATION
[18100] +
[18101] IDEMPOTENCY
[18102] +
[18103] ASYNC PROCESSING
[18104] +
[18105] RETRY
[18106] +
[18107] RECONCILIATION
[18108] +
[18109] OBSERVABILITY
[18110] +
[18111] AUDIT
[18112] +
[18113] RECOVERY
[18114] to create an integration architecture that remains trustworthy even when external systems are not.
[18115] The fundamental rule remains:
[18116] Never allow an external dependency's failure to become an internal data-integrity failure.
